# Enrang Architecture

Implementation blueprint. `SPEC.md` is authoritative for everything a programmer can observe; this document decides how it is built. `DEV_RUNTIME.md` covers the VM, sandbox and hot reload; `TESTING.md` covers `enrang test` and the compiler's own tests.

**Status: M0, M1, M2 and M3 are built: the workspace, diagnostics, target tables, test kit and command line; the lexer, parser with recovery and hints, and the formatter, which `enrang check` and `enrang fmt` run, and `enrang init`; then modules, resolution, typing and inference, narrowing, exhaustiveness, constant evaluation, layout and the `core` signatures, which `enrang check` runs as phase 2; then the MIR and its verifier, the borrow checker with inferred contracts, drop elaboration and module interface files, which `enrang check` runs as phase 3. Everything from M4 on is design, so nothing is compiled to machine code yet.** Items marked [measured] were run on the development host on 2026-11-05 (i5-12400F, WSL2, `as` 2.42, rustc 1.97.1); everything else is design knowledge or a target.

**Where things run.** The compiler and `enrang dev` run only on a modern x86-64 development machine and cross-compile. Only their output runs on old chips.

## 0. Load-bearing ideas

1. **R1 makes borrow checking a per-point dataflow.** References live only in frame slots and in the `private` reference fields of a borrowing class, which itself lives only where a reference can (SPEC 5.1). A borrowing value carries one loan set for all its reference fields, inside which the loans of its `readonly &` fields, its read set, are also kept apart (section 6.2), so it is one more slot, and a forward `held` map plus backward liveness is a complete checker. No regions, no variance, no drop check. Borrowing classes are the newest part of this rule, and their soundness is still under review.
2. **One MIR, two executors.** Post-mono, post-elaboration MIR is the input to both the VM and the native backend. Any disagreement between them is a bug in one of them, and the VM exists before any x86 does.
3. **Legalise before allocation.** LIR holds only what the target can execute: i64 pairs and soft-float are gone before register handling, and the register allocator never sees a pair.
4. **Location-agnostic emitter.** Any virtual register may live in a register or a stack slot, and fixed scratch registers absorb every spill. This makes the allocator optional, which makes it testable (`--regalloc=none|linear|chaos:<seed>`).
5. **The floor binary is the product.** Default i386 output uses 80386 integer instructions only and runs on every listed CPU. `--cpu` is an optimisation. Every encoder form carries a feature set and `Assembler::emit` refuses anything above the current function's allowed set.
6. **Modular checking, whole-program code generation from source or interface MIR.** The borrow checker infers each function's contract, what it does with its references, from its body, and each user module's interface file stores the contracts of everything reachable from its `public` items together with the post-inference MIR of every reachable body (section 6.8). `enrang check` reads an import's interface file and never its source. That file is never trusted: loading it runs the MIR verifier on the stored code, in release builds too, recomputes its contracts and flags from that code and reruns all of phase 3 on it (the borrow check, the narrowing check, fitting with EN0615, EN0714 and EN0418), so a file whose code fails the verifier or does not match its contracts is EN0316, and a module shipped without source is rechecked the same way when only an import's contracts or flags changed. Code generation stays whole-program, from source or from interface MIR: no object files, no linker step between Enrang modules, no libc. The embedded library is compiled lazily, so unreferenced code never exists in the output. Tree shaking is a consequence of the mono worklist rather than a separate pass.

## 1. Workspace

```text
Cargo.toml              [workspace] members = ["crates/*"]; no external dependency anywhere
Cargo.lock              lists workspace crates only (meta-test)
crates/                 see table below
lib/                    Enrang runtime source, embedded by enrang-rtlib
  core/  alloc/  os/{linux_x64,linux_i386,windows,vm,none,uefi}/  boot/  conformance/
corpus/                 conformance programs; corpus/regress/ holds shrunk generator failures
tests/golden/           blessed rendered output, one directory per suite
tests/oracle_exceptions.txt  recorded disagreements with the assembler oracle
evidence/               ledger.json (generated by tests), hardware.txt (owner-entered)
docs/
```

| **Total production Rust** | | **60,000** | |
| `enrang-testkit` (test only) | PRNG, shrinker, goldens, oracle wrappers, skip accounting, decoder and `x86sim` bench, ptrace stepper | test budget | M0 |

60k is the ceiling, and there is no reserve. `enrang-sema` needs its 9,500 lines, paid for by moving these past M10: the dev integration reserve, `--multiboot` and `--coverage` (EN1041). The VM's block counters stay, because the traceability meta-tests read them. Three later decisions add lines that the table does not count yet, and the ceiling rises by their size instead of something else being cut: `enrang fmt` at M1, which lives in `enrang-syntax` because the AST printer is the formatter (the name `enrang-format` is taken by the output writers); contract inference and module interface files (sections 6.7 and 6.8); and the x87 float strategy at M8 (section 7.10). None is estimated yet. For scale, comparable printers measure 3,744 lines (Zig's `Render.zig`) and 3,804 (Go's `go/printer`).

If the budget is threatened again, cut in this order: loop-head replacement, `chaos` shrinking. In the front end, cut in this order, each ending in a new EN10xx row: `toMatchPattern`, `test.each`, `p?.m(args)`. Target presets, `enrang fmt` and destructuring, which the owner chose for the first version, are never cut.

**Measured at M0.** About 5,300 production lines against the 4k estimate, or about 32% over, partly because rustfmt spreads tables vertically. Three crates already pass the budgets above: `enrang-base` about 810 of 600, `enrang-diag` about 2,270 of 1,800, `enrang-target` about 1,400 of 900. Treat 60k as a planning estimate that is likely to be exceeded, and reassess at M2, when the first large crate lands. The `for...of` rules and array spread of SPEC 5.6 and 4.13 add an estimated 300 lines across `enrang-syntax`, `enrang-sema`, `enrang-mirbuild` and `enrang-borrowck`, which come out of those crates' budgets.

The runtime is about 11,200 lines of Enrang under `lib/` (section 10). Test code and corpus meet or exceed implementation size (`TESTING.md`).

```text
enrang-base      ->
enrang-rtlib     ->
enrang-diag      -> base
enrang-target    -> base diag                (its `resolve` returns Diagnostics)
enrang-syntax    -> base diag
enrang-hir       -> base diag target syntax
enrang-layout    -> base target hir
enrang-sema      -> base diag target syntax hir layout rtlib
enrang-mir       -> base hir
enrang-mirbuild  -> base diag hir mir
enrang-borrowck  -> base diag hir mir
enrang-mono      -> base diag target hir layout mir
enrang-vm        -> base diag target hir layout mir mono
enrang-x86       -> base target
enrang-lir       -> base target
enrang-link      -> base diag target
enrang-codegen   -> base diag target hir layout mir mono x86 lir link
enrang-format    -> base target link
enrang-driver    -> all of the above
enrang-testrun   -> base diag target mono vm driver
enrang-dev       -> base diag target hir mir mono vm driver
enrang-cli       -> diag target syntax driver testrun dev
enrang-testkit   -> base                 (dev-dependency only)
enrang-suite     -> everything, testkit  (test only)
```

### Parallel work rules

- Contract crates (`base`, `hir`, `mir`, `lir`, `target`) change only through their owner. Every other crate has one owner and is the only code in its directory.
- **Text formats decouple the pipeline.** MIR and LIR both have a round-trip text form. A `.mir` fixture is a whole module: declarations, then bodies (section 2.5). `parse_module` builds its own `Tcx` through the public `Tcx::define_adt` and `Tcx::define_fn`, so `enrang-borrowck`, `enrang-vm` and `enrang-codegen` test against `.mir` fixtures, and the emitter against `.lir` fixtures, with no front end. This API lands in `enrang-hir` and `enrang-mir` before M3 work splits. Encoder and writers need nothing from upstream.
- The code registry is generated from SPEC 13.2 at M0 and frozen, so nobody edits a shared error enum. Each code names its phase, owner, milestone and fixture kind, so ownership is never in doubt. Explain pages live in `crates/enrang-diag/explain/EN06.txt`, one file per family.
- Encoder forms live in `crates/enrang-x86/src/forms/<family>.rs`. Library code is split by `lib/` directory. Corpus files are per-feature.
- `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` in every crate except `enrang-testkit`, whose ptrace stepper is the only `unsafe` Rust in the repository.
- **Determinism.** No `HashMap` iteration in anything affecting output: use `IdVec` or `BTreeMap`. Nothing that reaches output or an interface file follows the order of an `IdVec` indexed by `DefId` or `Ty`, whose ids depend on load and interning order: such data is ordered by stable item path (section 6.8). Interface hashes use the compiler's own hash function, never `std`'s, whose output may change between Rust releases. Symbols are ordered by first reach. No timestamps, no absolute paths in output. Two builds of the same input are byte-identical (meta-test, including `TimeDateStamp = 0` on PE). The compiler is single-threaded; only the test runner and the dev watcher use threads.

## 2. Shared contracts

### 2.1 Spans and sources

```rust
pub struct BytePos(pub u32);                  // global offset across all files of a session
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq, Hash)]
pub struct Span { pub lo: BytePos, pub hi: BytePos }
impl Span { pub const DUMMY: Span; pub fn to(self, end: Span) -> Span; }

pub struct FileId(pub u32);
pub enum FileOrigin { User, Library, Snippet { outer: Span } }  // compileFails maps back to the outer file
pub struct SourceFile {
    pub id: FileId, pub path: PathBuf, pub text: Arc<str>,
    pub start: BytePos, pub line_starts: Vec<u32>, pub origin: FileOrigin,
}
pub struct Loc { pub file: FileId, pub line: u32, pub col: u32 } // col counts Unicode scalars from 1 (SPEC 2)
pub struct SourceMap { /* files sorted by start */ }
```

Spans are 8 bytes and global, as in rustc. Line and column are computed only when rendering.

- `Span::DUMMY` is `(0, 0)`, and position 0 belongs to no file: the first file starts at `BytePos(1)`, and one position separates consecutive files, so each file's end-of-file position has a single owner, even for empty files. Build spans as `file.start + offset`, never from a bare offset.
- `Span::to` returns the smallest span enclosing both, in either order, and ignores a `DUMMY` argument.
- `line_starts` are relative to the file, and only LF starts a line, so a bare CR is an ordinary scalar. A position inside a character gets that character's column.
- `Symbol`'s `Ord` compares text rather than id, because ids depend on interning order. `DefId` and `Ty` ids depend on load order in the same way, so they order nothing that reaches output or an interface file (section 1). Section 1's "symbols are ordered by first reach" means linker symbols in output.

### 2.2 Diagnostics

```rust
pub enum Severity { Error, Warning, Fail, Note }  // Fail renders `fail[T0001]`; Note renders `note: text` (SPEC 13.1)
pub struct Label { pub span: Span, pub text: String }
pub enum EntryKind { Note, Why, Rule, FromTypeScript }
pub struct Entry { pub kind: EntryKind, pub text: String }
pub enum Applicability { MachineApplicable, MaybeIncorrect, HasPlaceholders }
pub struct Edit { pub span: Span, pub replacement: String }     // zero-width span = insertion
pub struct Help { pub message: String, pub edits: Vec<Edit>, pub applicability: Applicability }
pub struct Diagnostic {
    pub severity: Severity, pub code: Code, pub message: String,
    pub primary: Label, pub secondary: Vec<Label>,
    pub entries: Vec<Entry>, pub helps: Vec<Help>,
}
pub struct DiagCtx { /* buffered; dedupe on (code, primary span), and on the message too for a spanless diagnostic */ }
```

The code registry is one macro invocation, with one row per line of SPEC 13.2. It generates `enum Code`, `Code::ALL`, the title table and the data the meta-tests read:

```rust
define_codes! {
    EN0601 Error phase(3) owner(borrowck) since(M3) fixture(Compile) "use after move",
    EN0430 Warning phase(2) owner(sema) since(M2) fixture(Compile) "integer division truncates",
    EN0414 Error phase(5) owner(mono) since(M4) fixture(Compile) "monomorphisation depth above 64",
    EN1215 Error phase(R) owner(dev) since(M6) fixture(Reload) "census failure",
    T0001 Fail phase(R) owner(testrun) since(M4) fixture(TestRun) "value matcher found different values",
    /* every code of SPEC 13.2 */
}
pub const CURRENT_MILESTONE: Milestone = Milestone::M0;   // bumped by each milestone's closing commit
```

Meta-tests enforce only rows with `since <= CURRENT_MILESTONE` (`TESTING.md` section 18). The same `since` goes on `define_mir_statements!`, encoder forms, lang items and the extracted G and S cases.

The renderer implements SPEC 13.1 byte for byte: gutter width, annotation line per span with the primary first, `...` gaps, `+`/`~` marks, `= explain`, and the final `error: aborting due to N previous errors`. The G cases of SPEC 14 are its acceptance test. Output is deterministic and colourless unless on a TTY with `--color=auto`.

### 2.3 Tokens and AST

```rust
pub struct Token { pub kind: TokenKind, pub span: Span, pub space_before: bool }  // SPEC 2 and 3.4: drives `?`, `!` and `<`
// No trivia: `enrang fmt` takes comments from the source text between token spans.

pub struct NodeId(pub u32);
pub struct Ident { pub name: Symbol, pub span: Span }
pub struct File {
    pub imports: Vec<Import>,   // named, `* as ns`, or `public import` (a re-export)
    pub stmts: Vec<Stmt>,       // a program's top-level statements and local functions, in order
    pub items: Vec<Item>, pub span: Span,
}
pub struct Item { pub id: NodeId, pub decorators: Vec<Decorator>, pub visibility: Option<Visibility>, pub kind: ItemKind, pub span: Span }
pub enum Visibility { Public, Private, Protected }  // a declaration, `unsafe let` included, states `public` or `private`
                                                    // (missing: EN0226), while `extend`, test items and `assert` state none;
                                                    // class members may also be `protected`, and interface members state none
pub enum ItemKind {
    Function(FunctionDecl), Class(ClassDecl), Enum(EnumDecl), Interface(InterfaceDecl),
    TypeAlias(TypeAlias), TopConst(TopConst), UnsafeLet(UnsafeLet), DeclareFn(DeclareFn),
    Extend(ExtendDecl), StaticAssert { cond: Expr, message: Option<Symbol> },
    Describe(DescribeItem), Test(TestDecl), CompileCase(CompileCase),   // only in a `.test.en` part, else EN1112
}
pub enum Receiver { Plain, Let, Const, Move, Static }   // m(), let m(), const m(), move m(), static m()
pub enum ParamMark { Plain, Let, Const }                // x, let x, const x
pub struct Param { pub mark: ParamMark, pub binding: Binding, pub optional: bool /* x?: T */, pub ty: TypeExpr, pub default: Option<Expr> }
pub struct TypeExpr { pub id: NodeId, pub span: Span, pub kind: TypeExprKind }
pub enum TypeExprKind {
    Named { path: Vec<Ident>, args: Vec<TypeExpr> }, Nullable(Box<TypeExpr>),
    Ref(Box<TypeExpr>), Raw(Box<TypeExpr>),
    ReadonlyRef(Box<TypeExpr>),   // `readonly &T`, only in a field's type, as the field or a reference part of its tuple (SPEC 3.1);
                                  // `readonly` before a return type belongs to the function, not to its type
    Array(Box<TypeExpr>), Fixed(Box<TypeExpr>, Box<Expr>), Tuple(Vec<TypeExpr>), FnPtr(FnPtrType), Closure(ClosureType),
    Object(Vec<(Ident, bool /* x?: T */, TypeExpr)>), StrUnion(Vec<Symbol>), Void, Never, NullLit, Error,
}
pub struct Expr { pub id: NodeId, pub span: Span, pub kind: ExprKind }
pub enum ExprKind {
    Int { value: u128, text: Symbol /* spelling, printed back by fmt */ },
    Float(Symbol /* literal text, parsed once at its final width */), Str(Symbol),
    Template(Vec<TplPart>), RawTemplate(Symbol), Bool(bool), Null, Paren(Box<Expr>),
    Ident(Symbol), This, Array(Vec<ArrayElem>), Repeat(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    TypeMember { ty: TypeExpr, name: Ident },   // Stack<i64>.empty, i64[].withCapacity, E<i64>.V
    New { ty: TypeExpr, init: NewInit },        // new C(args) or new C { f: v }
    CaseLit { path: Vec<Ident>, args: Vec<TypeExpr>, fields: Vec<FieldInit> },   // Shape.Circle { radius: 2.5 }, no `new`
    ObjectLit(Vec<FieldInit>),                  // { f: v }: the expected class's named form, or an anonymous object
    Match { subject: Box<Expr>, arms: Vec<MatchArm> }, SizeForm(SizeForm), Asm(AsmExpr),
    Unary(UnOp, Box<Expr>), Borrow(Box<Expr>), Deref(Box<Expr>), VoidOp(Box<Expr>),
    Binary(BinOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), Logical(LogOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), Coalesce(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Ternary(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), Cast(Box<Expr>, TypeExpr),
    Assign { op: AssignOp, target: Box<Expr>, value: Box<Expr> },   // a value: the assigned value
    IncDec { target: Box<Expr>, inc: bool, prefix: bool },          // a value: new if prefix, else old
    Member { base: Box<Expr>, name: Ident, optional: bool },   // type arguments live only on Call and TypeMember
    Index(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), Call { callee: Box<Expr>, type_args: Vec<TypeExpr>, args: Vec<Expr> },
    Try(Box<Expr>), NonNull(Box<Expr>), Arrow(ArrowFn /* params `[let] x [: T]`, ret: Option<TypeExpr>, body */), Error,
}
pub enum NewInit { Args(Vec<Expr>), Fields(Vec<FieldInit>) }
pub enum ArrayElem { Item(Expr), Spread { dots: Span, expr: Expr } }
pub enum StmtKind {
    VarDecl(VarDecl), If(IfStmt), While(WhileStmt), DoWhile(WhileStmt), For(ForStmt), ForOf(ForOfStmt),
    Return(Option<Expr>), Break(Option<Ident>), Continue(Option<Ident>),
    Block(Block), Unsafe(Block), Labeled(Ident, Box<Stmt>),
    Expr(Expr),                              // a statement `match` drops its optional `;` (SPEC 3.3), which fmt never prints (SPEC 12.5)
    LocalFn { decorators: Vec<Decorator>, decl: FunctionDecl },   // only among a program's top-level statements
    Expect(ExpectStmt),
}
pub struct ForOfStmt { pub mutable: bool, pub binding: Binding, pub source: ForOfSource, pub body: Block, pub span: Span }
pub enum ForOfSource { Range(Expr, Expr), Expr(Expr) }   // no range expression exists, so a range lives here and in ArmValue
pub enum Binding {                                      // SPEC 3.3; destructuring binds, never tests, and nests
    Name(Ident), Object(Vec<(Ident, Option<Binding>)>) /* { a, b: c, d: { e } } */, Tuple(Vec<Binding>) /* [a, [b, c]] */,
}
pub struct MatchArm { pub values: Vec<ArmValue> /* empty for `default` */, pub guard: Option<Expr>, pub body: ArmBody, pub span: Span }
pub enum ArmValue { Value(Expr) /* a literal, a constant, or a condition under match (true) */, Null, Case(Vec<Ident>), Ok, Err, Range(Expr, Expr) }
pub enum ArmBody { Expr(Expr), Block(Block), Return(Option<Expr>), Break(Option<Ident>), Continue(Option<Ident>) }
pub struct ExpectStmt { pub actual: Expr, pub negated: bool, pub matcher: Ident, pub args: Vec<MatcherArg>, pub span: Span }
pub enum MatcherArg { Expr(Expr), Arm(ArmValue) }   // Arm only for toMatchPattern
```

The AST printer is the formatter, `enrang fmt`. It writes the canonical style of SPEC 12.5, takes every comment from the source between token spans, so each keeps its text and order, prints each number literal in its own spelling (`0xB8000` stays `0xB8000`, `1e308` stays `1e308`) and every paren node the source has, and writes a spread as `...e`. It changes a string literal's quotes and escapes only by SPEC 12.5's Quotes rule, and escapes the chosen quote wherever the text holds it, as Prettier does, so a tie or a switch never ends the literal early. It drops the parentheses around a single untyped arrow parameter, and sorts import lines and the names in each import brace list by SPEC 12.5's Imports rule. The key of an import line is its path's value, read after the Quotes rule and never from the source spelling, so a second run sorts as the first did; lines with equal paths keep their order, and a `public import` line sorts with the others by the same key. It never moves a line-sensitive parse: a path and the `{` of its named-form literal stay on one line, and a `<` that starts type arguments keeps no space before it. Print then reparse must give the input's AST with its import lines and import names in that sorted order, and an AST otherwise equal, which is the parser's oracle. The AST records a string literal's value, not its quotes or escapes, and has no parentheses around arrow parameters, so the other changes leave it equal. Printing the output again must change nothing.

### 2.4 Types and typed HIR

```rust
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq, Hash)] pub struct Ty(u32);     // interned; equality is identity
pub enum TyKind {
    Bool, Int(IntTy), Float(FloatTy), Void, Never, Str,
    Array(Ty), Fixed(Ty, u64), Slice(Ty),       // Slice: `Slice<T>`, a view, found only as the pointee of a Ref (SPEC 4.1),
                                                // or as the argument of a type parameter whose `views_ok` holds (section 4)
    Tuple(TyList), Boxed(Ty), Nullable(Ty),
    Ref(Ty), Raw(Ty),                           // one reference kind and one raw kind; read or write is a contract
    FnPtr(Abi, FnPtrSig), Closure(ClosureSig),  // Closure is written only as a parameter type, at most under one Nullable
                                                // at the top (EN0705), never nests, and is also the inferred type of a
                                                // local holding a capturing arrow
    Adt(AdtId, TyList), Param(ParamId),
    Infer(InferVar), Object(ShapeId),           // anonymous object type: structural, so one field set is one type
    Error,
}
pub enum IntTy { I8, I16, I32, I64, U8, U16, U32, U64, Isize, Usize }   // `int` is I32

/// V / R / B / S of SPEC 4.1 as a predicate; every position check calls these two.
/// V: no Ref, no Closure and no borrowing class or tuple anywhere, except that it does not descend
/// into a FnPtr signature. A borrowing class has a reference field; a borrowing tuple has a reference part.
/// An interface type `I` by itself is never in V, so no type parameter stands for one, inferred or written
/// (section 4); `Box<I>` is in V. `&I` is in R wherever a reference may stand: a parameter, a local, a result, a
/// borrowing class's `private` field, a tuple part and `&I | null`. Except as a per-class copy's own non-null `&I`
/// parameter it is two words, the object and its class's table for `I`, an `&I | null` parameter included, and so is
/// every `&I` argument of a call chosen at run time (section 7.7). A view `Slice<U>` is never in V either, but a
/// type parameter that its signature and body use only behind `&` may stand for one (`views_ok`, section 4).
pub fn is_value_type(tcx: &Tcx, ty: Ty) -> bool;
pub fn is_slot_type(tcx: &Tcx, ty: Ty) -> bool;    // S: V, a borrowing class or tuple, `Nullable` of one, or `Ref` or `Nullable(Ref)` of either, `Ref(Nullable(B))` included

pub struct FnSig {
    pub generics: GenericsId, pub where_bounds: Vec<(ParamId, Vec<BoundId>)>,   // method `where` clause
    pub receiver: Option<Receiver>, pub params: Vec<ParamSig>,   // ParamSig carries its Mark
    pub ret: Ty, pub ret_readonly: bool,           // `readonly` before the return type (SPEC 3.1)
    pub unsafety: bool, pub requires: FeatureSet, pub abi: Abi,
}
pub enum Receiver { Ref(Mark), Move, Static }       // m() or let m() or const m(); move m(); static m()
pub enum Mark { None, Let, Const }                  // written marks only (SPEC 5); bodies infer the rest
pub struct FnPtrSig {                               // part of Ty identity, marks and `readonly` included, so mono and
    pub unsafety: bool, pub params: Vec<(Ty, Mark)>, pub ret: Ty, pub ret_readonly: bool,   // layout tell rows apart;
}                                                   // typing unifies two FnPtr types ignoring marks, and phase 3
                                                    // compares the rows (N1, EN0615). Parameter names are not part of it
pub enum RetSources { NoLoans, Params(Vec<u32>) }   // sorted contract indices: index i is argument `_(i+1)` (2.5), `this` 0, hidden ones last
/// What a caller sees of a function (SPEC 5, N1-N14): inferred from the body in phase 3, or built from
/// the marks and SPEC 5.4's defaults where no body can be read or trusted. Type checking never reads one.
pub struct Contract {
    pub perms: Vec<Perm>, pub sources: RetSources, pub result: ResultPerm,
    pub through_fields: Vec<u32>,                   // sources the result reaches only through the reference fields of a
                                                    // borrowing value whose class never writes through them, so it holds
                                                    // no loan on that value (section 6.3)
    pub through_read: Vec<u32>,                     // sources the result reaches only through the `readonly &` fields of
                                                    // a borrowing value, whatever its class writes, so it holds only the
                                                    // loans of that value's read set (section 6.3)
    pub stores: Vec<Store>,
}
pub struct Store {                                  // what parameter `from` holds is stored in the borrowing value behind
    pub from: u32, pub into: u32,                   // parameter `into`, `this` for a method of a borrowing class;
    pub fields_only: bool,                          // fields_only: only what `from`'s reference fields hold (section 6.3)
    pub read_set_only: bool,                        // read_set_only: only what `from`'s `readonly &` fields hold, its read
                                                    // set, whatever its class writes; never also fields_only; with
                                                    // from == into, the caller runs no read-set check (section 6.3)
    pub read: bool,                                 // read: it reaches a `readonly &` field, so the value's read set too
}
pub enum Perm { Read, Write, Own }                  // not by contract index: one per `&` parameter, nullable or not, `this` first,
                                                    // then one per reference part of each by-value borrowing tuple parameter, nested
                                                    // parts included, in parameter and part order (section 6.7); Own only for a `move` method
/// One per borrowing class and borrowing tuple type, inferred with the contracts (section 6.7) and stored in the
/// interface file's hashed section: whether a body writes through a reference read from one of its reference fields,
/// passes one to a Write parameter, or returns one, or a part of one, as a returned reference or a reference part of
/// a returned tuple in a result that may be writable. A borrowing tuple has no methods to infer from, so its flag is
/// always set, and a field that holds one counts as a reference field whose parts `this` puts behind a reference, so
/// it sets its class's flag too, unless every reference part of it, at every depth, is declared `readonly`: a reference
/// to such a field is read-only as a whole (section 6.1). A reference field declared `readonly &` never sets it.
pub struct ClassContract { pub writes_through_fields: bool }
/// A class's field (SPEC 4.3). `readonly` before a reference's `&` is not part of `Ty`, as `ret_readonly` is not: the
/// field lists the reference parts declared so, itself or a part of its tuple, and a reference read from one of them
/// carries a read-only mark, as a reference to a tuple field with such a part does as a whole (section 6.1). The
/// `readonly` modifier is separate: the field is not reassigned after construction. A subclass's `override` of an
/// inherited field adds no `FieldDef`; its class records a new default for that `FieldIdx` (section 5).
pub struct FieldDef {
    pub name: Symbol, pub ty: Ty, pub vis: Visibility, pub readonly: bool,
    pub readonly_refs: Vec<Vec<u32>>,               // tuple positions from the field to each part declared `readonly`; [] is
                                                    // the field itself, declared `readonly &`, and only [] joins the read set (6.2)
}
pub enum ResultPerm { None, ReadOnly(Reason), Writable }   // Reason: the first cause, for the diagnostic
pub struct FnRef { pub def: DefId, pub args: TyList }
pub enum ConstValue { Int(u128, Ty), Float(u64, Ty), Bool(bool), Void, Str(StringId), FnAddr(FnRef), StaticAddr(DefId),
                      Overridden(FieldIdx) }   // in a constructor: whether its instance's override set holds the field;
                                               // mono folds it to a `Bool` (sections 2.6 and 5)
// Generated by define_intrinsics!, with the name table for @intrinsic("...").
// Lives here because HIR names it; enrang-mir re-exports it.
pub enum IntrinsicKind { /* section 2.5 */ }

pub struct Body {
    pub owner: DefId, pub params: Vec<LocalId>, pub locals: IdVec<LocalId, LocalDecl>,
    pub root: Block, pub arrows: IdVec<ArrowId, ArrowBody>, pub ret: Ty,
}
pub struct Block { pub stmts: Vec<Stmt>, pub span: Span }
pub struct Stmt { pub kind: StmtKind, pub span: Span }
pub enum StmtKind {
    Let { local: LocalId, init: Option<Expr> },     // no init: the type's default (SPEC O3), or unassigned if it has none
    Destructure { pat: Pat, mode: BindingMode, init: Expr },
    If { cond: Expr, then_: Block, else_: Option<Block> },
    While { id: LoopId, cond: Expr, body: Block }, DoWhile { id: LoopId, body: Block, cond: Expr },
    For { id: LoopId, init: Option<Box<Stmt>>, cond: Option<Expr>, step: Option<Box<Stmt>>, body: Block },
    ForOf { id: LoopId, pat: Pat, source: ForSource, body: Block },
    Return(Option<Expr>), Break(LoopId), Continue(LoopId),
    Block(Block), Unsafe(Block), Expr(Expr), Expect(ExpectForm),
}
pub enum BindingMode { Ref, Owned }   // SPEC 5.6, set by the source: a reference gives references; an owned value copies or moves each part
pub enum ForSource {
    Path { place: Expr },                           // a borrowed path of array type with an empty `adjust`; read or write is phase 3's
    Once { value: Expr, mode: BindingMode },        // Ref: a borrowed place or a reference rvalue; Owned: the loop takes it
    Range { lo: Expr, hi: Expr },
}
pub enum Pat { Bind(LocalId), Object(Vec<(FieldIdx, Pat)>), Tuple(Vec<Pat>) }   // destructuring only, nesting; arms bind nothing
pub struct Arm { pub values: Vec<ArmValue> /* empty for `default` */, pub guard: Option<Expr>, pub body: ArmBody, pub span: Span }
pub enum ArmValue { Const(ConstValue), Null, Case(VariantIdx) /* `Ok` and `Err` too */, Range(ConstValue, ConstValue), Cond(Expr) }
pub enum ArmBody { Expr(Expr), Block(Block), Return(Option<Expr>), Break(LoopId), Continue(LoopId) }
pub enum Callee {
    Fn(FnRef), BoundMember { param: ParamId, member: Symbol, args: TyList }, FnPtr(Box<Expr>),
    Closure(LocalId) /* a closure parameter, narrowed when nullable, or a local holding an arrow */, Foreign(DefId),
    Intrinsic(IntrinsicKind, TyList), HostCall(HostCallId),
    Form(FormKind),                                 // SPEC 6, compiler forms, `Err(ErrForm)` among them
}
pub enum TplPart { Text(StringId), Hole(Expr) }
pub enum TryKind { Result, ResultIntoFailure /* `?` into `Result<T>` from another error type keeps its text */, Nullable }
pub enum ErrForm {                                  // how `Err(x)` makes its `E` (SPEC 6), decided in typing
    Value,                                          // `x` is already an `E`
    Text,                                           // a fixed text or template at `Failure`, or a fixed text at a literal-union `E`
    TextMove,                                       // a `string` value at `Failure`, moved in with no copy
    TextCopy,                                       // an operand of type `&string`, copied at `Failure`; a `string` place
                                                    // no move may leave is never copied (EN0603-EN0605, section 4)
    IntoFailure,                                    // an error of another type at `Result<T>` keeps its text, as `?` does
}
pub enum CastKind { IntToInt, IntToFloat, FloatToFloat, FloatToInt, EnumToInt, BoolToInt, UsizeToRaw, RawToUsize, RefToRaw, RawToRaw }
pub enum ConvKind { FixedToSlice, ArrayToSlice, Upcast, BoxToInterface, RefToInterface }
                                     // C18, C21, C22: each `Adjust` of that name lowers to one `Rvalue::Convert`
                                     // (sections 2.5 and 5)
pub struct OptChain { pub base: Expr, pub member: OptMember, pub void_result: bool }
pub enum OptMember { Field(FieldIdx), Call { callee: Callee, args: Vec<Expr> } }
pub struct Expr { pub ty: Ty, pub span: Span, pub kind: ExprKind, pub adjust: Vec<Adjust> }
pub enum Adjust {          // SPEC 4.5 coercions made explicit; MIR building reads only these. Borrow modes are phase 3's
    AutoBorrow, Reborrow, ReadThrough, ReadThroughNullable, WrapNull, WrapNullRef, WrapOk /* `return v` in a `Result` function */,
    BoxDeref, NeverToAny, LiteralDescriptor, FnToPtr, FnToUnsafePtr, ArrowToClosure, ClosureReborrow,
    FixedToSlice /* C18: `&T[N]` to `&Slice<T>`, its address and static length (SPEC 11) */,
    ArrayToSlice /* C18: a whole `&T[]` to `&Slice<T>`, the array's `ptr` and `len` */,
    Upcast /* C21: `&D` or `Box<D>` to `&A` or `Box<A>`; also after a receiver's AutoBorrow or Reborrow when the method
              found is inherited, to the class that declares it, as for `super.m()` (section 4) */,
    BoxToInterface /* C22: `Box<I>` adds the class's table for `I` (7.7), read through the hidden pointer if the class can be extended */,
    RefToInterface /* C22: an `&C`, or a per-class copy's own `&I` parameter, becomes a two-word `&I` anywhere but at a
                      non-null `&I` parameter (a local, a result, a field, a tuple part, `&I | null`, an `&I | null`
                      parameter included, before `WrapNullRef`), adding the table as BoxToInterface does */,
    // C20, and C22's `I` and non-null `&I` at a parameter, need none: one instance per shape or class resolves them,
    // except at a call chosen at run time, where mono attaches the table to an `&I` argument whose class is known (2.6).
    // C22 never takes a `&string`, whole or a part, to any `&I`, parameter or not: it may be a part, whose length an
    // `&I` has no word for, so it is EN0401 (4); a `Box<string>` and a by-value `string` hold a whole descriptor.
    // Nor does an instance at `T := string` meet one: binding `string` to a `T` without `strings_ok` is EN0401 (4).
    // A two-word `&I`, taken from a `Box<I>` (`BoxDeref`, then the borrow) or read from a local, a result, a field, a
    // tuple part or a nullable, copies its two words and reaches the dynamic copy (2.6)
}
pub enum ExprKind {
    // places
    Local(LocalId), Static(DefId), Field(Box<Expr>, FieldIdx), Deref(Box<Expr>), Index(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    NarrowedPayload(Box<Expr>, VariantIdx),   // CheckTag + (as V): `Present` for a non-null fact, the case inside its arm
    // values
    Const(ConstValue), Template(Vec<TplPart>), ArrayLit(Vec<Expr>), Repeat(Box<Expr>, u64), TupleLit(Vec<Expr>),
    ArrayBuild { elem: Ty, parts: Vec<ArrayPart> },  // an array literal with a spread; ArrayLit has none
    NewNamed { adt: AdtId, args: TyList, fields: Vec<(FieldIdx, Expr)>, default_from: Vec<FieldIdx> },  // new C { ... }, or { ... } at a class type
    ObjectLit { shape: ShapeId, fields: Vec<Expr>, eval: Vec<u32> },  // an anonymous object, fields in shape order, evaluated as written (`eval`, O6)
    VariantLit { adt: AdtId, variant: VariantIdx, args: TyList, fields: Vec<Expr>, eval: Vec<u32> },  // declaration order, evaluated as written
    New { ctor: DefId, args: TyList, values: Vec<Expr> },
    Call { callee: Callee, receiver: Option<Box<Expr>>, args: Vec<Expr> },   // O6 takes the receiver's auto-borrow last
    Unary(UnOp, Box<Expr>), Binary(BinOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), Logical(LogOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Coalesce(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>), OptionalChain(Box<OptChain>), Ternary(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Cast(Box<Expr>, CastKind), Borrow(Box<Expr>), AddrOf(Box<Expr>),
    Assign { op: Option<BinOp>, target: Box<Expr>, value: Box<Expr> }, IncDec { target: Box<Expr>, inc: bool, prefix: bool },
    Match { subject: Box<Expr>, arms: Vec<Arm> }, Try(Box<Expr>, TryKind), NonNull(Box<Expr>),
    Arrow(ArrowId), Asm(Box<AsmExpr>), VoidOp(Box<Expr>), Never,
}
pub enum ArrayPart { Item(Expr), Spread(Expr, SpreadKind) }  // the operand carries its borrow adjust, which only reads
pub enum SpreadKind { Array, Fixed(u64), Slice }
/// A `Local` (`this` is a local), `Field(b)` or `NarrowedPayload(b, _)` with `b` a path, or `Deref(b)`
/// with `b` a path of type `Ref` or `Boxed`. Everything else is false: `Index`, `Static`, `Call`, a raw `Deref`.
pub fn is_path_place(e: &Expr) -> bool;
```

**Classifying a `for...of` source (SPEC 5.6).** Sema alone classifies, after typing and member lookup. Mirbuild only matches the enum, and borrowck sees only MIR. A loop can therefore not be checked as one shape and built as another, and a test-mode HIR check asserts `is_path_place` on every `Path`. A source whose type, after peeling one reference, is not `Array`, `Fixed` or `Slice` is EN1015. The first rule that fits wins:

1. A range from `ForOfSource::Range` is `Range`.
2. Strip one leading `Borrow(inner)`. If `inner` is a path, the result is `Path { place: inner }`. If `inner` is any other place, the result is `Once` in `Ref` mode over a borrow of it: `Index` (`&grid[r]`), `Static`, a raw `Deref`, a projection from a temporary. If `inner` is an rvalue, it is `Once` in `Ref` mode over a borrow of its statement temporary (`&make()`).
3. A path of reference type is `Path { place: Deref(e) }`: a source that is already a reference borrows.
4. A path of array type, with no `&`, is `Once` in `Owned` mode: the loop takes it, and a later use is EN0601. Taking a path through a reference, such as `this.items`, is EN0603 in phase 3.
5. A reference-typed rvalue is `Once` in `Ref` mode: a call, a ternary, `r!`.
6. Anything else is `Once` in `Owned` mode: an owned rvalue such as `[...q]`, `q.clone()` or a call returning an array, or a place whose move the move rules then check (EN0604 for an element).

Capture analysis treats `Path` as a borrow of `place`, because the `Borrow` node is gone; phase 3 decides its mode (section 6.2).

This contract is frozen before M2, so the sema and mirbuild owners can work in parallel against it. It has no `Mutability` in references, borrows or receivers: whether a borrow reads or writes is decided in phase 3 (section 6.7). Before the freeze it also gains the `contract(def)` query (section 6.7) and stable item paths (section 6.8), so that no contract and no interface entry names a `DefId`. Bodies are generic: `T` stays `Param`. `Copy` is a predicate consulted at MIR building; a `T` not bounded by `Copy` is non-Copy (SPEC 4.10).

### 2.5 MIR

A CFG of three-address statements over places. Generic until mono. No unwinding edges, so a panic is a diverging action inside a statement and never affects the shape of the CFG.

```rust
pub struct Local(pub u32);    // _0 is the return slot, _1.._n the arguments; _1 is `this: &C` for a `&` or `move` receiver and in a constructor
pub struct BlockId(pub u32);
pub enum MirPhase { Built, Elaborated, Mono }
pub struct Body {
    pub owner: BodyOwner, pub phase: MirPhase, pub arg_count: u32,
    pub locals: IdVec<Local, LocalDecl>, pub blocks: IdVec<BlockId, Block>,
    pub captures: Vec<Capture>, pub drop_flags: Vec<(Local, MovePathKey)>, pub span: Span,
    pub names: BodyNames,   // adt -> FieldIdx -> Symbol and adt -> VariantIdx -> Symbol, for remap_ids
    pub unsafe_regions: IdVec<RegionId, UnsafeRegion>,   // each statement and terminator records the innermost one it lies in
}
pub struct UnsafeRegion { pub kind: UnsafeKind, pub parent: Option<RegionId>, pub span: Span }
pub enum UnsafeKind { Block, Asm, WholeBody }      // `unsafe { }`, an `asm`, or an `unsafe function` or `@naked` body
pub enum BodyOwner { Fn(DefId), Arrow(DefId, ArrowId), Statements(ModuleId), Shim(ShimKind) }  // Statements: a program's top-level statements
pub enum LocalKind { Return, Arg, User, Temp, DropFlag, HiddenLoop(LoopRole), ClosureEnv }
pub enum LoopRole { Index, Length, Source, IntoIter, RangeCursor, RangeEnd }  // printed `#i`, `#n`, `#src`, `#iter`, `#cur`, `#end`
pub struct LocalDecl { pub ty: Ty, pub mutable: bool, pub name: Option<Symbol>, pub span: Span, pub kind: LocalKind }

pub struct Place { pub local: Local, pub proj: ProjId }  // interned projection list
pub enum Proj { Field(FieldIdx), Deref, Variant(VariantIdx), Index(Local), ConstIndex(u64) }
pub enum Operand { Copy(Place), Move(Place), Const(ConstValue) }
pub enum BorrowOrigin { Explicit, AutoBorrow, Reborrow, Destructure, ForOfElement, ForOfHidden, ClosureCapture, HiddenArg, TempArg,
                        CompoundTarget, LiteralView }   // the place of a compound assignment that isn't Copy, a reservation
                                                        // that turns exclusive at the store (section 6.2), and a constant
                                                        // literal view such as `&[2, 3]` (section 5)
pub enum DropOrigin { Scope, Assign }                            // Assign: the old value in SPEC 5.7 D3
pub enum ClassOp { Unbox, Assign, CopyOut, MoveThis(Symbol), Replace, Swap, Clone }
                                     // the whole-object copy or move a CheckClass guards (section 5), MoveThis at a `move` method's
                                     // entry; the panic prints `unbox`, assignment, copy, the `move` method's name, `mem.replace`,
                                     // `mem.swap`, `clone` (SPEC 9.1)
pub enum ClassHolder { Box, Ref }                                // what reaches the object: "the box holds" or "the reference points at"
pub enum Rvalue {
    Use(Operand),
    Ref { place: Place, origin: BorrowOrigin },                  // every loan site; phase 3 decides shared or exclusive (N4)
    AddrOf(Place),                                               // raw pointer, no loan
    Binary(BinOp, Operand, Operand),                             // wrapping or comparison
    Checked(CheckedOp, Operand, Operand),                        // panics on overflow, zero divisor, shift range
    Unary(UnOp, Operand), CheckedNeg(Operand),
    Cast(CastKind, Operand, Ty),
    Convert(ConvKind, Operand),                                  // C18, C21, C22 on a reference or a `Box`: the operand's
                                                                 // loans and marks carry over (6.1, 6.2)
    Aggregate(AggregateKind, Vec<Operand>),
    Discriminant(Place), Len(Place),                             // Len yields isize; mono folds it on Fixed; on a
                                                                 // Slice it reads the reference's length word
    ClosureValue { func: ClosureFn, env: Place },                // enum ClosureFn { Arrow(ArrowId), Named(FnRef), FnValue } (5)
    ClosureReborrow(Place),                                      // passing a closure parameter (SPEC 5.5)
}
define_mir_statements! {            // generates StatementKind plus ALL, for the traceability meta-test
    Assign { dest: Place, value: Rvalue },
    CheckBounds { index: Operand, len: Operand },               // panics; address computed afterwards (O6)
    CheckTag { place: Place, variant: VariantIdx },             // in the run of checks before the projection, same block
    CheckClass { place: Place, op: ClassOp, via: ClassHolder }, // panics unless the object is exactly the place's class;
                                                                // in the run of checks before the move (section 5)
    SetTable { place: Place, class: Ty },                       // writes `class`'s method table into the object's hidden
                                                                // pointer: first at a `new`, the class built, or in the
                                                                // `NamedFill` of a named form (section 5), and in drop
                                                                // glue before a base's cleanup (2.6)
    FieldInit { field: Place, value: Operand },                 // a field initialiser's or type default's store in a
                                                                // constructor of a class that can be extended, or in an
                                                                // implicit constructor, closing the blocks that compute
                                                                // `value`, which a `Switch` on `Overridden(f)` enters;
                                                                // an unconditional init (section 5)
    StorageLive(Local), StorageDead(Local),
    Drop(Place, DropOrigin),                                     // Built phase only
    DropGlue(Place),                                             // Elaborated: run the type's glue
    Intrinsic { kind: IntrinsicKind, args: Vec<Operand>, dest: Option<Place> },
    Asm(Box<AsmStmt>),
    HostCall { id: HostCallId, args: Vec<Operand>, dest: Option<Place> },  // legal only in os/vm
    Nop,
}
pub enum Callee {
    Fn(FnRef), FnPtr(Operand), ClosureLocal(Local), Foreign(ForeignRef),
    Virtual { method: FnRef, table: Operand },   // through a method table: the receiver place's last step dereferences a
                                                 // `&C` or `Box<C>` of a class that isn't `final`, or it is `this` in such
                                                 // a class, and the method can be overridden (not `private`, not
                                                 // `static`, not compiled once per type, as `show<T>` is, 2.6); or
                                                 // an interface `Box` or a two-word `&I` (its second word).
                                                 // For a class, `method` is the nearest declaration at or above `C`,
                                                 // whose slot the call loads (2.6). Every other receiver place of type
                                                 // `C` (an element, a tuple part, a temporary, a local) is `Fn` (6.3).
                                                 // A call on a `T extends C` becomes one in an instance whose class can
                                                 // be extended (2.6, 6.3)
}
pub enum Terminator {
    Goto(BlockId),
    Switch { value: Operand, arms: Vec<(u128, BlockId)>, otherwise: Option<BlockId> },
    Call { callee: Callee, args: Vec<Operand>, dest: Place, next: Option<BlockId> },  // None: returns never
    Return, Unreachable,
}
define_intrinsics! {        // in enrang-hir; generates IntrinsicKind, ALL, the @intrinsic name table and a safety bit
                            // per kind: set for a kind that a safe declaration names, as `SizeOf` is, or that mirbuild
                            // emits in safe code, as `AllocArray` is for an array literal; clear for `SliceFromRaw`, the
                            // memory and volatile kinds and every other kind only an `unsafe` declaration names. The
                            // verifier reads it (below), and a meta-test compares it with every `@intrinsic` declaration
    VolatileRead, VolatileWrite, ReadUnaligned, WriteUnaligned, MemCopy, MemCopyOverlap, MemSet,
    AtomicLoad, AtomicStore, AtomicExchange, AtomicAlu(AluOp), AtomicIncDecTest(bool),
    AtomicBitTest(bool), AtomicFence, AtomicCompareExchange, AtomicFetchAdd,
    MulWideU32,                       // (a, b) -> { lo, hi }
    DivWideU32,                       // (hi, lo, d) -> { q, r }; precondition hi < d, checked by the VM
    CpuHas(Feature), SizeOf(Ty), AlignOf(Ty), OffsetOf(Ty, FieldIdx), AllocArray(Ty), ToBitsCanon, FromBits,
    SliceFromRaw(Ty),                 // (p: *T, n: isize) -> &Slice<T>: the views of `slice`, `splitAt` and `fromRaw` (section 10)
}
```

A `Place` root is always a local. **Statics and constants** are reached through a raw pointer: `_t = const static_addr(X); (*_t).f = ...`. So a static access creates no loan, consistent with SPEC 5.9, and `&CONST` is a reference with an empty loan set, as is a constant literal view once elaboration makes it a static (section 5). Generic `SizeOf`, `AlignOf` and `OffsetOf` carry their type, so mono can fold them.

**`unsafe` regions are recorded explicitly.** `Body.unsafe_regions` holds each `unsafe { }` block, each `asm` and, for an `unsafe function` or an `@naked` function, the whole body, and every statement and terminator records the innermost region it lies in. Whether an operation sits inside `unsafe` is therefore a fact of the MIR itself, which stage 3 of section 6.7 reads to find the bodies that contain `unsafe` or `asm`, nested arrows included, and never a claim stored beside the code, in a fixture or in an interface file (section 6.8). The text form prints each statement's region.

`Index` projections work on `T[N]`, `T[]` and `Slice<T>`, whose length is the second word of the `&Slice<T>` that reaches it; the element address is computed at lowering. **Text form.** A fixture is a module: declarations, then bodies. `print_body` emits the declarations a body references, so the round trip holds, and mono fixtures add an `instantiate` list. A declaration with no body states its contract as a body-less Enrang position does (SPEC 4.4): `let` before a reference parameter it writes, `let` or `move` before the name, `readonly` before a read-only result type, and `sources(...)`, plus `through_fields(...)`, `through_read(...)` and `stores(...)`, each store with its read bit, where it stands for a function with a body, and a class declaration states `writes_through_fields`, so a borrowck fixture needs no callee body. Built MIR records no borrow modes; phase 3 computes them.

```text
class Stack<T> { items: T[]; }
copy class P { x: i64; }
fn Stack<T>::pop(let _1: &Stack<T>) -> T|null sources();
instantiate Stack<i64>::pop;

fn pop2(_1: &Stack<i64>) -> Result<i64,Failure> {
  let _2: i64|null;  // v
  let _3: &Stack<i64>;
  let _4: u32;
  bb0: { _3 = &*_1 [reborrow]; _2 = Stack<i64>::pop(move _3) -> bb1; }
  bb1: { _4 = discriminant(_2); switch _4 [0: bb2, otherwise: bb3]; }
  ...
}
```

The verifier runs after every pass in tests, and in every build, release included, on each body read from an interface file (section 6.8): locals declared once, types agree, `HostCall` only in `os/vm` bodies, `SetTable` only on a class with a hidden pointer, by the current declarations, and only in a `DropGlue` shim, in a `NamedFill` shim or as the first write to the object a `new` builds, every recorded region inside its parent, `Drop` absent after elaboration, no `Param` or `Infer` after mono, every `CheckTag`, `CheckBounds` and `CheckClass` in a run of checks, in one block, that ends directly before the statement using it, or before the block's `Call` for a `CheckClass` on the object of a call to `unbox` or `clone`, or before the borrow that passes it for one on an object of `mem.replace` or `mem.swap` (section 5), with nothing else between, except a `MoveThis` check, which is the first statement of a `move` method's body, nothing but the compare, the `Switch` and `StorageLive(x)` between a `for...of` head's `Len` and its `CheckBounds`, no `Move` of a reference except from a `Temp`, every `Convert` between the pairs its kind allows, `&T[N]` to `&Slice<T>` for `FixedToSlice`, `&T[]` to `&Slice<T>` for `ArrayToSlice`, `&D` or `Box<D>` to `&A` or `Box<A>` for a base class `A` of `D` for `Upcast`, `Box<C>` to `Box<I>` for `BoxToInterface` and `&C` to `&I` for `RefToInterface`, each for a `C` that satisfies `I` by the current declarations, a `FieldInit` only in a constructor of a class that can be extended or in an implicit constructor (section 5), and only in blocks entered through a `Switch` on its own field's `Overridden`, a `FieldInit` for each field the constructor's class overrides before its `super(...)` on every path, and, after mono, a constructor instance's override set naming only inherited fields that the classes below it can see, and, for an implicit constructor that a `NamedFill` reaches, the fields its named form names.

In `Built` MIR, which is what an interface file stores (section 6.8), it also checks that every run-time guard that lowering places is there, since phase 3, mono, codegen and the VM rely on each: a `CheckBounds` on the same index operand, and on a `Len` of the indexed place, before every `Index`, and before every `ConstIndex` on a `T[]` or a view, while a `ConstIndex` on a `T[N]` or a tuple must lie below its static length; a `CheckTag` of the projected case before every `Variant` projection, which mirbuild emits even directly after the `Switch` that chose the case, in `?`, `??` and `match` alike, so the rule has no exception; a `CheckClass` with the matching `ClassOp` before every event of section 5's exact-class list whose object has a class that can be extended, by the current declarations, or a type parameter; each `Intrinsic` inside a recorded `unsafe` region unless its kind's safety bit is set; a `Callee::Virtual` only where its comment allows one, on a receiver whose class can be extended, by the current declarations, calling a method that can be overridden, or on an interface `Box` or a two-word `&I`; and `SetTable` only on a class with a hidden pointer, as above. So forged stored bodies that index with no `CheckBounds`, project `(*_1 as Present).0` with no `CheckTag`, assign a whole object through `&Animal` with no `CheckClass`, run `SliceFromRaw` outside every region, or make a `Virtual` call or a `SetTable` on a `final` class with no base, are EN0316 each, while a guarded `Index` and a `SizeOf` outside `unsafe` load (`TESTING.md` `MD3-02-guards`).

On a stored body it also checks the types against the current declarations, which the body names by stable item path, and every operation that needs `unsafe` (EN0801, EN0803, EN0907) against the regions the body records, and it reruns every phase-2 rule whose verdict phase 3, mono, codegen or the VM rely on, over the file's stored declarations and bodies and against the current declarations, never a fixed list of a few. These are: member and item visibility (EN0308, EN0311) for every field, method, constructor and item the stored code names; the class rules EN0416, EN0418, EN0442, EN0443 and EN0444, with SPEC 4.3's equal signature, field type, `readonly` and visibility for an override, EN0445, EN0446, EN0457, EN0458, which keeps out of stored code every call that would enter an inherited `move` method through a receiver whose static type is a class, and EN0460, which keeps every method compiled once per type out of the table; bound satisfaction (EN0419) for every type argument of the stored code, and the view and string checks of section 4 (EN0401), against the `views_ok` and `strings_ok` the check recomputes, for every one bound to a view or to `string`, and the string clause of satisfaction for every satisfaction the stored code relies on (section 4); the construction rules EN0433, EN0447 and EN0448 for every stored `new` and named form; and the position rules EN0701 to EN0703 and EN1006 for every type in the stored declarations and in every local, field, element and generic argument of the stored code. So a forged `poke` that writes through `View`'s `private r`, a stored `describe(): i64` overriding `describe(): string`, a stored `new Shape()` of an abstract class and a stored `(&i64)[]` local are each EN0316 (`TESTING.md` `MD3-01-private`, `MD3-01-override`, `MD3-01-abstract`, `MD3-01-position`), and a genuine file never fails this, since no named form in another module writes a `private` field: the declaring module fills the fields it leaves out (section 5, `MD3-01-named`). Any failure on load is EN0316, naming the file; running the verifier in release builds costs compile time only.

### 2.6 Elaboration, monomorphisation, glue

```text
hir::Body -> mirbuild -> mir(Built) -> contracts -> borrowck -> elaborate_drops -> mir(Elaborated, generic)
          -> mono worklist -> mir(Mono) -> { enrang-vm predecode | enrang-codegen lower }
```

**Checks run once on generic MIR**, so instantiation cannot add a type or borrow error. Only EN0414 (depth above 64), EN0435 (`layout_of` on a concrete type, which returns an error and never wraps) and EN0820 depend on an instance. EN0820 does so for a `?` or an `Err(e)` into `Result<T>` in a generic body whose error type is a type parameter, since whether the conversion builds a text at run time depends on `E` (section 5): with no `@globalAllocator`, `lift<T, E>(r: Result<T, E>): Result<T> { return r?; }` compiles as `lift<u8, BusError>` and is EN0820 as `lift<u8, DiskError>`. It is reported in phase 5 with the reach chain, its primary label at the instantiating call, `lift(readSector(7))`, a note at the `?`, and a help that names the generic form, `` help: return `Result<T, E>` to pass the error on as it is ``. Sealed bounds keep EN0805 and EN0910 out of mono (SPEC 4.10).

**Drop elaboration** runs on generic `Built` MIR using the init dataflow that borrowck already computed. It also turns each `LiteralView` borrow that borrowck found shared into a static (section 5). Each `Drop(p)` becomes one of:

| Init state of `p` at the point | Rewrite |
|---|---|
| Definitely uninit | Removed |
| Definitely init, no child moved | `DropGlue(p)` |
| Maybe init | Flag local plus guard; init sites set the flag, moves clear it, including a parent's move clearing its children's flags |
| Partly moved class, tuple or anonymous object | Ladder over remaining fields in reverse declaration order (an object's in reverse shape order), each static or flagged. A class with a destructor, and a value of a type parameter, are never partly moved (EN0607) |
| Partly moved enum, nullable or `Result` | `Switch` on the tag first, then a ladder for the active variant only. A flag is meaningful only inside its variant's arm |

The tag switch is what makes `f(null, true)` safe for `public function f(o: string | null, c: bool): void { if (o != null && c) { take(o); } }`. `Body.drop_flags` records which flag belongs to which move path. The dev runtime uses that map when rebasing frames (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 6). A flag may also start from a mono constant: in a constructor, a field with no default of its own that a class below may override starts maybe-initialised, with its flag set at entry from `Overridden(f)` (section 6.1), and mono folds it, so the D3 drop of the constructor's assignment to that field remains only in the instances whose override set holds it (section 5).

**Mono worklist.** Roots: `_start` lang item, then the entry (a program's top-level statements, `main` or `@entry`), `@panicHandler`, `@globalAllocator` methods, `.boot*` functions and statics, and the test table in test builds. For each `Instance { owner, args, overrides }`:
1. Clone the elaborated body and substitute. A type parameter that stands for a view `Slice<U>` (section 4) appears only under a `Ref`, so `&T` becomes `&Slice<U>`, two words, the address and the length (SPEC 11), as an `&I` outside a per-class copy's parameters is two words.
2. Resolve `T.m(...)` by member lookup on the concrete type (static structural dispatch, SPEC 4.4). Satisfaction has already fixed the member's exact shape, and auto-deref never contributes here. A function taking an anonymous object type or an interface type by value or by reference gets one instance per shape or class passed, the same way. In a copy or generic instance for a class that can be extended, an interface or bound member call is a `Virtual` call through the object's own table, never a static call to the concrete type's member, because a `&Animal` may hold a `Dog` and the object's own method runs (SPEC 4.3), except a call to a member compiled once per type, which has no slot and is direct (below); this covers a call on a `T extends Animal` (section 6.3), while in an instance for a `final` class the call is static. An `&I` that is not a per-class copy's own non-null `&I` parameter, one taken from a `Box<I>` or held in a local, a result, a field, a tuple part or an `&I | null`, has no known class: it is two words, the object and its class's table for `I`, as a box carries them (section 7.7). An `&I | null` parameter is no implicit type parameter and always has that form, in every copy, so a function gets per-class copies only for a non-null `&I` or a by-value `I` parameter, and one copy of `greet(s: &Speaker | null)` takes both `greet(&d)` and `greet(null)`, which writes 0 to both words (`TESTING.md` `VI-09-null`). A per-class copy that returns, stores or wraps its `&I` parameter, or passes it where a two-word `&I` is expected, attaches its class's table there (`RefToInterface`, section 2.4), read through the hidden pointer when the class can be extended, so `pick(a: &Speaker, b: &Speaker, first: bool): &Speaker` returns `b` with `Robot`'s table and `console.log(pick(&rex, &robo, false).speak());` prints `beep` (`TESTING.md` `S2-17-pick`). A function with an interface parameter therefore gets one more instance, the dynamic copy, made only when such a two-word reference reaches it, at a call chosen at run time too (below); its implicit type parameters are the interfaces themselves, so `Ref(I)` keeps its two-word layout there, every member call on an interface parameter in it is a `Virtual` call through that table word, and an argument whose class is known travels with that class's table for `I`, read through the object's hidden pointer when the class can be extended, since the reference may reach a subclass (section 7.7). So `for (const s of &all) { introduce(s); }` over a `Box<Speaker>[]` reaches the dynamic copy of `introduce`, while `introduce(&rex)` still reaches the copy for `Dog` (SPEC 4.4). A by-value `I` has no such copy: `b.unbox()` on a `Box<I>` is EN1006 in phase 2, since its size is not known. Per-class copies serve direct calls only: at a call whose callee is chosen at run time, a `Virtual` call, a call through an interface table, or one through an `fn` value or a closure, every `&I` or `&I | null` argument travels in the two-word form, mono attaching its class's table where the class is known, and the method-table slot, the interface-table entry, and the target of `FnToPtr` or `FnAsClosure`, name the method's or function's dynamic copy, so each still holds one function and the table order of section 6.8 is unchanged. An `&Self` result of a call through a table is paired with the receiver's table. So with a `pet: Box<Animal>` holding a `Dog` whose `hear(s: &Speaker)` override returns `` `dog hears ${s.speak()}` ``, `console.log(pet.hear(&r), pet.hear(&p));` prints `dog hears beep dog hears hello`, and `const f: fn(s: &Speaker) => string = describe;` makes `f(&r)` reach `describe`'s dynamic copy (`TESTING.md` `VI-06-tables`). A method with type parameters of its own, a by-value interface parameter or an object-type parameter is compiled once per type, so no one function could fill a slot, an interface-table entry or an `fn` value for it, and none is ever made: such a method is never overridable, has no method-table slot, and is always called directly with its declaring class's body, an `override` of it or an `abstract` one being EN0460 (section 4), so with `Animal.show<T>(x: &T)` and a `Dog` that has no `show` of its own, a call through `p: &Animal` holding a `Dog` runs `Animal.show` at each type it is called with. An interface with such a member is still a bound and an `&I` parameter, which per-class copies resolve, but never needs an interface table: a `Box<I>` of it, and an `&I` of it wherever that takes the two-word form, are EN1006, as for a member that takes or returns `Self` by value (section 4), so a `Box<Dumper>` with `Dumper.dump<T>(x: &T)` is EN1006. A function with a by-value `I` parameter has one copy per class and no dynamic copy (above), so converting it to an `fn` value, or passing it as a closure, is EN1006 too. `fmt` on a class or enum with no written `fmt`, of its own or inherited, resolves to its `DisplayGlue` shim, as a synthesised `clone` resolves to `CloneGlue`, because every class satisfies `Display` (SPEC 4.4). A `Virtual` call, through a reference to a class that can be extended, through an interface `Box` or through a two-word `&I` (SPEC 4.3, 4.4), enqueues the method's override in every class that mono has reached, its dynamic copy where it has an `&I` or `&I | null` parameter, and each such class's method table.
3. Erase `DropGlue` where `needs_drop(ty)` is false; otherwise point it at a shim instance. Erase `CheckClass` where its place's type is not a class that can be extended, so it stays only for a class that is not `final`, generic bodies included once `T` is known.
4. Fold `SizeOf`, `AlignOf`, `OffsetOf`, statically decided `CpuHas` and, in a constructor, `Overridden(f)`, true exactly when the instance's override set holds `f`, which removes the initialisers and type defaults of the fields a class below overrides, with their `FieldInit`s, and the drop guards that can no longer fire (section 5).
5. Enqueue callees, a `super(...)` call at the base constructor's instance for this instance's set with its own class's overrides added, a `NamedFill`'s call at its class's implicit constructor for the fields its form names, every function whose address is taken (`FnAddr`, including inside constants), and referenced statics, recording `(instance, first_reach_span, parent)` for reachability diagnostics.

```rust
pub enum ShimKind {
    DropGlue(Ty), CloneGlue(Ty), EqGlue(Ty), ShowGlue(Ty),  // Eq and Show only in test builds
    DisplayGlue(Ty),                                        // `fmt` of a class or enum with no written `fmt`, own or inherited
    FnAsClosure(FnRef),                                     // named function passed as closure: drops the env argument
    StringForm(FnRef),                                      // reaches a function whose `&string` forms differ from the row it is called by
    StringFormEnv { from: Ty, to: Ty },                     // the same for an `fn` or closure value passed to a closure parameter
                                                            // of row `to`: a closure whose env is that value
    NamedFill(Ty),                                          // a named form's fill function: writes the class's table, if any, then
                                                            // calls its implicit constructor for the fields the form names (section 5)
}
pub struct Instance {
    pub owner: BodyOwner, pub args: TyList,
    pub overrides: Vec<FieldIdx>,   // a constructor's override set, sorted (section 5), with the fields a named form names
                                    // for an implicit constructor its `NamedFill` reaches; for a `NamedFill`, the fields its
                                    // form names, one instance per set of left-out fields; empty for every other body
}
pub struct MonoProgram {
    pub target: Arc<TargetSpec>, pub instances: IdVec<InstanceId, Instance>, pub bodies: IdVec<InstanceId, Body>,
    pub statics: IdVec<StaticId, StaticDef>, pub strings: StringTable, pub entry: InstanceId,
    pub layouts: LayoutTable, pub reach: ReachTree,
}
```

Glue shims are synthesised MIR, so the VM and native code run the same drop logic. The class's `destructor` first, then its fields in reverse declaration order, then the base class's cleanup, since a derived class cleans up before its base, as in C++; enum switch on tag; array elements by decreasing index then the buffer; `Box` inner then block, through the method table when the box can hold a subclass or an interface; `string` frees only if `cap > 0` (SPEC 5.7 D2). Before a base's cleanup runs, the glue writes the base's table into the object's hidden pointer (`SetTable`), so `this.m()` and printing `this` in a base's destructor run the base's code and never an override that reads the subclass's fields, which are already gone: with `public destructor() { console.log(this); }` in `Animal`, dropping a `Dog` prints `Animal { name: 'Rex' }` (`TESTING.md` `S2-02-dtor`). An abstract class is never created, so its own table is in an object only while a base's cleanup runs, and there an abstract method has no body to run: every abstract slot of an abstract class's own table, and of its tables for interfaces, points at a stub that panics, as the C++ ABI points a pure virtual slot at one, and never at nothing. So with an abstract `Shape` whose destructor prints `this.describe()`, which calls the abstract `area`, a block that prints `q.describe()` for a `Sq` of side 2 prints `area 4` and then, as `q` is dropped, panics in `Shape`'s destructor with `` `Shape.area` is abstract, and the subclass that implements it is already cleaned up `` (SPEC 9.1, `TESTING.md` `C4-abstract-dtor`); the VM pauses on it as on any panic. The stub knows its slot's method, but not the dropped object's class, since the object's hidden pointer names `Shape`'s table by then. Construction runs the other way (section 5): a subclass's fields are set before its base part is built, and the object holds its own class's table from the start, so `this.m()` and printing `this` in a base's constructor run the object's own class's code, as everywhere else, and read only fields already set (`S2-02-ctor`). Freeing the block of a `Box<A>` whose `A` can be extended uses the size and alignment that the object's method table records (section 7.7), never `sizeOf<A>()`, because the box may hold a larger subclass, and the `Box`'s glue reads them before it runs the contents' cleanup, so the table writes of that cleanup never change the size passed to `dealloc` (`S2-02-box`); a `Box<I>` reads them from its table word the same way. A whole-object copy or move through such a box, through such an `&A` or of `this` in such a class (`unbox`, `*a = v`, a copy out by C05 or C08 or by an explicit `*r` or `*b`, the entry to a `move` method of `A`, which takes the whole object as `this`, `mem.replace`, `mem.swap`, `clone`) moves exactly `sizeOf<A>()` bytes, so the `CheckClass` before it (section 5) compares the object's method table with `A`'s and panics when they differ: `` `unbox` expected an `Animal`, but the box holds a `Dog` ``. A move never slices a subclass, and an `A` is never written into a subclass's storage. A call through the box, a field read and a drop need no check, because each goes through the object's own table or reads only `A`'s part. A `move` method disposes of the fields of `this` it does not move out with its own class's glue, which would skip a subclass's destructor and own fields, so the check on entry to a `move` method of an extendable class makes `this` exactly that class, and nothing is leaked (SPEC 4.3, D4).

`DisplayGlue` is the synthesised `fmt` of an enum, and of a class with no written `fmt`, of its own or in any base, which is how every class satisfies `Display` (SPEC 4.4); mono makes one per type on demand. It writes the class's own name, then each field in declaration order, a base class's fields first and private fields included, and a field that a subclass overrides once, in its base's place, each as a nested value (section 5's `console.log` bullet), so a reference field prints what it points to, a string field in single quotes and an `fn` field the address of its code in hex: `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }`, and with a `tag: string` field holding `x, flags: 0` in place of `flags`, `Header { len: 3, tag: 'x, flags: 0' }` (`TESTING.md` `EP-09`). A field of a `@packed` class is copied to a temporary first, as `console.log` copies one (section 5), since a reference to it would be EN0804, so `GdtPointer { limit: 23, base: 4096 }` prints with no misaligned access. For an enum it writes the case's text, or else its qualified name, followed by its fields in the same form if it has any, `DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 }`; only `Result`, which `core` prints, names its cases bare, `Ok { value: 5 }` (section 5's `console.log` bullet). A field of type `void` is not printed, in a class or in an enum case, so `Ok()` of a `Result<void>` prints `Ok`. An enum that writes `fmt` gets no `DisplayGlue`, and its case texts play no part in how it prints: it prints through its `fmt` at the top level and nested alike, unquoted, as a class with a written `fmt` does, and that print is its text as an error (section 5). It streams through the `Formatter` it is given and never allocates. A written `fmt` is inherited like any method, so a `Dog` whose base `Animal` writes one prints through `Animal.fmt` unless `Dog` writes its own, and `rex.fmt(f)` called by name runs what `console.log(rex)` runs. A synthesised printer is not a declared member, so a class's first written `fmt`, with none in its bases, takes no `override`, and `override` there is EN0443 (`TESTING.md` `S2-29-fmt`). A class that can be extended keeps its `fmt`, written, inherited or synthesised, in its method table like any method, so printing through an `&Animal` that holds a `Dog` prints the `Dog` by the `Dog`'s own rule. A class's table for an interface holds its `fmt` too, and beside it the type's `fmtNested` (section 7.7), so a `Box<I>` or a two-word `&I` prints its object, a `final` class with no hidden pointer included, at the top level and nested exactly as the object would print there itself. Every other type prints through an implementation in `core`, or, for `T[]` and `Box<T>`, which `alloc` declares, in `alloc` (section 5's `console.log` bullet), so every type meets `Display`, a type parameter with no bound written included, and an instance prints its `T` by that type's own rule.

`StringForm` exists because the form of a `&string` follows its permission and the result's writability (section 7.7), while a function may be reached by a row that allows more: an `fn` row or closure parameter that writes the parameter (SPEC 4.5), a base method that writes it, by its body or a `let` mark, or an interface member marked `let` on it. The shim has the row's forms. For each `&string` or `&string | null` parameter that the function only reads and the row writes, it loads `ptr` and `len` from the whole descriptor it is given, keeping a `null` as it is; for a writable `&string` result, or a writable `&string` part of a tuple result, where the row's is read-only, it loads them from the descriptor the function returns, rebuilding the tuple; then it calls the function. `FnToPtr` and `FnAsClosure` point at it when the forms differ, and so does an implementation's slot in the table a `Box<I>` or a dynamic copy uses. So `run(show, &msg)` with `function run(f: fn(let s: &string) => void, s: &string): void` and a `show` that only reads gets the string `msg` holds (`TESTING.md` `MR-1`). A method-table slot has the `&string` forms of the declaration that introduces it: an override whose forms differ from those of the slot it overrides introduces a slot of its own in its class's table, with its own forms, and the inherited slot points at the shim. A `Virtual` call through a `&C` or `Box<C>` loads the slot of the nearest declaration of the method at or above `C`, so with an `Animal.label` whose read-only result is a pointer and a length, and an override in a `Dog` that isn't `final` returning the whole `&this.name`, a call through a `&Dog` gets the descriptor it may grow and a call through a `&Animal` gets two words (`TESTING.md` `S2-05-dog`). A per-class copy or generic instance that calls an interface or bound member through a class's table converts, at the call, between the member's forms and that slot's. An `fn` value is one code pointer (SPEC 11), with no room to name the function an adapter should call, so a conversion into an `fn` type that changes a `&string` form needs a static target, a function name or a non-capturing arrow, which `FnToPtr` points at the shim; from any other `fn` value it is EN0615 in phase 3, `` `choose(false)` may hold a function that takes `s` as a part, so it can't become a `fn(let s: &string) => void` ``, with the help to give the value's declaration the wider row, so `run(choose(false), &msg)` with a `choose(loud: bool): fn(s: &string) => void` is rejected. Into a closure parameter, whose value is a code and an env word, the conversion builds a `StringFormEnv` adapter instead: a closure on the caller's stack whose env is the original `fn` or closure value, which converts the forms as `StringForm` does and calls through it, so a closure parameter forwarded by C14 to one whose row writes `s` works. A row nested below the top must equal the function's (SPEC 4.5), so no other conversion needs a shim, and arrays need none, since `&T[]` is always one pointer and `&Slice<T>` always two words.

**Reachability phase** runs on `ReachTree` after mono: EN0812, EN0809, EN0823, and on VM targets EN1201-EN1203 and EN1205; EN1204 is no phase-5 error but a VM fault at the first read or write through an integer-derived pointer with no provenance, since making a pointer from an integer never faults (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 2.3). EN0820 fires, with no `@globalAllocator`, when a reached instance has a local, field, parameter or return of owned `string`, `T[]` or `Box<T>`, a template in an allocating position, or a call into `alloc`. It names the first use in BFS order, and `&string` stays legal because it is `core`. A `Failure` that `Err` builds from a fixed text holds a static descriptor (`cap == 0`) and does not count; a template in `Err` does (SPEC 6), and so does a `&string` that `Err` copies (`ErrForm::TextCopy`), as `Err(why)` with `why: &string` does, while a `string` that `Err` moves in (`TextMove`) adds nothing of its own, since the owned `string` it moves already counts where it is held. So does a conversion into `Failure`, by `?` into `Result<T>` or by `Err(x)` at `Result<T>`, whose text is built at run time, from an enum with a case that has fields, from an enum that writes `fmt`, or from an `implements Error` class (section 5): its call to the `alloc` lang item `failure_from_display` is EN0820 at the `?` or the `Err`, `` turning `DiskError.BadSector` into a text needs memory, and this kernel has no heap ``, with the label `` `BadSector` has fields, so its text is built at run time ``, or `` `AgeError` writes `fmt`, so its text is built at run time `` for every case of such an enum, and `` help: return `Result<u8, DiskError>` to pass the error on as it is ``. A conversion of an enum with no written `fmt` that can meet only cases with no fields, or with a text, copies a static descriptor (`cap == 0`) and does not count: a case written in place, as in `Err(DiskError.Timeout)`, or an enum none of whose cases has fields, as for `?` on a `Result<u8, BusError>`, compiles there.
