  - `CheckBounds` is never elided, even though the head proves it, and the bind is unconditional in `Built` MIR, including for an unused binding, so the `Ref` still reads `S` for init dataflow. A move of the source inside the loop is therefore caught twice over, by the hold and by this read, and section 6.1 says which one reports it.
  - `Ref`, `Len` and the head's and bind's `CheckTag` runs carry the source expression's span, so EN0608, EN0609, EN0615, EN0616 and EN0804 point at the source.
  - A `Once` source in `Owned` mode of type `T[]` converts into the `alloc` lang class `ArrayIntoIter<T>` (`HiddenLoop(IntoIter)`), whose destructor frees the remainder on `break`, `return` or `?`. Of type `T[N]` it uses the static length, needs Copy `T` and copies each element, otherwise EN1015 (SPEC 5.6). Ranges use a plain counter (`RangeCursor`, `RangeEnd`).
- **Array spread.** `ArrayLit` stays for plain literals, through `AllocArray`. `ArrayBuild` lowers in order. `_out` is a `Temp` of type `T[]`, built empty, with `Drop` scheduled in the statement scope, so a `?` in a later part drops what was built. Each part, left to right:
  - `Item`: evaluate the value, take `_r = &_out [auto-borrow]`, call `push`.
  - `Spread(_, _)`: evaluate `_s` from the operand's adjust (`&src [auto-borrow]`, `&*r [reborrow]`, `&tmp [temp-arg]` for a statement temporary, or a reference rvalue as it is), which ends in a `Convert(ArrayToSlice)` for a `T[]` and a `Convert(FixedToSlice)` for a `T[N]` (reference conversions, above), take `_r = &_out [auto-borrow]`, and call `extendCloned`, which takes `o: &Slice<T>` (SPEC 8.3). The view conversion belongs to that generated call, never to the operand's type, so a spread operand is still no coercion site (SPEC 4.13), and a `T[N]` needs neither a loop of its own nor a name in `alloc`.

  Then `_out` moves to the destination. `_out` holds no reference and these calls return `void`, so O9's clause for `V` values ends the source's loan when the copy is made. The `Ref` on the source is what makes O11 run: `const r = &f; const c = [...f]; r[0] = 1;` is EN0708, because `r` writes later and so holds an exclusive loan, and `[...p.arr]` on a packed `u32[4]` is EN0804.
- **Constant literal views (SPEC 4.7 rule 7).** A bracket literal whose elements are all constants, number, `bool` and string literals, top-level `const`s, enum cases with no fields and nested literals of these, written behind `&` where a `&Slice<T>` or a `&T[N]` is expected, lowers as any literal does into a statement temporary `_t`, with `_r = &_t [literal-view]`. Phase 3 decides it with the borrow's mode (N4): when the slot it fills is not write-live, so nothing writes through the view or passes it to a Write parameter, the site makes no loan, as `&CONST` makes none, and marks the slot as a top-level `const` does (section 6.1), and drop elaboration, which already reads borrowck's results (section 2.6), turns each site of `BorrowFacts::static_views` (section 3), the `[literal-view]` sites the check gave no loan, into `_r = const static_addr(L)`, a read-only static in `.rodata` (section 7.7), and removes `_t` with its construction and its `Drop`. The check hands the set over because elaboration cannot recompute it: what writes through the view may be an arrow that captures it, a body of its own that the elaboration of this one never sees, so `const p: &Slice<i64> = &[1, 2]; run(() => { p[0] = 9; });` keeps the temporary and is EN0712 at the literal, as with `p[0] = 1;` below. So `const primes: &Slice<i64> = &[2, 3, 5, 7];` and `return &[0, 0];` from a `&Slice<i64>` function compile with no heap, and the second's result is read-only, since it points at a constant (section 6.7). A literal that is written through stays the temporary, with an exclusive loan, since a temporary's life is never extended (SPEC 5.4): `const p: &Slice<i64> = &[2, 3, 5, 7]; p[0] = 1;` is EN0712 at the literal, while `fill(&[1, 2, 3], 7)` writes the temporary and drops it. So does a literal with an element that isn't a constant: returning `&[n, n]` is EN0710 (`TESTING.md` `SL-07-literal`, `Q41-written`).
- **Closures.** Capture lowering is by the kind of the captured local. For an owned path: one `env_i = &path [closure-capture]`. Through a reference local `r`: `env_i = &*r [closure-capture]`, which phase 3 decides by N4 like any copy, so a capture whose arrow writes through it takes an exclusive loan on `*r`, held by the env. The env also holds a shared loan on the local `r` itself, which has no storage, so repointing `r` while the closure value is live is EN0715 and the arrow never runs with a target the code has since replaced (SPEC 5.5). A closure parameter `f` that is captured: `env_i = &f`, always exclusive (section 6.4). Then `env = Aggregate(ClosureEnv, ...)` and `c = ClosureValue { func, env }`, passed as an argument or stored in a local. The arrow body is its own `Body` taking `_env`; captured paths are `*(_env.i)`. Named functions passed as closures get a `FnAsClosure` shim. So `func` is a `ClosureFn` (section 2.5): `Arrow(k)` for an arrow, `closure(arrow#k, _e)` in the text form; `Named(f)` for a named function, `closure(fn f, _e)`, whose env is empty, except that a local function's env holds its hidden arguments as captures, `_e = env(&v [closure-capture], ..)`, with `&*v` for a reference variable and `copy v` for a nullable one, which the shim passes on as the hidden arguments, and phase 3 decides these captures as it decides an arrow's: when the function's contract makes any hidden argument Write, the closure value counts as writing its env (N3), as an arrow's does when its body writes through its env, so the captures that fill it are exclusive; and `FnValue` for an `fn` value `f` passed where a closure parameter is expected (C12), `_e = env(copy f); c = closure(fn_value, _e)`, whose env holds the value as its one field, which the closure calls with its own arguments.
- **Forwarding a closure parameter.** Passing `f` as an argument is `tmp = ClosureReborrow(f)`, with a new exclusive loan on `f`.
- **Nullable closure parameters.** `handler?: (k: u8) => void` holds a tag beside the code and env words. A call, legal only where narrowing proves it non-null, lowers to the `CheckTag` run, `_f = ClosureReborrow((handler as Present).0)` and a call through `_f`, so it conflicts exactly as a call of a non-null closure parameter does. Passing `handler` on to another nullable closure parameter is a `ClosureReborrow` of the whole value.
- **Local functions (N13).** A function among a program's top-level statements takes one hidden argument per top-level variable it uses, directly or through the local functions it calls (section 4), evaluated at each call: `&v [hidden-arg]` for an owned variable, a copy for a reference variable. So `inc()` lowers as `inc(&count)` would, and its calls conflict with live loans as explicit arguments do.
- **Top-level statements.** A program's statements build one body, `BodyOwner::Statements`, which is its entry (section 7.6).
- **Templates.** Passed directly to `console.log`, `console.error`, `writeTo`, or `panic`: `Display::fmt` calls on a stream `Formatter`, no allocation; for `panic`, the stream is the static panic buffer. Its parts become operands of the form beside the other arguments' operands, and the callee `Callee::FormParts { form, parts }` records how many operands each written argument gave, `form log[3, 1](...)` in the text form, so one `fmt` runs per argument with a space between (below) and a template's parts run with none. Elsewhere: an `alloc` buffer `Formatter`, yielding a `string`. `x.toString()` is a compiler form (`Callee::Form`, SPEC 6), lowered as the template `` `${x}` `` in an allocating position, so it accepts any value, a borrowing one included, as `console.log` does (`TESTING.md` `EP-07`).
- **`console.log`.** `hir::Callee::Form(ConsoleLog)` lowers to `console.begin(fd)`, one `fmt` per argument with a space between, and `console.end()`. Arguments are implicit shared borrows, and a packed field is copied to a temporary first. The stack buffer flushes at the end, so one call is one `write`. An anonymous object prints its fields in byte order of their names, never in layout order, which differs between `i386` and `x86_64` (section 7.7), so `{ name: "Ada", age: 36 }` prints `{ age: 36, name: 'Ada' }` on every target. Every value can be printed, and no type fails a `Display` bound, so `console.log`, `${}`, `toString` and error text accept any value (SPEC 4.4): a class with no written `fmt`, of its own or inherited, prints through its `DisplayGlue` (section 2.6), `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }`, its fields in declaration order, a base class's first and private ones included, and an error case with fields prints as `DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 }`. An array or a `&Slice<T>` prints as `[1, 2, 3]`, a tuple as `[1, 5]`, a string on its own without quotes, `null` as `null`, a reference or a `Box<T>` as what it points to, a raw pointer as its address in hex, and an `fn` value or a closure as the address of its code in hex, each address written `0x` and lowercase digits with no padding, so `0xB8000 as *u16` prints `0xb8000`; on the VM such an address is a VM address, which no native run shares (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 2.3). A string inside another value prints in single quotes: `DisplayGlue` and `core`'s printers of arrays, views, tuples, anonymous objects, enum case fields and `Result` print each part through the `core/fmt` lang function `fmtNested<T>`, which mono resolves per type. For a `string`, a `&string`, a string-literal-union value, a `Failure` and a case that carries a text, of an enum with no written `fmt`, it writes the text between `'` and `'`, escaping `\` as `\\`, `'` as `\'` and control characters as `\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\0` or `\xNN`; a `T | null`, a `&T` and a `Box<T>` pass it on to what they hold, and a `Box<I>` or a two-word `&I`, whose class is not known, to the `fmtNested` slot of the table it carries (section 7.7), so a `Box<Labeled>[]` holding `Mood.Happy` and a `Robot` prints `['happy', Robot { id: 7 }]` and a `[&Labeled, i64]` prints `['happy', 1]` (`TESTING.md` `V-ME-08-erased`); every other type runs its own `fmt`, whose parts are nested in turn, and so does an enum that writes `fmt`, which prints what its `fmt` writes, unquoted, nested or not (`V-ME-07-fmt`). A field of type `void` is not printed, in an anonymous object, a class or an enum case. `Result`'s printer writes its cases bare, as code writes them, in the enum form, so `Ok(5)` prints `Ok { value: 5 }`, `Err("boom")` prints `Err { error: 'boom' }`, `[Ok("x")]` prints `[Ok { value: 'x' }]`, and `Ok()` of a `Result<void>`, whose one field is `void`, prints `Ok`, while every other enum keeps its qualified name, as `Shape.Circle { r: 1 }` does (`TESTING.md` `V-ME-11-result`). So `["a, b", "c"]` prints `['a, b', 'c']`, a `Header` holding `tag: "x, flags: 0"` prints `Header { len: 3, tag: 'x, flags: 0' }`, and `PacketError.Short { got: ["a, b", "c"] }` prints `PacketError.Short { got: ['a, b', 'c'] }` (`TESTING.md` `EP-09`). A value printed on its own is never quoted: an argument of `console.log`, so `console.log("a", s)` prints `a Ada` for `s` holding `Ada`, as does a present `s: string | null`; a template hole; `toString`; and an error's own text at the top level, a `Failure`, a text case, the `error: <e>` of `main` and of a test that returns `Err`, and the text a `toBeErr` compares. A written `fmt` writes exactly what it writes. All of it streams through the `Formatter`, so the printing itself never allocates; only the `string` that a template in an allocating position or `toString` builds does.
- **Intrinsics and host calls.** `Callee::Intrinsic` and `Callee::HostCall` lower directly to the `Intrinsic` and `HostCall` statements; library `@intrinsic` and `@hostCall` declarations are the only way to name them.
- **Test forms.** `expect(a).toBe(b)` lowers to `&a`, `&b`, a call to `EqGlue<T>`, and a cold call to `__fail`, copying a packed field to a temporary first. These are real shared borrows for one statement. A failing matcher's values reach the runner as `ShowGlue` records, and its diff quotes a string, and every text value SPEC 4.4 quotes inside another, by that nested quoting rule at every level, a top-level string included, since no value in a diff is printed on its own: `toBe("hi Ada")` against `"hi Ada "` shows `'hi Ada'` and `'hi Ada '`, so the trailing space can be seen (`TESTING.md` `V-ME-10-diff`). A value whose type writes `fmt`, of its own or inherited, is shown by that rule too, through its own `fmt`, unquoted, which no structural record can rebuild: its `ShowGlue` record is a `T` record carrying the text that `fmt` writes, which the test binary streams through the `Formatter` itself once the matcher has failed (`TESTING.md` section 5). A test whose body returns `Err` prints the error itself, `error: <e>` through `Display`, exactly as `__rt_start` prints one from `main` (section 7.6), and sends that text to the runner as T0007's failure text, in its `Fail` frame, so a test returning `Err("disk, full")` shows `error: disk, full`, and one returning an `AgeError` that writes `fmt` shows what that `fmt` writes (`TESTING.md` `V-ME-10-err`). Test binaries, freestanding ones included, therefore carry the formatter code, which costs size and no allocation, since the `Formatter` streams. `TESTING.md` section 2 covers the rest.

## 6. Borrow checking

Runs per `Body` in the `Built` phase, generically, once the contracts of the body's callees exist (section 6.7). The checker is exactly SPEC 5 with its inference rules N1-N14; this section makes it implementable. It is deliberately not rustc's NLL: there are no region variables and no constraint graph.

### 6.1 Moves, init, read-only marks

**Move paths.** A tree over `local(.field | as variant .field)*`, with a node per path that is moved, assigned or dropped. `Box` deref and `[*]` are not move-path steps: an `Operand::Move` whose place crosses a reference deref, raw deref, `*box` or an index reports EN0603, EN0605 or EN0604.

**Dataflow.** Forward `MaybeInit` and `MaybeUninit` over move paths, union at joins.

| State at a use | Report |
|---|---|
| Path or any prefix or child not definitely init, last moved | EN0601, or EN0611 when the move reaches itself around a back edge, or reaches a `for...of` head or bind around one |
| Never assigned, and its type has no default: a class, an enum, `Box`, `Result` or a reference | EN0602 |
| Any assignment to a `const`, or to a by-value parameter not marked `let` | EN0613 |
| A `Move` that mirbuild records as an assignment's value: the target of an assignment or compound assignment to a place that is not Copy, used where the value would be kept (section 5) | EN0621, and the place stays initialised |
| Partial move where a prefix type has a destructor, or is a type parameter (an implicit one for an object-type parameter included, a `@devMigrate` hook's `old` among them until M6), whose value may have one (H41) | EN0607 |
| `this` read, a method called or a `return` reached in a constructor before every field without a default is assigned, an inherited one that the `super()` of a base chain with no constructor leaves unset included (section 5), as `` `owner` is not set ``, with `help: assign this.owner after super()`, the closing brace counting as a `return`; before `super(...)`, which is wherever the `super` pseudo-path (below) is not definitely initialised, anything but an assignment to the class's own fields (reading `this` or a base field, calling a method, passing `this`), or `super(...)` reached with an own field that has no default unassigned, with `` help: set `this.owner` before `super(name)`, because `Animal`'s constructor may call `Dog`'s methods `` and a fix-it that moves the assignment above the call (SPEC 14 G24); or `this` in a field initialiser, a subclass's new default included | EN0610 |
| In a constructor of a class with a base, a normal exit, a `return` or the closing brace, where `super` is not definitely initialised, or a `super(...)` where it is not definitely uninitialised; or a `super(...)` inside an arrow or a local function of a constructor, so not a statement of the constructor's own body (a `super` in a class with no base, or a `super(...)` in any other function, is phase 2's EN0219) | EN0619 |
| `x.f = v` where a proper prefix is not definitely init, other than by moves of its own fields | EN0601 if moved, EN0602 if never assigned. A constructor's `this` is exempt |
| A call to a local function, directly or through others, above a top-level variable it reads | EN0618 |

**Constructors.** In a constructor of a class with a base, the dataflow tracks one more move path, the pseudo-path `super`, uninitialised at entry and initialised by the `super(...)` call, so the order of SPEC 4.3 is one more init fact and needs no pass of its own. `super` must be definitely initialised at every normal exit, a `return` or the closing brace: otherwise EN0619, `` `super(name)` isn't called on every path before the constructor returns ``, labelled at that exit, with a note naming the branch that skips it, `` when `quiet` is true ``, so `if (quick) { return; } super(name);` is EN0619 at the `return` and `if (!quiet) { super(name); }` at the closing brace. A `super(...)` where `super` may already be initialised runs it twice, EN0619, `` `super(age)` may already have run here ``, as `super(age); super(age);` does with `age: i64`. A path that ends in a call of type `never`, as `panic(...)` is, reaches no exit and needs nothing, so `if (bad) { panic("no"); } super(name);` compiles, as does `if (quiet) { super("anon"); } else { super(name); }`. Loops need no rule of their own: in `while (i < n) { super(); i++; }` the back edge makes the call's second run a maybe-run one and the zero-turn path leaves `super` uninitialised, which gives both EN0619s. Phase 3 also reports EN0619 for a `super(...)` inside an arrow or a local function of a constructor, which is not a statement of the constructor's own body, such as `const f = () => super(name);`. A `super` in a class with no base, and a `super(...)` in any function that is not a constructor, are EN0219, reported by phase 2, which meets the mistake first, so phase 3 never sees them (SPEC 4.3, 13.2). Reading `this` where `super` may not have run stays EN0610, by the EN0610 row's case for what comes before `super(...)`, so `if (!quiet) { super(name); } console.log(this);` is EN0610 at `this`. A `FieldInit` (section 5) initialises its field on both edges of the `Switch` that may skip it, since in an instance that skips it a class below stored the field before its `super(...)`, and a field with no default of its own that a class below may override starts maybe-initialised, with its drop flag set from `Overridden(f)` (section 2.6): a read of `this` treats it as not definitely initialised, so its class's constructor must still assign it on every path, and the D3 drop of that assignment is guarded by the flag. A `super()` that calls the implicit constructor of a base chain with no constructor initialises the inherited fields it gives a value, and every inherited field with no default stays as it was, uninitialised, or maybe-initialised with its flag where a class below may override it, so the subclass's constructor assigns it after `super()` by the same rules: the EN0610 row covers the reads, calls and exits before that assignment, and its case for what comes before `super(...)` covers an assignment to it there, so `this.owner = o; super();` is EN0610 at `this.owner` (section 5, `TESTING.md` `C4-ctorless-base`).

To explain a move, `move_sites(path, point)` walks the CFG backward to collect every move reaching the point; the first in source order is labelled, and a second is mentioned when arms differ.

**One report per set of move sites,** as rustc does. `for (const x of &xs) { console.log(q); take(q); }` moves `q` in every turn, and in the next turn both `console.log(q)` and `take(q)` read it: one EN0611, primary the move, labelled `moved here, in an earlier turn`, secondary the first such read. `take(q); break;` and `take(q); q = [];` are not errors. When the moved path is the loop's own source, as in `for (const x of &q) { take(q); }`, the move also conflicts with the loop's hold (section 6.2), and only that EN0709 is reported, since it names the cause.

**Read-only marks** (SPEC 4.11, N5) are a forward may-analysis over the same flows as `held`. A slot is marked if it may hold a reference from: a `const` local, or anything it owns through a field, element or `Box`; a by-value parameter not marked `let`; a top-level `const`, a string literal or a constant literal view (section 5); a `readonly` field, even in a constructor; a reference read from a field declared `readonly &`, or from a tuple part declared `readonly` (`FieldDef.readonly_refs`), in any body of its class, nested arrows included (section 6.7); a reference to or a copy of a tuple field, or of a part of one at any depth, whose place contains, at any depth, a tuple part declared `readonly` (a path of `FieldDef.readonly_refs` under it), a nullable payload included, as `&this.t` does for `private t: [readonly &i64[], i32]`, and as `&this.t` and `&this.t[1]` both do for `private t: [&i64[], [readonly &i64[], i32]]`, while a reference to a sub-tuple that contains none is not marked; it carries the mark for the whole place, so writing any part through it or through a result reached from it, the `i32` included, is EN0616 naming the field, as `p.tup()[1] = 5;` is with a `tup()` that returns `&this.t`, while the class's own `this.t[1] = 5;` writes the field through `this` and takes no such reference (`TESTING.md` `FV-05-pad`); a raw-derived `&*p` or a view `SliceFromRaw` makes, which a body containing `unsafe` may still return in a writable result, since that result follows the signature-default sources (SPEC 5.4), but only when there are some: under rule 3, with no reference parameters and so no sources, a returned `&*p` keeps its mark, in an `unsafe function` as in a safe one, as a reference to a static does, so writing through `cell(0)` of `public function cell(i: isize): &u16 { unsafe { const p = &cells[0] as *u16; return &*p.add(i); } }` is EN0616, with a help to take the storage as a `&` parameter, while the view that `Slice<T>.fromRaw(p, n)` returns carries none in the slot where it is made and used, since its contract comes from the compiler's table, not from a body, and its `unsafe` caller takes on SPEC 5.9's obligations for it, so it is as writable as any `&Slice<T>` there, and so is a result that returns it under some source, as the library's `slice` and `splitAt` do under `this`; but at a `Return` of a function with no reference parameters it is marked as a returned `&*p` is there, under rule 3, in an `unsafe function` as in a safe one, so writing through `cells4()` of `public function cells4(): &Slice<u16> { unsafe { return Slice<u16>.fromRaw(&cells[0] as *u16, 4); } }` is EN0616 with the same help, while a `Screen` holding the base pointer, whose `pixels()` returns the view with `this` as its source, hands out a writable one (`TESTING.md` `S2-30-view`, section 10); a call result whose callee gives a read-only result, by `readonly` before its return type or for a reason of section 6.7, or whose source argument is marked; any result that may point at a constant or static; a string part (`slice` on a string). A result derived from `this` carries no mark of its own: as one derived from any other parameter, it is as changeable as its source (SPEC 5.4). Repointing replaces the mark, and joins take the union. Writing through a marked slot, or passing it where N3 finds a write (a Write parameter, a source parameter whose result is write-live, a store into a borrowing value whose class writes through its reference fields, rule (e), or the source of a `for...of` loop whose binding is write-live, below), reports the mark's origin once: EN0608 for a `const` root or a top-level `const`, EN0609 for `readonly`, EN0616 for a read-only result, a string part, a string literal, a `readonly &` field, a tuple part declared `readonly` or a tuple field with such a part, which it names, EN0614 for the receiver of a Write `this`, whatever the mark's origin, with a help that names the origin. Writing through a result that is read-only by `readonly` names it, `` help: to allow this, remove `readonly` from the return type of `count` ``, and so does a changing method called on one: with `public view(): readonly &i64[]`, `s.view().push(99);` is EN0614 with `` help: to allow this, remove `readonly` from the return type of `view` ``. Growing a returned `&string` that may be a part, as `*longest(&s, &t) += "!";` does, is EN0616, `` `longest` returns a string that may be a part, so it can't grow ``, with `` help: change `s` directly: `s += "!";` ``. A `const` receiver of an accessor whose result is written, as in `lib.book(1).title = "Dune";` on a `const lib` with `public book(id: i64): &Book`, is one EN0608 at the call, `` `book` hands out a writable reference, but `lib` is `const` ``, never also EN0614. A store into a part of a tuple local, through tuple parts and nullable payloads alone, joins the stored source's marks into the tuple's, as the literal `[&k, 0]` would, so `t[0] = &k; t[0].push(1);` over a `const k` is EN0614 at the write; a store into a reference part of a tuple reached through a reference, `p[0] = &k` with `const p = &t`, or of a tuple field of a class that writes through its reference fields, a part declared `readonly` excepted, names no local whose marks could follow, so its source is checked at the store, as rule (e) checks one stored into a reference field. At a call, each permission is the argument's of A:311's layout, `this` first, then each `&` parameter, then each reference part of a by-value borrowing tuple parameter, whose marks are those of the operand that built that part when the tuple is a literal there, else the whole tuple's; and a class passed for an object type or a fields-only interface to a parameter the callee may write, behind `&`, nullable or not, with a Write permission or by value marked `let`, a parameter of an `fn` value or a closure included, is EN0609 at the argument when the type names a field the class declares `readonly`, and a call result whose sources include such an argument carries that field's `readonly` mark, as a result of a marked source argument carries its mark (SPEC 4.11, H42). Marks do not follow a `store` into a borrowing value (section 6.2): where the class writes through its reference fields, rule (e) already rejects a marked source at the store, and where it does not, or where the field stored into is declared `readonly &`, which rule (e) skips (section 6.7), nothing writes through the stored reference, and one read back from a `readonly &` field carries that field's mark. So a `Lexer` built over a `const` source still advances, and `lx.next()` is no EN0614. Statement temporaries are not marked, so `make().push(1)` compiles. A `for...of` binding carries its source's mark: the bind's `Ref` of the source's element marks the binding as any `Ref` of that place is marked, so it is marked over a `const` root or anything it owns, a top-level `const`, a `readonly` field, a field declared `readonly &`, a tuple part declared `readonly`, a read-only, constant-pointing or raw-derived result, or a `_src` that holds any of these, and not over `&q` for a `let q`, so such a loop still writes the elements of `q`. The mark records that it came through loop l's bind, in every slot it is copied into. A loop whose binding is write-live anywhere in its body writes through its source, which makes its hold exclusive, and at the pre-header its source slot, the hold's path for a `Path` source and `_src` for a `Once` source, is checked as an argument to a Write parameter is, so a mark is reported once, at the source (SPEC 5.6, O8): `for (const t of &TABLE) { *t = 0; }` over a top-level `const` is EN0608, a loop over `pick(&v)` whose `pick` may return a top-level constant, or over a read-only, constant-pointing or raw-derived result, is EN0616, and one over a path through a `readonly` field is EN0609. A write through a slot whose mark came through loop l's bind, or such a slot passed where N3 finds a write, is that pre-header's report and is never reported again: `let keep: &i64 | null = null; for (const x of &K) { keep = x; } if (keep != null) { *keep = 0; }` over a `const K` is one EN0608, at the source, and none at `*keep = 0`. Every other reading of the mark sees it as any mark, stage 4's "a returned value may carry a read-only mark" and "a call result whose source argument is marked" included, so a `find` that returns a binding of a loop over a `readonly &` field, or a `firstOf()` that returns a binding of a loop over `zeros()`, which may return a constant, gives a read-only result (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-finder`, `RO-loop-const`). A `Convert` (section 2.5) carries its operand's mark and bind mark, as `Use(Copy|Move t)` does, so a view of a `const` array, or an upcast of a read-only result, stays marked.

### 6.2 Loans

```rust
pub struct LoanPath { pub root: Local, pub through_ref: bool, pub steps: Vec<Step> }
pub enum Step { Field(FieldIdx), Variant(VariantIdx), Elem /* [*] */, BoxDeref, Capture(u32), Fields }
pub struct Loan { pub path: LoanPath, pub exclusive: bool, pub span: Span, pub origin: BorrowOrigin }   // exclusive: N4
pub enum LoanKey { Site(LoanId, Turn), Param(u32), Fields(u32), RFields(u32), Hold(LoopId) }
pub enum Turn { Any, This, EarlierThisRun, EarlierRuns }
// Site(l, t): the loan of borrow site l. A `for...of` bind site has three keys, This, EarlierThisRun and EarlierRuns,
// for its binds of this turn, of earlier turns of this run and of earlier runs (turn keys, below); any other site
// has one, Any. Param(i): a synthetic loan, "the caller's data behind parameter i". Fields(i): the part of it that
// the reference fields of the borrowing value behind parameter i carry. RFields(i): the part of that its fields
// declared `readonly &` carry, the value's read set. Hold(l): loop l's hold on its source.
```

**Modes (N4, N8).** A borrow site (an explicit `&`, a receiver's auto-borrow, a reborrow, a capture) is exclusive iff the slot it fills is write-live right after it (section 6.7), and shared otherwise. An argument's loan is exclusive iff the parameter's permission is Write, or the parameter is among the result's sources and the result is write-live, which only a reference result or a reference part of a tuple result can be, since a borrowing class value is never write-live itself (section 6.7); it is held while the result lives iff the parameter is among the sources and in neither `through_fields` nor `through_read`, where a `through_fields` source gives the result what the value's fields hold instead, and a `through_read` source the value's read set (section 6.3). The loan of each reference part of a by-value borrowing tuple argument follows that part's permission the same way (section 6.7). An exclusive loan stays exclusive until the last use of any slot holding it. Modes are per loan, not per binding, and are fixed before the access checks run. The one loan whose mode changes is a `CompoundTarget` (section 5), a reservation, since SPEC O6 takes a compound target's exclusive borrow last, as it takes a receiver's: from its `Ref` to the store it meets a write or an exclusive borrow of an overlapping path as an exclusive loan does, EN0707, and a move with EN0709, but lets reads and shared borrows pass; the operation's use of `_p` at the store turns it exclusive, which is checked as an exclusive borrow of the place against every other loan live there, and a loan on an overlapping path that the right side left live is EN0708 at that loan's site, as at `s.slice(0, 1)` in `s += s.slice(0, 1)`.

Because references exist only in frame slots and in the reference fields of borrowing values, whose loans the value's slot carries as one set, with the read set of its `readonly &` fields kept apart inside it (below), a path crosses at most one reference after normalisation, which is why `LoanPath` is flat. Three in-scope shapes need the normal form:

- **Narrowed reference.** `(x as Present).0` followed by a deref normalises to `through_ref` with root `x`, and both steps drop: `(*(x as Present).0).f` with `x: &Node | null` becomes root `x`, `through_ref`, `.f`.
- **Inside an arrow.** The root is `_env`, `through_ref` is true, and the steps begin with `Step::Capture(i)`, counted as the one crossing: `*((*_env).i).f` becomes root `_env`, `Capture(i)`, `.f`.
- **Through a borrowing value.** A reference read out of a reference field or a reference part of a borrowing value counts as the one crossing, and the steps that name that field or part stay, after `through_ref`, while what the loan holds is the one set that covers every field. For a local `b`, `(*b.src).f` becomes root `b`, `through_ref`, `.src`, `.f`, and it holds `held(b)`; `(*p.0).f` for a tuple local `p` becomes root `p`, `through_ref`, `.0`, `.f`. For the value behind a reference slot `t`, such as `this` in a `Lexer` method, `(*(*t).src).f` becomes root `t`, `through_ref`, `Step::Fields`, `.src`, `.f`, and it holds `fields(t)` and `site(t)` (below), not `held(t)`. A second crossing below the first truncates the steps to the first one's. Through a field declared `readonly &` of `b` or `*t` itself it holds only `rfields(b)` or `rfields(t)`, the value's read set (below); through one of a borrowing value nested in a field or tuple part of `b` or `*t` it holds `held(b)`, or `fields(t)` and `site(t)`, since that value's read set lies inside the outer value's main set. Either way it holds the field's loans and no key that stands for the borrowing value. Through `t`, other than through a field declared `readonly &`, it also keeps `site(t)`, `t`'s own loans on the value, as SPEC O9 gives every borrow through a reference the set of that reference: they are none for `this` and for a parameter that was never repointed, where only the key `Param(i)` stands for the value and every access to it goes through `t`, but with `let t: [&u8[], i32] = [&buf, 1]; const r = &t; const e = &r[0][0];`, `t[0].push(9)` while `e` is used is EN0706, as it is with `const e = &t[0][0];`, where a token holding only what `t`'s fields hold let the push reallocate `buf` under `e` once `r` was dead (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-through-ref`). A read-set token needs no such loan, since nothing writes through a read-set loan and the read set names no value. Across a call, the `through_fields` rule below is what lets several tokens of one `Lexer` be kept while `lx.next()` runs again (SPEC 5.1), and only for a class that never writes through its reference fields (section 6.7), and the `through_read` rule what lets a token read from a `readonly &` field be kept while the value itself changes, whatever its class writes (section 6.3). Keeping the step keeps loans through two different reference fields or parts apart, as two loans on different fields of an owned value are, which N6's part test and the store rules need: with the step dropped, `TESTING.md` `TK-01-split-wrap` would be EN0707 where its row compiles, and `FV-02-skip`'s `share` would give a second EN0706 beside its row's one. That is sound because two fields or parts that point at the same data already conflict where the aliasing is made: N8 gives each part its own loan, so `let t: [&i64[], &i64[]] = [&v, &v];` with `t[0]` written through while `&t[1][0]` is used is EN0708 at the second `&v`, as `f([&v, &v])` is for an `f` that does the same with its by-value tuple parameter, and EN0707 there when it is done through `&t`; rule (e) of N3 makes each store into a class that writes through its fields exclusive, so `new H(&v, &v)` for such an `H` is EN0707 at the second `&v`, and so is `new H(r, r)` at the second `r`; a copy from one field or part into another is met by the loan that copy takes or the store it makes, so `t[1] = t[0]; const e = &t[1][0]; t[0].push(3);` with `e` used afterwards is EN0706 at the push, and a method that runs `this.b = this.a` stores the value into itself, so the next exclusive borrow of it is EN0707; and a class that never writes through its fields holds only shared loans there, which nothing writes through.

A place crossing a raw pointer deref creates no loan and is never access-checked (SPEC 5.9).

**Slots.** `SlotId` is every local whose type is ref-carrying (`is_slot_type` and not `is_value_type`: a reference, a borrowing class or tuple, or either one nullable, and a reference to a nullable reference or borrowing value, `&(W | null)` as a parameter or `const r = &w` has it, or `&(&T | null)` as a form's `[temp-arg]` or `[auto-borrow]` makes it for an `R | null` argument), plus closure-env and closure-value locals, plus one phantom slot per `for...of` loop over a `Path` source, plus one phantom slot `caller(m)` per parameter `m` whose pointee is a borrowing value, standing for that value in the caller. An Own `this` has none: the object moves into the call and counts as locally owned (SPEC 5.4), so a write into it is a write into a local borrowing value, not a store. All other locals hold nothing, which is O9's clause for `V` values.

**`held`: forward analysis.** Domain `IdVec<SlotId, BitSet<LoanKey>>`, with a bind mark on some keys (below), union at joins, reverse-postorder worklist. Entry: `held(p) = {Param(i)}` for each ref-carrying parameter.

**References to borrowing values.** A second map of the same domain, `fields(s)`, a subset of `held(s)`, is kept for every slot whose pointee is a borrowing value (`this` in a method of a borrowing class, `&B`, `&B | null`, `&(B | null)`): the part of `held(s)` that the pointee's reference fields carry. A third map, `rfields(s)`, a subset of `fields(s)`, is the pointee's read set (SPEC 5.1): the loans that its fields declared `readonly &` carry, which nothing in the value writes through (`store`, below). For a borrowing value `b` itself, `fields(b)` is no set of its own: it denotes `held(b)`, is never stored or assigned apart, and a row that names it for such a b reads `held(b)`, so every store that grows `held(b)` grows it; `rfields(b)` is its read set. A parameter `p` whose pointee is borrowing enters with `held(p) = {Param(i), Fields(i), RFields(i)}`, `fields(p) = {Fields(i), RFields(i)}`, `rfields(p) = {RFields(i)}` and an empty `caller(i)`. For such a slot `t`, `site(t)` denotes the site loans of `held(t)` that `fields(t)` lacks, the loan of the `&x` or the reborrow that made `t` and those on its path, by which `t` keeps the value itself from changing (SPEC O9); it is no set of its own, and it is empty for `this` and for a parameter until it is repointed, whose `held` adds only the key `Param(i)` to its `fields`. A row that takes what the value's fields hold through `t`, other than through a field declared `readonly &` of `*t` itself, takes `site(t)` with it (below). A fourth map, `wfields(b)`, a subset of `held(b)`, is kept for every local, temporary or by-value parameter `b` that is itself a borrowing value, of a borrowing class or tuple type or a nullable one: the loans that its reference fields other than its own `readonly &` ones may hold and write through, a value nested in it included, which only the read-set check of `store` reads (below). A by-value borrowing parameter enters with `held(b) = {Param(i)}` and the other three sets equal to it. `fields`, `rfields` and `wfields` join by union. Every row below that defines d begins with `kill(d)`, which empties all of d's sets, for a borrowing value d its `held`, `rfields` and `wfields`, since its `fields` is its `held`; if the row then sets `held(d)` and not `fields(d)`, it sets `fields(d) = held(d)`, if it does not set `rfields(d)`, it sets `rfields(d) = fields(d)`, and if d is a borrowing value and the row does not set `wfields(d)`, it sets `wfields(d) = held(d)`. From then on these are d's sets, which later stores grow by `∪=` like any other. So a borrowing value obtained by value from a call, or copied or moved out of a field, keeps its whole set as its read set, since no contract says which of a result's loans are read-set loans, and a `through_read` result of it holds everything it holds, which only makes it hold more (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-factory`, `FV-02-make`); for the same reason it keeps its whole set in `wfields`, as a by-value parameter does, so the read-set check counts every loan it holds as one its other fields may write through (`FV-02-alias`, `FV-02-alias-param`). Both maps are upper bounds, and a loan may sit in both: a `through_read` result holds it, and the check counts it as written through. These defaults can hide which loans name the borrowing value itself, the loan of `&lx` or `Param(i)`: a `&Lexer` returned by a call carries them in `fields` too. So `store` below reads all of `held(t)`, and never `held(t)` minus `fields(t)`.

```text
transfer(stmt):
  a `for...of` loop's pre-header, l its bind site, before the hold:      // turn keys, below: a new run
      in every held, fields, rfields and wfields set, Site(l, This) and Site(l, EarlierThisRun) become
      Site(l, EarlierRuns), merging by the union rule with a copy already in the slot (bind marks, below),
      and every key marked via bind l becomes plain               // bind marks, below: a mark never outlives its run
  that loop's bind, before its Ref:                                        // a new turn
      in every held, fields, rfields and wfields set, Site(l, This) becomes Site(l, EarlierThisRun); the Ref's loan L is
      Site(l, This); a renamed key keeps its bind mark and merges by the union rule with a copy already in the slot
  d = Ref(p), loan L, or no new loan where N4 gives none:
      if p crosses a reference field of a borrowing value (a local b, or *t):   // the one crossing, as above
          S = {L} ∪ (rfields(b or t) if that field is declared `readonly &` in b or *t itself, else held(b), or
              fields(t) ∪ site(t));
                                                  // the field itself (`readonly_refs` holds []): a reference part
                                                  // of a tuple field, `readonly` or not, takes held(b), or fields(t)
                                                  // ∪ site(t)
          F = R = S                               // the field's loans and t's own loans on the value: no key of the
                                                  // value, nothing else of held(t), and through a `readonly &` field
                                                  // only the read set; one of a value nested in a field or tuple part
                                                  // of b or *t gets held(b), or fields(t) ∪ site(t)
      else:
          S = {L}; if p crosses *t: S |= held(t), minus every Fields(i) and RFields(i) key when t is `this` or a
              parameter not marked `let`, so always the value behind parameter i, and p's type holds no reference
              (is_value_type): such a place, as `&this.own`, lies in that value's own storage and reaches nothing
              those keys stand for, while one through any other t, `this.lx` copied out included, keeps them all
          F = if p's type is borrowing then (fields(t) if p crosses *t, else held(b) for p's root local b) else S
          R = if p's type is borrowing and p is *t or the local b, or the payload of either narrowed to present,
              then rfields(t) or rfields(b), else F  // a borrowing value nested in a field or tuple part keeps its read
                                                  // set inside the outer value's main set: `&h.rd` gets F, never rfields(h)
          S |= F                                  // `&lx` keeps what `lx` holds, for as long as d lives
      if this Ref is loop l's bind: every key of S but L and the keys of l's own site is marked via bind l   // bind marks
      kill(d); held(d) = S minus { loans rooted at d with through_ref }; fields(d) = F and rfields(d) = R, minus the same
      if L stays in held(d): each key of held(d) that L crosses is covered there   // covered keys, below
  d = Use(Copy t), t a borrowing tuple or a nullable one:  kill(d); held(d) = held(t) ∪ parts(t); fields(d) = held(d)
  d = Use(Copy|Move t), any other t ref-carrying:  kill(d); held(d) = held(t); fields(d) = fields(t)   // a moved bare
                                               rfields(d) = rfields(t); wfields(d) = wfields(t)  // reference is always
                                                                                // a Temp (O7)
                                               for a copy of a nullable reference local t, held(d) also gets the loan
                                               N4 gives on its payload, `*(t as Present).0` normalised to root t with
                                               through_ref, as a reborrow of a reference local does (section 5)
  d = Convert(_, t):                           kill(d); held(d) = held(t); fields(d) = fields(t); rfields(d) = rfields(t)
                                               // C18, C21, C22 (section 2.5): read-only and bind marks carry over, as for Use
  d = Use(Copy b.f) or Use(Copy (*t).f), f a reference field or a reference part, nullable or not, or a borrowing tuple
      in one, at any depth of a borrowing value b or *t (a field of a class value, a part of a tuple):   // section 5
                                               kill(d); held(d) = (rfields(b or t) if f itself is declared `readonly &`
                                               in b or *t, never a tuple part, else held(b), or fields(t) ∪ site(t)) ∪ copies(f);
                                               fields(d) = held(d); rfields(d) = held(d)
                                               each key of held(d) that a loan of copies(f) crosses is covered there
      // copies(f): for each reference that d copies from f, f itself or each reference part of a tuple f, nested
      // parts included, a new loan on what it points at, normalised to the one crossing (root b, through_ref, f's steps,
      // or root t, through_ref, Step::Fields, f's steps; a nullable one on its payload): N4 with the source counted as
      // write-live, so exclusive when that reference of d is write-live just after and shared otherwise, never none;
      // only for a part of a tuple b held directly in a local, a parameter or a temporary, which N3 follows part by
      // part, is it parts(b)'s choice, with none when neither that part of d nor that part of b is write-live
  d = Use(Copy|Move b.f) or Use(Copy (*t).f), f a borrowing class field at any depth of a borrowing value:
                                               kill(d); held(d) = held(b), or fields(t) ∪ site(t); fields(d) = held(d);
                                               rfields(d) = held(d)   // a borrowing f keeps its whole set as its read set
  d = Use(Copy *t), t's pointee borrowing:     kill(d); held(d) = fields(t) ∪ site(t)   // what the fields hold, and t's loans,
                                               held(d) ∪= parts(*t) for a borrowing tuple pointee, nullable or not; fields(d) = held(d);
                                               rfields(d) = rfields(t) for a class pointee
      // parts(p): for each reference part k of the tuple at p, nested parts included, a new loan on what part k points at,
      // `*p.k` normalised (root t, through_ref, Step::Fields, or the local, through_ref, then k's steps), exclusive when
      // part k of d is write-live just after, else shared when part k of p is, else none: O7 per part, since an exclusive loan has no second holder
  d = Use(Move *t), or Use(Move (*t).f) with f a borrowing field, in a `move` method (t is `this`):
                                               kill(d); held(d) = fields(t); rfields(d) = rfields(t) for `*t`
  d = Aggregate(present, [t]) / (t as Present).0:  kill(d); held(d) = held(t); fields(d) = fields(t); rfields(d) = rfields(t)
                                               wfields(d) = wfields(t)
  d = Aggregate(class or tuple, ops), d borrowing:  kill(d); held(d) = union of held(ops)   // one set per borrowing value
                                               rfields(d) = union of held(ops) placed in fields declared `readonly &`
                                               wfields(d) = union of held(ops) placed anywhere else
  a write inside a local borrowing value d, at any depth (d.f = u, d.f.0 = u):
                                               held(d) = held(d) ∪ held(u)    // no kill: the set covers every field
                                               and, for d.f = u with f itself declared `readonly &` in d or in d's payload
                                               narrowed to present, the read-set check of store below, with held(u) for T
                                               and d for b, then rfields(d) ∪= held(u); for any other write, wfields(d) ∪=
                                               held(u), after the read-set check, for the declared class of the nested value,
                                               which a field or part holds exactly, and against all of held(d) in place of
                                               wfields(d), when it writes a field declared `readonly &` of a borrowing value
                                               nested in a field or tuple part of d, as `d[0].src = u` does: such a value
                                               keeps no sets of its own, and its parts all lie in d's other part
  a write inside t's borrowing pointee, at any depth (*t = u, (*t).f = u, (*t).f.0 = u):
                                               store(t, held(u), read, not read)   // no kill, as above; read: f itself
                                               // is declared `readonly &` in (*t).f = u, never for a tuple field or a
                                               // part of one, whatever it says. A whole class value, *t = u, is one
                                               // store whose sets each go to their own: the read-set check of store
                                               // runs once, with rfields(u) for T, on the sets as they stood before
                                               // the write; then held(u) joins every main set that store would grow,
                                               // rfields(u) the read sets beside them, and wfields(u) the wfields of
                                               // each local b reached, so u's read set stays a read set and only what
                                               // u's other fields may write through counts so in b (SPEC O9)
                                               // t an Own `this`: if read, the read-set check's part for a parameter
                                               // first, with t = this: T holding Fields(0) while the class's flag is
                                               // set is EN0616, so `this.src = this.dst.slice(0, 1)` in a `move`
                                               // method is EN0616 and `this.src = this.src.slice(1, n)` passes; an
                                               // Own `this` has no wfields, since what its other fields hold reaches
                                               // T only through Fields(0) or through what the body stored there,
                                               // whose second reborrow of one operand conflicts (EN0708); then
                                               // held(t), fields(t) and, if read, rfields(t) ∪= held(u), or, for a
                                               // whole value, rfields(t) ∪= rfields(u); no store. A loan of held(u)
                                               // taken through `this`, as the slice's receiver loan in either form,
                                               // stays in held(t), so a later `return this` meets it, EN0709 (SPEC
                                               // 5.8 H05, `TESTING.md` `FV-02-move`). A field declared `readonly &` of a
                                               // borrowing value nested in a field or tuple part of *t, (*t)[0].src = u,
                                               // gets the read-set check first, for the declared class of that value,
                                               // which a field or part holds exactly, with fields(t) for the keys and
                                               // held(b) for a local b, in place of Fields(i) and wfields(b), as for a
                                               // local d
  d = Aggregate(ClosureEnv, ops):              kill(d); held(d) = union of held(ops); fields(d) = rfields(d) = held(d)
  d = ClosureValue { env }:                    kill(d); held(d) = held(env); fields(d) = rfields(d) = held(d)
  d = ClosureReborrow(f), new exclusive loan L on f:  kill(d); held(d) = {L} ∪ held(f); fields(d) = rfields(d) = held(d)
  d = a reference read or reborrowed through _env, loan L on that path:  kill(d); held(d) = held(_env) ∪ {L};
                                               fields(d) = rfields(d) = held(d)
  d = anything else:                           kill(d); held(d) = {}
  d = call f(args) (terminator):
      kill(d); held(d) = if ret is ref-carrying then union over i in sources of
                           (rfields(args[i]) if i in through_read, fields(args[i]) if i in through_fields,
                            else held(args[i])) else {}
      fields(d) = held(d); rfields(d) = held(d)   // a by-value borrowing result keeps its whole set as its read set
      for each store (j, k): store(args[k], rfields(args[j]) if the store is read_set_only, fields(args[j]) if it is
                                   fields_only, else held(args[j]), its read bit, wr = false for a read_set_only
                                   store, whose loans nothing writes through, else true unless the store has its
                                   read bit and j is a `&` parameter, `this` included, whose permission is Read
                                   (never an Own `this`, which like a by-value borrowing class parameter has no
                                   permission), or a by-value borrowing tuple parameter whose reference parts are
                                   all Read; a by-value borrowing class parameter, nullable or not, has no
                                   permission and never counts as Read, since `*this = u` may carry what u's own
                                   other fields write through)
      // every store of one call, its read-set check and each of store()'s loops included, tests and reads held, fields,
      // rfields and wfields of every slot as they stood before the call, as the result does, and all the unions are
      // applied after, so neither the order of the stores nor that of the slots changes the result: two arguments of
      // one call are kept apart by their loans, as at any call, the callee checked its own stores against *t, and it
      // applied them one statement at a time, so it records a store that reaches a value through another value it
      // stored: a body that stores s into c and then c into h stores (s, h) as well, while one that stores c into h
      // and then stores into c meets the loan on *c that caller(h) keeps (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-store-snapshot`)
      // at one call the read-set check is reported only where no borrow the call takes, an argument's or the
      // receiver's, conflicted, since a conflicting argument already names that aliasing (one report per mistake)
      // a read_set_only store (j, j), of a parameter into the value behind itself, runs no read-set check: it moves
      // only what that value's own `readonly &` fields reach, which nothing its other fields write through holds
      // (6.3); the store itself still applies, since it may land in a value behind the value's reference fields,
      // and so do the EN0616s of store for a key of an Own this or a by-value borrowing parameter and for a capture
      // sources, through_fields, through_read, stores: the callee's contract for Callee::Fn; for Callee::Virtual the
      // declared one, an interface member's, or an overridable method's with its base body's sources plus `this` (6.3);
      // SPEC 5.4's signature default for Callee::FnPtr and closures, with no through_fields, no through_read and no
      // stores. Fitting makes that sound: nothing that stores fits an `fn` type, a closure value or a body-less member (6.7).

store(t, T, read, wr):  // T is now held by the borrowing value that t points at, and by every value that holds it;
                    // read: T goes into a field declared `readonly &`, so into the value's read set as well; wr: T may go
                    // into a field the value writes through, which is `not read` for a write in this body (above);
                    // every test below reads held(t) and each held(s) as they stood before this store, or before
                    // the call for a call's stores
  if read, the store is not a call's read_set_only (j, j) (above), and T holds Fields(i) for a Param(i) in held(t)
      while the class behind t has its flag set (6.7), or, for a local b that t reaches (next line), a loan of
      wfields(b) when the loan by which t reaches b has the path b, or b's payload narrowed to present, and b's own
      class has its flag set, or a loan of held(b) when that path steps into a field or tuple part of b other than
      that payload and the declared class of the value there, which a field or part holds exactly, has its flag
      set:  EN0616                                                       // the read-set check, below
  for each loan in held(t) without through_ref, with a slot b as root:  held(b) ∪= T; if read, rfields(b) ∪= T;
                                                                                if wr, wfields(b) ∪= T
                                                                                // `lx` itself, also when t
                                                                                // reaches it through a value holding `&lx`
  for each slot s whose pointee is borrowing and whose held(s) meets held(t):  held(s) ∪= T; fields(s) ∪= T;
                                                                                if read, rfields(s) ∪= T
  for each Param(m), Fields(m) or RFields(m) in held(t):  held(caller(m)) ∪= T; if read, rfields(caller(m)) ∪= T
                                                                                // the caller's value, section 6.3
  inside an arrow, when held(t) holds a loan rooted at _env:  EN0616                // section 6.4
  for each Param(m), Fields(m) or RFields(m) in held(t) where m is an Own `this` or a by-value parameter of a
      borrowing class or tuple type, nullable or not (neither has a caller(m)):  EN0616    // section 6.3: nothing
                                                                                // carries such a store to the caller;
                                                                                // t may point at any value holding
                                                                                // such a key: m itself, one reached
                                                                                // through it, or a local built from
                                                                                // what it holds

kill(s):  for every slot, remove every loan whose path has root s and through_ref from held, fields, rfields and
          wfields; held(s) = {}; fields(s) = {}; rfields(s) = {}; wfields(s) = {}   // old *s is renamed away; a row
                                                              // that defines s then sets all four (above)
          if s is a parameter, every Param(s), Fields(s) and RFields(s) key becomes bare in every slot, the row that
          repoints s included, since every loan that crossed it is gone   // covered keys, below
```

So a call keeps an argument's own loan only for a result that may point into the object. A source in `through_fields` gives the result `fields(args[i])`, what the borrowing value's reference fields hold, and the auto-borrow `&lx` ends with the call. Taking `&lx` puts `held(lx)` into the reference, so `source` stays locked while any reference to `lx` is live, even after `lx`'s own last use. So does taking a whole closure local `f`, as an arrow that captures it does, since calling `f` reads through its captures (SPEC 5.5), and a whole `R | null` local, as a form's argument does, since the form reads through it (section 5, `console.log`). A store changes the caller's value, never only the temporary that borrowed it. It reads all of `held(t)`, because the loans that name the value may sit anywhere in it: `const r = id(&lx); r.setSrc(&tmp);` reaches `lx` through the loan of `&lx` that `r` holds. Inside a `Wrapper` method `retarget(s)` that calls `this.lx.setSrc(s)` through a `&Lexer` field, the key `Fields(0)` carries the store to `caller(0)`, so `retarget` stores `(1, 0)`, and at `w.retarget(&tmp)` the set of `&w` holds the loan of `&lx` that `w` was built with, which reaches `lx`. It updates every slot that shares a loan or key with `held(t)`. That may add loans to a slot that only shares a field's loan, which is over-strict but never unsound, since a store only adds loans. Any other slot that reaches the value holds a separate loan on it, which conflicts with the store's exclusive borrow if that slot is used again. A write of a whole borrowing value or of a part nested in one stores like a field write, so `*this = new Lexer(s)` stores `(1, 0)` and `*t = [&tmp, 1]` on a borrowing tuple gives `pair` the loan on `tmp`. A copy `*t` of a borrowing value holds what the value's fields hold and site(t), `t`'s own loans on it (the `Use(Copy *t)` row), and a move out of `this` in a `move` method holds what its fields hold, so `return this.lx;` in a `move` method keeps the loans of the `Lexer` it hands out. A copy of a borrowing tuple, or of a nullable one, takes a new loan per reference part, by O7, instead of sharing the source's loans as they are, because a tuple's flag is always set and its stored loans may be exclusive: `const p2 = pair; const f = &p2[0][0]; pair[0].push(4);` with `f` used afterwards is EN0706 at the push, where sharing would give the exclusive loan on `data` two holders with no loan between them. A borrowing class value copies its loans as they are, which is sound because a class whose flag is set cannot be Copy (EN0418, section 6.7).

**The read set (SPEC 5.1).** A borrowing value keeps the loans stored into its fields declared `readonly &` apart, in `rfields`, inside the main set of every stored loan: a store into such a field carries the read bit, written directly or as a call's store (section 6.3), and rule (e) skips it (section 6.7). A source in `through_read` gives the result `rfields(args[i])`, the read set alone: no loan on the value and none of what its other fields hold, whatever its class writes. That is sound because nothing in the value writes through a read-set loan: a reference read from a `readonly &` field is marked (section 6.1), and the read-set check of `store` keeps such a field from taking what another reference field of the same value holds, in a class whose flag is set. A reference read through any other reference field of the value behind `t` holds `Fields(i)` for the `Param(i)` that `held(t)` holds, or, for a local `b`, all of `held(b)`, and so every loan of `wfields(b)`, the loans that `b`'s other fields may write through, so storing it into a `readonly &` field, as `this.src = this.dst.slice(0, 1);` does in a `Copier` whose `step` writes through `dst`, is EN0616 at the store, naming the field, as `rustc` rejects `self.src = &self.dst[..]` with `lifetime may not live long enough`, while a reference from a parameter, `this.src = s;`, or from a `readonly &` field, `this.src = this.src.slice(1, n);`, holds neither and compiles (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-copier`). A reference read from another value's field, `this.src = other.dst;` with `other: &Copier`, holds `Fields(1)`, and since `Copier` writes through `dst` that store is not `fields_only` (section 6.3), so the caller's `c.alias(&b)` puts the loan on `b` into `c`'s read set as well, and `b.step()` while `c` is still used is EN0706. Nor does one loan reach both a `readonly &` field and another reference field of such a value: each store reborrows its operand, so `this.src = s; this.dst = s;` takes a shared and then, by rule (e), an exclusive loan on `*s`, and the second conflicts with the first, which `caller(0)` keeps live, EN0706, and an operand copied out of a field takes a loan through the value it is read from (section 5), so `this.dst = o.dst; this.src = o.dst;` is EN0708 at the second `o.dst`, and `const p = c.dst; const q = c.dst;` with `q` stored into a field written through is EN0706 at the second `c.dst`, where two copies with no loan let one buffer reach both fields of a new `Copier` (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-field-copy`). For a local value, `wfields(b)` is exact where an `Aggregate` built it and its own body wrote it field by field; every store through a call joins it, except a `read_set_only` one, whose loans are marked and go into no field written through (section 6.1), and one into a `readonly &` field through a `&` parameter, or the reference parts of a by-value tuple parameter, that the callee only reads, Read in its contract, which never reaches a field written through, since storing it into one would make that parameter Write by rule (e), while a by-value borrowing class parameter, like an Own `this`, has no permission, and `*this = u` may carry what `u`'s own other fields write through, so its store always joins, and `c.reset(Copier.make(&tbl, &buf))` followed by `const p = c.dst!; c.src = p;` is EN0616 at the store (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-reset`); and it is all of `held(b)` where a call, a copy or a move out of a field defined `b`, or `b` is a by-value parameter. So `let w = Copier.make(s, d); const p = w.dst; w.src = p;` is EN0616 at the store, as `rustc` rejects its twin with `lifetime may not live long enough` and E0505, where reading `held(b)` outside `rfields(b)`, which these defaults make empty, let `c.peek()` keep a token into the buffer that `c.step()` reallocates, while `w.src = t;` from a third parameter compiles (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-alias`, `FV-02-alias-other`). Where one loan does reach both fields, as the loan on the receiver that both parts of one `splitAt` hold when a constructor stores one part into each, the parameter is Write, so the caller's store counts that loan in `wfields` as well as in the read set, and storing what the other field holds into the `readonly &` field is EN0616 (`FV-02-alias-split`). So a `through_read` result keeps nothing that writing, moving, dropping or repointing the value would invalidate: in `const a = c.peek(); c.step();`, `a` holds only a shared loan on the source `c` was built over, which the exclusive borrow of `c` does not overlap (section 6.6). The check's part for a reference parameter keys on the static class behind `t`, never on the classes below it, whose flags other modules may set and G3 keeps out of the base's module, and the caller checks such a store again against what it knows of its own argument; its part for a local `b` keys on `b`'s own class, which is exact, since a local, a temporary or a by-value parameter holds exactly its class and never a subclass (SPEC 4.3), and known to the module doing the check, so an inherited `Base.retarget` that stores into a `readonly &` field, called on a local `Sub` whose flag is on, is checked with `Sub`'s flag: `w.retarget(w.peek())` on a `w` that `Sub.make` returned is EN0616, as `w.src = q;` is (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-upcast-local`), where keying on `Base`, whose flag is off, skipped the check. Where `t` reaches `b` by a loan on a value nested in a field or tuple part of `b` other than `b`'s payload narrowed to present, which counts as `b` itself, as after `const r = &o.inner;`, the part keys on that value's declared class, which a field or part holds exactly, and tests all of `held(b)`, as the write `o.inner.src = p` is checked (above), so `const p = r.dst; r.src = p;` is EN0616 at the store whatever `o`'s own class writes, where keying on `o`'s class and `wfields(o)` let a nested `Copier` take into `src` what its `dst` writes through (`TESTING.md` `FV-02-nested-ref`). Inside a subclass method an upcast of `this` cannot carry what another field holds into such a store: `const b: &Base = this; b.retarget(this.dst.slice(0, 1));` is EN0708 at `this.dst`, since `b`'s exclusive borrow of `*this` is still live while the part is taken, and taking the part first is EN0706 at `this`, as `rustc` rejects both (`INF-04-upcast`). A tuple part declared `readonly` is no `readonly &` field here, until a mark per part is added: a reference read through it takes `fields`, never `rfields`, so it is `through_fields` at most, or an ordinary source where the flag is on, and never `through_read`, and a store into a tuple field never carries the read bit, so its loans join the main set, exclusive ones in a class whose flag is set (section 6.7, `TESTING.md` `FV-05-pad-readset`, `FV-05-pad-flag`).

**A source that is stored and returned (SPEC 5.4, I2).** When a call's result may come from a source `j` that the call also stores into the value behind argument `m`, the stored copy outlives the call beside the result, and both hold the loans of argument `j`. No loan between them is needed. Stage 4 of section 6.7 makes the result read-only, so the caller never writes through it. When the class behind `m` writes through its reference fields, and the field it stores into is not declared `readonly &`, the store's reborrow of `j` is exclusive (N3 (e)) and `caller(m)` holds it until every `Return` (liveness, below), so no path of the body that stores `j` can also return a reference derived from it: with `Holder.keep(x: &i64[]): &Slice<i64> { this.r = x; return x.slice(0, 1); }` and a `grow` that pushes through `this.r`, `keep` is EN0708 at `x.slice(0, 1)`, as `rustc` gives E0502 there, and a free `aim(c: &Cursor, xs: &i64[])` that stores `xs` into `c` and returns a view of it is EN0708 at `xs.slice(0, 1)`. A body that stores `j` on one path and returns from it on another compiles, and is sound, because on any one run the result and the stored copy never both point at `j`'s data. When the class only reads through its reference fields, or the field is declared `readonly &`, neither holder writes through the stored loan, whatever its mode, so a `Lexer` whose `reset(s)` stores `s` and returns `s.slice(0, 1)` leaves `lx` free: `const first = lx.reset(&text); const t = lx.next(); console.log(first, t);` compiles (`TESTING.md` `S2-04-reset`).

`kill` is what makes the cursor pattern work. `cur = &(*cur).next` builds the new set first, removes loans on the old `*cur` everywhere, and removes its own new loan from the result, because that loan's path names the old `*cur`. The loan on `head` stays held. So a later `const v = &(*cur).val; cur2 = &(*cur).next` inherits nothing stale, and the SPEC 5.3 cursor pattern is accepted.

**The `for...of` hold (N9).** For a `Path` source the loop's phantom slot exists only here: no MIR local, no storage. Taking the hold at the pre-header is a whole definition of that slot: it kills the slot's old set and sets it to `Hold(loop)`, a loan on the source path, plus a shared loan on every reference slot the path goes through and on every reference field of a borrowing value the path goes through. A field's loan sits on the field place itself, `(this, through_ref, Field(view))` for `for (const x of this.view)`, not on what the field points at, so a write of the field overlaps it. These crossing loans are shallow on their own side (`overlap`, below): they meet an access to the slot or field itself, a write, a move, a reassignment or its `StorageDead`, and never an access that only goes through the reference stored there, so `for (const x of &this.items) { this.count += 1; }` compiles, as `rustc` accepts `for x in self.items.iter() { self.count += 1; }` (`TESTING.md` `HOLD-through`), and a written binding's exclusive bind `Ref` through that reference does not meet them, while a write through it to the source itself still meets the hold's loan on the path. So neither the source nor a reference that reaches it can change, and `this.view = other;` in that loop's body is EN0706, `` cannot reassign `this.view` during iteration ``. The hold is shared, or exclusive when the binding is write-live anywhere in the body (SPEC 5.6). The phantom slot is defined at the pre-header and used at the head, so its loans are live wherever a next head of the same run is reachable: `q = []; break;`, `q = []; return;` and `q.push(1); break;` are accepted, while `q = []; continue;` or a change that falls through to the head is EN0706, also against a hold that a written loop variable made exclusive (SPEC 5.6). The same holds for a change made by a called function, since passing `&h` to a Write parameter is an exclusive borrow, and by a local function through its hidden argument. A run's hold is dead once the run ends, even inside an outer loop that comes back to the loop's start, because every path from outside the loop to its head passes the pre-header, which redefines the slot: `while (n < 2) { for (const x of &q) { *x += 1; } q.push(n); n += 1; }` compiles, as does a nested loop that ends with `q.push(1); break;`, and the pre-header check below never meets the loop's own earlier hold. The loop's own head and bind accesses, the head's `CheckTag` run and `Len` and the bind's `CheckBounds` and `CheckTag` runs and `Ref`, are checked against every live loan except the hold's and those of the loop's own bind site (turn keys, below). Exempting the tag checks is sound, since a tag on the source path changes only by a write of that nullable or a prefix, or by repointing a reference slot or reference field on the path, and each of these meets the hold or its shared loans, so a writing loop over `&b.items` inside `if (b.items != null)`, or over a narrowed `xs?: &Slice<i64>`, compiles. A `Once` source needs no phantom slot: `_src` is a real slot, and the head keeps it live (section 5).

**Turn keys (O2).** Each turn of a `for...of` binds a different element, so a later turn of one run never meets the binds of earlier turns at the loop's own head and bind, as with Rust's `iter_mut`. The bind site has three keys, and the transfer renames them in every `held`, `fields`, `rfields` and `wfields` set (above): at the bind's `Ref`, the key of this turn, `Site(l, This)`, becomes `Site(l, EarlierThisRun)` before the `Ref` adds this turn's loan as `This`, and at the loop's pre-header, before the hold, `This` and `EarlierThisRun` both become `Site(l, EarlierRuns)`, the last turn of the run before included. A loan records its site and which of the three its turn is, never the turn's number. The check skips a live loan with any key of the loop's own bind site at that loop's head and bind accesses, its head's `CheckTag` run and `Len` and its bind's checks and `Ref`, the only accesses where a later turn meets an earlier one: `let keep: &i64 | null = null; for (const x of &q) { if (x > 2) { keep = x; } } if (keep != null) { *keep = 0; }` compiles and prints `[1, 5, 3, 0]` for `q = [1, 5, 3, 7]`. Every other access meets those loans as `[*]` meets `[*]`: a read, write or index of `q` in the body or after the loop, the head and bind of another loop over `q`, nested or later, and the hold. A comparison of one loan against another, `loans_overlap` below, which N6's part test uses for the copies O7 would make (section 6.7), treats two keys of one bind site as disjoint only for `This` against `EarlierThisRun`: within one run the hold keeps the source from changing except through the binds, and the index only grows, so this turn's element is none of the earlier turns' elements. This turn's key against itself, `EarlierThisRun` against itself, since both may stand for one earlier turn, and `EarlierRuns` against any key overlap, since a later run may bind the same elements again, or those of a source that changed between runs. The pre-header takes the hold as a borrow of the source, checked against every live loan, so a binding kept from an earlier run of the same loop, inside an outer loop, conflicts when the loop starts again, with EN0707 or EN0708 by mode at the pre-header. This is what keeps the skip sound: the skip at the head covers all three keys of the site, so a bind kept from an earlier run must be met before the head. With a `use` that writes through its parameter, `let keep: &i64 | null = null; while (c) { for (const x of &q) { keep = x; } } use(keep);` is EN0707 at the inner loop's source, whose hold is exclusive because `x` is write-live through `keep`. For a `Once` source, the borrow that sets `_src` at the pre-header, `&place` or the reborrow or call that yields a reference rvalue, is that check.

**Bind marks (O2).** Every key that a loop's bind takes from its source, each key of its `Ref`'s set but its own loan, which come from `held(_src)` for a `Once` source and from the slot or field the source path crosses for a `Path` source, carries the mark *via bind l* in the binding, l being that loop, and keeps it in every slot it is copied into: beside each slot's set, `held` records for each key the loop whose bind alone gave it, if any. A key that reaches a slot any other way, an assignment from another path or a `Ref` through the parameter itself, as `a = &xs[1]` takes `xs`'s key, is plain, and wherever two copies of one key end up in one slot, by any row, a join, an `Aggregate` (class, tuple, present or `ClosureEnv`), a store, a `Ref`, a call result, or the renames at a bind and at a pre-header, their marks merge by the union rule: marked with plain, or two different marks, gives plain, so a mark only ever says that every way the key came was that bind. That covers a literal: `t = [whole, x]` with `const whole = xs;` holds `xs`'s key plain, so `t[0]` is compared with a later `x` by path (`TESTING.md` `TK-03-mark-literal`); and a rename: an inner loop m's binding that a join keeps beside a plain copy of one of the outer loop's site loans meets, at the outer loop's next bind, the copy that the rename brings in marked via m, and the key stays plain (`TK-03-mark-rename`), where keeping either copy's mark alone made a pair aliasing one element writable. A bind never marks a key of its own site, which its source holds when it holds a binding of an earlier turn, as one does after `w.last = x;` in `for (const x of w.src)`, and stage 4's exception under that loop never applies to such a key, which `loans_overlap` decides unless another loop's bind marked it, as an inner loop over the outer loop's binding marks the outer loop's loan of this turn, when the exception under that loop decides it like any key that bind marks (`TESTING.md` `TK-03-inner-pair`). A mark never outlives its run: the loop's pre-header makes every key marked via bind l plain again, in every slot, because evaluating the source again kills `_src`, and repointing a source path's slot between runs kills that slot, which removes an earlier run's site loans from the bindings kept from it while their marked keys stay.
