The declarations are each class's fields in order with their types, a `readonly` before a reference's `&` included, which of them have a default, and the inherited fields whose default it replaces (section 5), from which an importer computes the override sets its constructors pass on through `super(...)`, so constructor instances need nothing more in the file, while a named form in an importer calls the class's `NamedFill` (section 5), so no default's value is in the API section or in an importer's MIR, and editing one changes only the declaring module's stored code; whether it is `final` or `abstract` and its base class, which decide its hidden method-table pointer; its methods with their signatures, which of them are `abstract` and which can be overridden, and its overridable methods in table order, one slot each, which names a method's dynamic copy where it has an `&I` or `&I | null` parameter, while a method compiled once per type is never overridable and has no slot (section 2.6); each interface's members; each generic item's bounds, and for each declaration, every method's included, which of its type parameters have `views_ok`, a declaration with no body none, and which have `strings_ok`, an interface member all (section 4); each item's visibility, `readonly` and `@requires`; each constant's value, which an importer's MIR copies where it reads it (SPEC 4.9), except that a `&string` constant or a constant literal view names the one read-only static its defining module emits, whose literal the file stores, so every importer points at that static (section 4, **Const eval**); each enum's cases; which parameters of each function and method have defaults, since a call passes the number of parameters it supplied (section 5); and the public layouts and offsets. The module's API hash covers this section together with the API hashes of its direct imports, as Rust's crate hash chains, so a change anywhere in its import closure changes it. Making an imported class `final`, or reordering its fields, therefore rebuilds the modules whose MIR made a `Virtual` call or used a field index under the old declaration, also when the class reaches them only through another import, as the result type of `make(): Thing` or a re-export (`TESTING.md` `S2-01-transitive`);
- the post-inference MIR of every reachable body, each with its `bodyHash`;
- in an unhashed section, the witnesses of section 6.7, each ending at the module's boundary, so the file holds no other module's witness and no freshness key needs to cover one.

Entries are keyed by stable item path, never by `DefId`: the module path, the item, the member, and for an overload its parameter types. The file lists them in path order, and its hashes use the compiler's own hash function (section 1).

**Freshness.** The key is the file format, the compiler and embedded library identity, target, the module's own source hash, each direct import's API hash, a used-items hash over every item, from any module, that the module's stored MIR mentions, the names it imports and the types it reaches through them, as results, fields or re-exports, alike (each item's existence and every fact that its module's API section states of its declaration, its type and layout included, but not contracts and flags), and a hash of the file's own contents, which its header records and the driver recomputes on load, so a damaged or partly written file is never fresh. The mode is not part of it: `ParseMode::Test` adds only the module's `.test.en` part, a unit above the module, checked from its own source and never stored, and the module's contracts, layouts and API hash are the same in both modes (section 4), so the file that `build` writes serves `check` and `test`, for a module with no source too (`TESTING.md` `MD3-06-modes`). Nor are `cpu` and `float`, for a file with source or without, because nothing the file stores depends on them, an invariant the compiler keeps: its MIR, contracts, flags, `views_ok`, `strings_ok`, layouts and API hash are the same at every level and float strategy, since `cpu.has` folds only at mono (section 8.3) and the strategy acts only in code generation (section 7.10), while the target, on which layouts depend, stays in the key, so `build --cpu=i386` and `build --cpu=sse2 --float=sse2` write byte-identical interface files (`MD3-06-same-bytes`). What does depend on them is never cached: phase 4's level checks, EN0901, EN0910 and the float rules, run in every command that reaches phase 4, over the MIR of every module in the program, read from an interface file or freshly built (section 7.14), so a module with no source whose file `build --cpu=i586` wrote serves a `--cpu=i386` build, which compiles when its code has nothing above `i386` and reports an `` asm(`cpuid`, ...) `` it contains as EN0901, naming that module's instruction, never EN0315 (`MD3-06-cpu`). A file whose key does not match is rebuilt from source. A module that has no source, built from its interface file alone, cannot be rebuilt, but it can be rechecked. When the only parts of its key that do not match are direct imports' API hashes, and the used-items hash, recomputed against the new interfaces, still matches, every item its stored code uses keeps its existence and every declared fact, and only contracts or flags moved, so the driver rechecks the module: it recomputes its contracts and flags from the stored MIR against the imports' new contracts, by the stages of section 6.7, and then runs all of phase 3 over its stored declarations and bodies, as for a module compiled from source (section 2.6): the borrow check, the narrowing check (N2), and every comparison that reads contracts or flags, EN0615 for each stored override against its base's current declared contract, for every satisfaction the stored code relies on, for each bound and for each `fn` coercion, EN0714, and EN0418 for `implements Copy` against the recomputed flags; on success it writes a fresh file with the new key and the recomputed contracts, which may change the module's own API hash and so rebuild its importers. A borrow or contract error that the recheck finds is reported with its ordinary code at the stored span, with a note at the interface file naming the changed import, `` note: this module has no source; it was rechecked against the new `lib` ``: with no source for `mid`, whose `twice(xs: &i64[])` keeps `const f = &xs[0];` across `lib`'s `grow(xs)` and then reads `f`, a `grow` that changes from reading `xs` to pushing to it makes that stored line EN0706, and a base method whose body edit makes its declared store `fields_only` makes a sourceless override that keeps the whole parameter EN0615 at that override, where rerunning only the borrow check left `Sub.take` storing a reference to a dead frame through a `Virtual` call that applied the base's narrower store (`TESTING.md` `OQ16-refit-override`, `OQ16-refit-copy`, `OQ16-refit-narrow`). A used item whose declaration changed in any way other than its contract or flag, or that no longer exists, and any other stale part of the key, the compiler identity, the target or the file's own contents among them, is EN0315, naming the file, as Rust's E0460 does, and is never rechecked, because the recheck reruns only phase 3: `grow` gaining a parameter, a used class made `final` while its layout stays, which would free a sourceless subclass with the wrong size, and a used method made `abstract`, which would leave a sourceless subclass's slot empty, are each EN0315 (`TESTING.md` `S2-11-final`, `S2-11-abstract`). Freshness decides only whether a file may be used; a fresh file is still checked when it is loaded (below).

**Who reads what.** `enrang check` checks a caller against an import's contracts from its interface file, so it needs no dependency source. `enrang build` may use an import's interface MIR instead of its source; code generation stays whole-program from MIR, with no object files and no linker step between Enrang modules. When `build`, `run`, `test` or `dev` compile a module from source, they derive its contracts from its bodies, never from its interface file. The file's key records the compiler identity, so a file written by another compiler rebuilds from source, or is EN0315 for a module with no source, like Rust's E0514.

**Checked on load.** An interface file is never trusted. Whenever a module is used from one, by any command, the compiler first runs the MIR verifier of section 2.5 on every stored body, in release builds too: besides the verifier's own rules, among them that every run-time guard is there, a `CheckBounds`, `CheckTag` or `CheckClass` before each access that needs one, each `Intrinsic` of a kind that is not safe inside a recorded `unsafe` region, a `Virtual` call only where section 2.5 allows one, a `Convert` only between the type pairs its kind allows, and a `SetTable` only on a class with a hidden pointer, it checks the stored code's types against the current declarations and every operation that needs `unsafe` (EN0801, EN0803, EN0907) against the regions the code records, and reruns every phase-2 rule whose verdict phase 3, mono, codegen or the VM rely on, over the file's stored declarations and bodies: visibility, the class, construction and position rules, bound satisfaction, the view check and the string clause of satisfaction for every satisfaction the stored code relies on (section 4), as section 2.5 lists them. Stored code that fails it is EN0316, naming the file. The compiler then recomputes each contract, its bits for G07's help included, and each class flag from the stored MIR, by the stages of section 6.7, and each declaration's `views_ok` and `strings_ok` (section 4), and runs all of phase 3 over the stored declarations and bodies after that: the borrow check, the narrowing check and every comparison that reads contracts or flags, EN0615, EN0714 and EN0418 against the recomputed flags; phase 3 only, once per module, since that MIR is already typed and verified. For a fresh file, a contract, flag, `views_ok` or `strings_ok` that differs from the one the file states, or a stored body or declaration that fails one of those checks, is EN0316 too, naming the file, since its imports are the ones it was written against: `` the interface file of `lib.en` says `grow` only reads `xs`, but its code pushes to it ``, with `note: the file was not written by this compiler, or it was changed afterwards`. A recheck (above) runs the same pass but replaces the stated contracts and flags instead of comparing them, because an import moved under them, and gives a borrow or contract error its ordinary code. A forged fresh file that states a sourceless override's own store truthfully still fails, since the override no longer fits its base's current declared store: EN0316 (`TESTING.md` `OQ16-refit-forged`). Freshness cannot catch this alone, because whoever changes the file can also recompute its content hash. So the guarantees of SPEC 5 hold for any interface file, written by this compiler or not, a module shipped without its source included, and an importer is always checked against contracts that the stored code really has. The stored MIR records its `unsafe` regions explicitly (section 2.5), so stage 3 reads which bodies contain `unsafe` from the code itself and not from a claim beside it, and the verifier checks the `unsafe` boundary against the same regions: a forged `poke(x: &i64)` whose raw write through `x as *i64` has lost its `unsafe` region is EN0316, never a write through a read-only reference, and so is a stored `add(a: i32): i32` that returns a `string` (`TESTING.md` `S2-18-unsafe`, `Q34-types`). The cost is compile time only: the verifier is one pass over the stored MIR, as the borrow check is.

**Hashes.** `sigHash` includes the whole contract of section 6.7: the permissions, the sources, `through_fields` and `through_read`, the result, and the stores with their `fields_only`, `read_set_only` and read bits, so a body edit that adds a store changes it; it leaves out only the bit that says whether a body moves or writes a by-value parameter, which G07's help reads and no caller's check does. It also covers which parameters have defaults, since a call passes the number of parameters it supplied (section 5). An indirect call in `enrang dev` compares a pointer hash that covers only the parameter types and the result type. It leaves out the whole contract (permissions, sources, `through_fields`, `through_read`, result writability and stores), unsafety and required features, because the coercion to the `fn` type has already checked that they fit, and fitting lets them differ from the `fn` type's. So with `private function first(a: &string, b: &string): &string { return a; }` stored as `const pick: fn(a: &string, b: &string) => &string = first;`, `console.log(pick(&ada, &grace));` prints `Ada` in `dev`, as it does in a build (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` ADV-54), and a reload that changes the target's contract pins the retired body for the values that name it, with a warning (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` REF-10). That premise needs every function value to name the body its coercion was checked against, so every function reference in a body version, a direct call, an `FnAddr`, an `FnToPtr` or `FnAsClosure` and the callee of a `StringForm` shim alike, resolves to the `(FnId, sigHash)` that version was compiled against: a function value that an old frame makes after a reload names the retired body when the target's `sigHash` changed, as a value made before the reload does (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 2.3, ADV-65). A pinned body counts as reachable code at every later reload, as a frame's does, so a reload under which it would read a removed or retyped field, call through a table entry whose `sigHash` changed, build, through a retired constructor or an implicit one, a class that has since gained a field with no default, or call a method on, or build with `new`, a class that gained or lost `final`, is refused with EN1213, naming the value's path, with a restart offered, since a pin lasts as long as the value (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` FAIL-14, ADV-64). `bodyHash` covers post-inference MIR, and a frame adopts a new body only if both hashes match (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 5). Method tables are versioned with the code table: when a reload commits, every live object's hidden method-table pointer and every table word of a `Box<I>` or of a two-word `&I`, in a frame, a field, a tuple part or a nullable, is retargeted to the new version of the table it names, its class's own table or its class's table for that interface, also for a class whose table changed while its schema did not, except the table word of an `&I | null` whose object word is 0, a `null`, which names no table and is left as it is (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` REF-13, `TESTING.md` `VI-09-reload`); a live object whose class no longer fits the place it is held through, a `Box<Animal>` whose `Dog` no longer extends `Animal` or a `Box<Speaker>` whose `Robot` no longer satisfies `Speaker`, refuses the reload with EN1210, naming the path (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` MIG-16), while a class that only drops `implements Speaker` and keeps its members still satisfies it, structurally, and commit builds its new table for `Speaker`; a frame whose reachable code would make such a value, by an upcast, a conversion to `Box<I>` or `&I`, or a per-class copy for a class that no longer fits, blocks the reload, and a pinned body that would refuses it with EN1213 (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` ADV-69); a frame whose reachable code calls through a table entry whose `sigHash` changed, or that is in the middle of such a call, blocks the reload, so a `Virtual` call never reaches a retired override that new code was not checked against (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` sections 5 to 7); a frame whose reachable code, or a retired body it would bind or make a function value of, calls a method on a place of a class that gained or lost `final` in this reload, or builds one by `new`, blocks the reload too, since mirbuild fixed under the old declaration whether that call is `Virtual` and whether that `new` writes a hidden pointer, as stored MIR fixes them for EN0315 (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 6, MIG-15, ADV-73, ADV-74), while a named form does not, since the class's `NamedFill`, which it binds by `sigHash`, writes the table under the current declaration (section 5); and a frame whose reachable code would build, through a retired constructor or an implicit one, a class that has since gained a field with no default blocks it as well, while a gained field with a default first gets its initialiser or type default stored, and a frame running a constructor, whose `this` is tracked field by field, never takes loop-head replacement (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 6, ADV-70, ADV-71). A `CheckClass` names `A`'s table by its class, so after a commit it compares with the live version's table, and an exact `Animal` still unboxes (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` ADV-62).

**Meta-tests** (`TESTING.md`): a cold build equals a warm one, in output and in bytes; the API section is identical across checkout paths and shuffled imports; a permission flipped in an interface file leaves the output of a build from source unchanged, because the file's own content hash makes it stale, and for a module with no source it is EN0315; a module with no source whose import changed only contracts is rechecked against them, a borrow error the recheck finds keeps its ordinary code at the stored span with a note naming the changed import, and a changed type, layout or existence of an import item it uses is EN0315 (`OQ16-recheck`); the recheck and the check on load run all of phase 3, so a stored override that no longer fits is EN0615, a stored `implements Copy` on a class whose recomputed flag is on is EN0418, a stored narrowed use after a call that now writes is EN0401, and a forged fresh file that hides any of them is EN0316 (`OQ16-refit-override`, `OQ16-refit-copy`, `OQ16-refit-narrow`, `OQ16-refit-forged`); an edit that keeps every contract of a module but changes a witness chain leaves a warm build equal to a cold one, in output and in bytes (`OQ16-witness`); a fresh file whose stored code does not match its contracts is EN0316, never a run that reads freed memory (`OQ16-grow`); toggling `final` on an imported class, or reordering its fields, rebuilds its importers, and the warm build then equals the cold one, also for a class that reaches a module only through another import, while a module with no source that uses it is EN0315 (`S2-01-transitive`); a used class made `final`, or a used method made `abstract`, with type and layout kept, is EN0315 for a module with no source, never a recheck (`S2-11-final`, `S2-11-abstract`); the stored code records its `unsafe` regions, and stored code that fails the verifier on load, a raw write moved out of its `unsafe` region or a result type changed, is EN0316 in a release build too (`S2-18-unsafe`, `Q34-types`); stored code that breaks a phase-2 rule the later phases rely on, a write through another class's `private` field, an override with another result type, a `new` of an abstract class or a reference in an element type, is EN0316, while a genuine named form in another module that leaves out a `private` field with a default loads (`MD3-01-private`, `MD3-01-override`, `MD3-01-abstract`, `MD3-01-position`, `MD3-01-named`); stored code that lacks a run-time guard, or runs an intrinsic whose kind is not safe outside every region, is EN0316 (`MD3-02-guards`); the safety bit of each `define_intrinsics!` kind is set exactly when a safe declaration names it or mirbuild emits it in safe code, checked against every `@intrinsic` declaration (section 2.5); an edited field default that a named form in another module leaves out changes only the declaring module's stored code, so the warm build equals the cold one, and a module with no source that holds the named form needs no EN0315 (`MD3-03-default`); a file that `build` wrote serves `check` and `test` with no EN0315 (`MD3-06-modes`); `build --cpu=i386` and `build --cpu=sse2 --float=sse2` write byte-identical interface files (`MD3-06-same-bytes`); a module with no source whose file was written at `i586` serves an `i386` build, which compiles when its code has nothing above `i386`, and is EN0901 naming its instruction when it has, never EN0315 (`MD3-06-cpu`); an edit to a leaf propagates through modules that only forward it; `check --cpu=i486` then `check --cpu=i386` reports EN0901 both times, since the level checks are never cached; test-item errors survive an existing interface file; each visible library item has one contract across target rows and between build and test modes; each mark on a library declaration equals the contract inferred from its body.

## 7. Native backend

### 7.1 Pipeline

```text
Mono MIR -> lower + legalise -> LIR -> simplify -> allocate (vreg -> Loc)
  -> emit (Assembler: form + operands) -> relax branches, fixups -> CodeBlob per function
  -> link::Object -> link (order, GC, resolve) -> ELF64 | ELF32 | PE32+ | flat | BIOS image
```

No optimiser beyond: LIR copy propagation and dead-def removal (`--opt=1`, default; `--opt=0` disables), fused compare-and-branch, shortest-form selection, and the allocator. No inliner. `xs[i]` is built inline by the lowering, so the most common small operation needs no call.

### 7.2 LIR

```rust
pub enum VRegClass { G32, G64 /* 64-bit targets only */, X /* SSE2 only */ }
pub enum Val { V(VReg), Imm(i32) }
pub enum Mem {
    Base { base: VReg, index: Option<(VReg, u8)>, disp: i32 }, Slot { slot: SlotId, disp: i32 }, Sym { sym: SymbolRef, disp: i32 },
    Indirect { ptr_slot: SlotId, disp: i32 },   // over-aligned local reached through its hidden pointer slot
}
pub enum Inst {
    Mov { dst: VReg, src: Val, w: Width }, MovImm64 { dst: VReg, imm: u64 },
    Load { dst: VReg, mem: Mem, w: Width, sign: bool, volatile: bool },
    Store { mem: Mem, src: Val, w: Width, volatile: bool }, Lea { dst: VReg, mem: Mem },
    Alu { op: AluOp, dst: VReg, a: Val, b: Val, w: Width },
    AluPair { op: PairOp, dst: (VReg, VReg), a: (Val, Val), b: (Val, Val) },   // add/adc, sub/sbb; i386 only
    Checked { op: CheckOp, dst: VReg, a: Val, b: Val, w: Width, signed: bool, site: PanicSite },
    CheckedPair { op: PairOp, dst: (VReg, VReg), a: (Val, Val), b: (Val, Val), signed: bool, site: PanicSite },
    Bounds { index: Val, len: Val, site: PanicSite },
    SetCc { cc: Cond, dst: VReg, a: Val, b: Val, w: Width },
    CmpBranch { cc: Cond, a: Val, b: Val, w: Width, then_: BlockId, else_: BlockId },
    CmpPairBranch { cc: Cond, a: (Val, Val), b: (Val, Val), then_: BlockId, else_: BlockId },
    MulWide { lo: VReg, hi: VReg, a: Val, b: Val }, DivRem { op: DivOp, dst: VReg, a: Val, b: Val, w: Width, signed: bool },
    Shift { op: ShiftOp, dst: VReg, a: Val, count: Val, w: Width }, ShiftPair { op: ShiftOp, dst: (VReg, VReg), a: (Val, Val), count: Val },
    Fp { op: FpOp, dst: VReg, a: VReg, b: Val, w: FpWidth }, FpCmpSet { .. }, FpCmpBranch { .. }, FpConvert { .. },
    Call { target: CallTarget, args: Vec<ArgLoc>, rets: Vec<VReg> }, CallForeign { .. },
    Asm(Box<LirAsm>), MemCopy { dst: Mem, src: Mem, bytes: u32 }, MemSet { dst: Mem, value: u8, bytes: u32 },
    MemCopyDyn { dst: Val, src: Val, bytes: Val, overlap: bool }, MemSetDyn { dst: Val, value: Val, bytes: Val },
    DivWide { q: VReg, r: VReg, hi: Val, lo: Val, d: Val },   // div r32; only from the DivWideU32 intrinsic
    Jmp(BlockId), Ret(Vec<Val>), Unreachable,
}
```

**Key invariant: flags never live across an LIR instruction.** `AluPair` emits `add` and `adc` (or `sub` and `sbb`) as one unit with only `mov` between them, because `mov` preserves flags and `xor r, r` does not, so the emitter never uses the zero idiom inside a pair. `Checked` contains its own `jo` or `jc`. Spill code therefore cannot break a carry or an overflow check. Checked by `lir::verify` and by the chaos allocator.

**Values.** Never-borrowed scalars are vregs. Aggregates and address-taken scalars live in frame slots with explicit size and alignment. Narrow values (`i8`, `i16`) have undefined upper bits and use native 8- and 16-bit forms, except for multiply and divide, which widen (section 7.8). `MemCopy` carries a `u32`; a constant size above `2^31`, which a heap element type can reach on `x86_64`, uses `MemCopyDyn`.

### 7.3 Legalisation

| MIR | `x86_64` | `i386` soft | `i386` `sse2` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `i64` add, sub | `Alu` G64 | `AluPair` | `AluPair` |
| Checked `i64` add, sub | `Checked` | `CheckedPair`, OF or CF of the top word | same |
| `i64` mul | `imul` with `jo`; `mul` for `u64` | `__mulo64`, `__umulo64` | same |
| `i64` div, rem | `idiv`/`div` with checks | zero and `MIN / -1` inline, then `__sdiv64`, `__udiv64`, `__srem64`, `__urem64` | same |
| `i64` compare | `cmp` | `CmpPairBranch`: high word, then low word | same |
| `i64` shift | `Shift` | `ShiftPair`: `shld`/`shrd` plus a branch for counts of 32 or more | same |
| `f32`, `f64` move | `movss`, `movsd`, X vreg | G32 vregs | X vreg |
| Float add, sub, mul, div, sqrt | `Fp` | `__addf64` and friends | `Fp` |
| Float `%` | `__remf64` | same | same |
| Float compare | `ucomisd` | `__cmpf64` | `ucomisd` |
| Float to int, after the range and NaN check | `i8` to `i32`, `u8`, `u16`: `cvttsd2si r32`. `u32`, `i64`: `cvttsd2si r64`. `u64`: `cvttsd2si r64` if `x < 2^63`, otherwise `cvttsd2si(x - 2^63)` xor `1 << 63` | `__f64toi32` and friends | `i8`, `i16`, `i32`, `u8`, `u16`: `cvttsd2si r32`. `u32`, `i64`, `u64`: helpers |
| Int to float | `cvtsi2sd`; `u64` by shift-and-sticky | helper | `cvtsi2sd` for `i32`, helper otherwise |
| `toBits`, `fromBits` | `movq` plus canonical check | integer moves | `movd`, `movq` via slot |

`cvttsd2si` returns the indefinite value `0x8000000000000000` for `1.0e19` as `r64` [measured], which is why `u64` needs the second path and why `u32` needs `r64` or a helper. GenExpr and GenFloat force edges at `2^31`, `2^32`, `2^63`, `2^64` and one ulp below each, on every cell.

**`i386` `x87`** legalises integers exactly as the `soft` column does. Floats follow SPEC 9.3's recipe: they live in memory slots, never in vregs across instructions; each arithmetic `Fp` loads its operands onto the FPU stack, operates and stores the result, so no excess precision survives an operation; f64 multiply and divide run between the strictfp scale pair (`2^-15360`, `2^15360`); moves, copies and `toBits` are integer moves; `%` is the `core` function. Int-float conversions, a control-word switch for truncation or software, are chosen at M8.

### 7.4 Register strategy

Each vreg gets one location for its whole lifetime, with no splitting.

| Role | i386 | x86-64 |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed | `esp`, `ebp` | `rsp`, `rbp` |
| Scratch, never allocated | `eax`, `ecx`, `edx` | `rax`, `rcx`, `rdx`, `r10`, `r11` |
| Allocatable, callee-saved | `ebx`, `esi`, `edi` | `rbx`, `r12`-`r15` |
| Allocatable, caller-saved | none | `rsi`, `rdi`, `r8`, `r9` |
| XMM allocatable | `xmm0`-`xmm5` | `xmm0`-`xmm13` |
| XMM scratch | `xmm6`, `xmm7` | `xmm14`, `xmm15` |

The scratch set is exactly what `mul`, `div` and shifts need, so fixed-register constraints vanish. All XMM registers are caller-saved in the internal convention. Byte operations on `esi` or `edi` route through a scratch register, since only `eax`-`ebx` have byte forms on i386. `rsi` and `rdi` have them with REX.

**Allocators.**
- `none`: every vreg in a slot. Exercises every memory-operand path.
- `linear`: linear scan without holes or splitting. Each physical register has blocked positions (calls for caller-saved, `MemCopy` for `rsi`, `rdi`, `Asm` for its clobbers and bindings). An interval overlapping a blocked position cannot use that register. Move hints prefer the incoming register. On pressure, spill the interval with the furthest end.
- `chaos:<seed>`: random legal locations. The corpus must match across all three.

**Parallel moves** at calls and function entry use the standard cycle resolver with one scratch register.

### 7.5 Frames

Frame pointers are always on: they give stack traces and stable slot addressing.

```text
i386:    [ebp+8...] args   [ebp+4] return   [ebp] saved ebp   [ebp-4...] saved ebx/esi/edi (used only)
         slots below that; esp free, so argument pushes are natural
x86-64:  [rbp+16...] stack args  [rbp+8] return  [rbp] saved rbp  saved callee-saved  slots  outgoing area at [rsp]
```

- **i386 prologue:** `push ebp; mov ebp, esp; sub esp, N`. Epilogue: restore, `leave`, `ret args`.
- **Alignment:** i386 keeps 4 (no aligned-SSE assumption anywhere); x86-64 keeps 16 at every call. A local whose alignment exceeds that gets a hidden pointer slot, because `ebp` mod A is unknown and `esp` moves with argument pushes. The prologue does `mov eax, esp; sub eax, size; and eax, -A; mov esp, eax; mov [ebp-k], eax`, LIR reaches the local through `Mem::Indirect`, and `esp` is restored through `ebp`. Stack-limit and probe checks use `size + A - 1`.
- **Stack probes:** frames of 4 KB or more on hosted targets, not only Windows, because safe recursion must not jump a guard page. Probes run before the frame is used and addresses descend from the current stack pointer, at most one page per step: for `off = 0x1000, 0x2000, ...` while `off < N`: `test eax, [rsp-off]`; then `sub rsp, N`. An ascending probe's first touch is the deepest page, past the guard. On Windows 10.0.26300 with commit 0x1000 and reserve 8 MiB, a first touch at `rsp-0x8000` and an ascending probe over 64 KB both exit `0xC0000005`, and a descending probe over 256 KB succeeds [measured]. Linux grows the stack on any touch within the rlimit, so `lx64` cannot show this: the `win64` cell runs a corpus program with a 1 MiB local, and an LIR golden pins the probe order.
- **Freestanding limit check** on by default. Small frames: `cmp esp, [__stack_limit]; jb stub`. Frames of 256 bytes or more use a form that cannot wrap: `mov eax, esp; sub eax, N; jb stub; cmp eax, [__stack_limit]; jb stub; mov esp, eax`. So `let a: u8[131072]` on a 16 KB stack at base `0x10000` reports `stack overflow` instead of wrapping through address 0.
- **`__stack_limit`** is a writable `.data` word. Start sets it to stack bottom plus a fixed reserve: 2 KB under `soft`, 3 KB under `sse2` and `x87`. The reserve covers the hardware frame, stub pushes, the 512-byte `fxsave` with its alignment or the 108-byte `fnsave`, and panic printing. Island panic functions compile without the check, and the overflow stub writes 0 to `__stack_limit` before anything else, so the panic path cannot re-enter it. Float values in panic messages use a fixed work area in `.bss` under the panic guard flag, so printing fits the reserve. A bench test runs every panic message at the limit and fails if stack use passes the reserve. `unsafe cpu.setStackLimit(addr)` updates the word for a kernel that switches stacks, adding the reserve. Disabling the check (`--no-stack-limit`) forfeits soundness on overflow, and the option's help says so.
- **`DF` is clear** at every call boundary; the `asm` contract requires restoring it.
- **`MemCopy`:** small constant sizes as inline `mov`s through scratch; larger as `rep movsd` then `rep movsb`, with the allocator treating `rsi`, `rdi` as clobbered on x86-64 and the emitter wrapping `esi`, `edi` in push/pop on i386. Slots are `ebp`-relative, so the pushes are safe. `MemCopyDyn` and `MemSetDyn` take run-time sizes and emit the same string forms; with `overlap` they add a backward path that sets and then restores DF.
- **Padding:** `int3` between functions (never executed). No loop alignment at the floor, because multi-byte NOP is forbidden and `0x90` runs would slow loops for no benefit.

### 7.6 Calling conventions

Internal, unstable, one per architecture, independent of OS.

| | i386 | x86-64 |
|---|---|---|
| Int args | Stack, pushed right to left | `rdi rsi rdx rcx r8 r9`, then stack |
| Float args | Stack words (all strategies) | `xmm0`-`xmm7`, then stack |
| Returns | `eax`; `edx:eax` for `i64`, `f64`; `eax` for `f32` | `rax`; `xmm0` |
| Cleanup | Callee (`ret n`), saving bytes per call | Caller, fixed outgoing area |
| Callee-saved | `ebx esi edi ebp` | `rbx rbp r12-r15` |
| Closure value | Two words: code, env | Two registers or stack words: code, env |
| Closure call | Env pushed as hidden first argument | Env in `rdi`, shifting the rest; code through scratch |

The i386 float ABI is identical under all strategies, so a float strategy never changes the calling convention. **Aggregates** pass by pointer to caller-owned memory, and the callee owns the contents after a move, including the right to write them. So a Copy-typed source is copied to a temporary, and that temporary is elided only when all of these hold: the operand is rooted at an owned local with no `Deref` and no static; no live loan overlaps it (not merely no later direct use); the local is not address-taken; and this is its last use. Everything else copies. Otherwise `bump(r.big)` would write through a `&T`, `bump(K)` would write `.rodata`, and `bump(h.big)` would change what a live `q = &h` sees. Each case has a VM-against-native differential fixture. Returns go through a hidden first pointer.

**Return slot aliasing.** `x = f(x)` or `x = f(&x)` must not write directly into `x`. The destination is elided only when it is a fresh, unborrowed local that the arguments do not mention; otherwise a temporary is used.

**Foreign calls** take scalars, raw pointers and `fn` pointers only (EN0816). Struct-by-value is EN1030, so neither SysV classification nor Win64 by-reference rules exist.

- **Win64:** `rcx rdx r8 r9`, positional `xmm0-3`, 32 bytes of shadow space, 16-byte alignment, stack args from `[rsp+32]`. The internal callee-saved set is a subset of both platform ABIs, so `CallForeign` needs no extra saves.
- **`@abi("win64")` entry functions** (UEFI entry, callbacks) additionally save `rsi`, `rdi` and `xmm6-15`.
- **Windows imports** call `FF 15 rel32` through the IAT slot [measured with hand-written PE]. `cdecl` and `stdcall` are parsed and report EN1030, so 32-bit Windows is not precluded.

**Process entry.**

| Target | `_start` | Exit |
|---|---|---|
| `x86_64-linux` | `xor ebp, ebp; mov rdi, rsp; and rsp, -16; call __rt_start` (entry `rsp` is 0 mod 16 [measured]) | `exit_group` #231 |
| `i386-linux` | `xor ebp, ebp; mov eax, esp; push eax; call __rt_start` | `exit` #1 |
| `x86_64-windows` | `xor ebp, ebp; and rsp, -16; sub rsp, 32; call __rt_start` (entry `rsp` is 8 mod 16 [measured]) | `ExitProcess` |
| `i386-none` | Section 9.6 | `halt` |

`__rt_start` runs the level check, sets up the panic state, runs the entry (a program's top-level statements, or `main` in a file with none) and maps its result: falling off the end or `void` to 0, `return N;` among the statements or an `i32` from `main` to that code, and a failed `?` among the statements or `Err` from `main` to `error: <e>` on stderr, printed through `Display`, and 1.

### 7.7 Value representation

- **Layout** is exactly SPEC 11. A class keeps its fields in declaration order, with no reordering, after a hidden method-table pointer when it can be extended, and a class that extends one keeps it, `final` or not; a `final` class with no base has none. A subclass's own fields start after its base's whole size, padding included, so it never reuses its base's tail padding: with `public class A { public a: i64; public b: u8; }` and `public class D extends A { public c: u8; }`, `offsetOf<D>("c")` is 24 and `sizeOf<D>()` is 32 on `x86_64`. A field that a subclass overrides (SPEC 4.3) is its base's field in its base's slot, so it adds no slot and moves nothing; only the default that construction stores differs (section 5). A method table records its class's size and alignment beside the method slots, which freeing a `Box<A>` reads before the contents' cleanup runs (section 2.6), and its name, which the exact-class panic prints. Each slot belongs to the declaration that introduces it and has that declaration's `&string` forms, so an override whose forms differ gets a slot of its own (section 2.6); a `private` or `static` method has none, and neither has a method compiled once per type, which is never overridable (section 2.6). An abstract method's slot in an abstract class's own table, and in that class's tables for interfaces, names a stub that panics (section 2.6), so no slot is ever empty. For each interface `I` that mono sees one of its classes converted to, a class's table also holds a pointer to that class's table for `I`, which lists `I`'s members in `I`'s order with the class's size, alignment, name, drop glue and `fmt`, written, inherited or synthesised, and a second printer, the type's `fmtNested` (section 5), which writes a `string`, a `&string`, a string-literal-union value, a `Failure` or a text case of an enum with no written `fmt` in quotes and is the `fmt` for every other type, so a `Box<I>` or a two-word `&I` nested in another value prints as its object would there. The pointer sits at an index fixed in the table layout of the class `C` whose box or reference is converted, which every subclass's table repeats and fills with its own table for `I`. `BoxToInterface` from a `Box<C>` and `RefToInterface` from a `&C` of a `C` that can be extended, and an argument of such a class passed to a dynamic copy, read that table through the hidden pointer, so a `Box<Animal>` holding a `Dog` becomes a `Box<Speaker>` whose word names `Dog`'s table for `Speaker`, and `speak`, the size freed and the drop glue are the `Dog`'s (`TESTING.md` `S2-16-upcast`); for a `final` `C` the static table for `I` is exact. Anonymous object types and tuples place their fields by alignment, largest first, ties broken by name or position. No niche, with one exception, `&I | null` (below). `T | null` is tag + payload, so a one-word `&T | null` is `2P`. Enums have a `u32` tag at 0. A `Box<I>` for an interface `I` holds the object pointer and its class's table for `I`, `2P`, align `P`, and so does every `&I` outside a per-class copy's own non-null `&I` parameters: one taken from a box, and one held in a local, a result, a borrowing class's field, a tuple part or an `&I | null`. An `&I | null` is those same two words, `2P`, align `P`, a parameter included, with an object word of 0 meaning `null`, the one exception to SPEC 11's no niche, since a reference is never null (SPEC 5.9), not even one to an element of a zero-sized `T[]` (below): `Aggregate(present, [r])` of one stores `r`'s two words as they are, `null` stores 0 in both words, and `CheckTag`, `Discriminant`, `??` and `DisplayGlue` read whether one is present by comparing its object word with 0, where any other `T | null` reads its tag, and nothing reads its table word while its object word is 0. A per-class copy attaches its class's table where it returns, stores or wraps its `&I` parameter, read through the hidden pointer when the class can be extended (section 2.6). A dynamic copy takes every interface parameter in that form, and every call whose callee is chosen at run time, through a method table, an interface table, an `fn` value or a closure, passes its `&I` and `&I | null` arguments in it (section 2.6); a non-null `&I` parameter of any other copy is one word, because its copy knows the class.
- **Zero-sized types** occupy slots of size 0. Arrays of them never allocate and have `cap` equal to the maximum `usize`, and growing one past `len == isize.MAX` panics with `capacity overflow`, so `Len` always fits `isize` and `i386` stops at 2^31 elements. One that has elements has `ptr` equal to `alignOf<T>()`, never 0, which whatever gives it its first element sets, as its first `push` does, so a reference to one of its elements is never 0 and never reads as the `null` of an `&I | null`; an empty one may keep 0, since nothing references its elements (`TESTING.md` `VI-08-zst`). `Box` of one is a dangling aligned pointer with no allocation.
- **Sum types.** `Switch` lowers to a compare chain for up to 4 arms and a balanced binary search otherwise. No jump tables, so code needs no absolute data relocations and no indirect branches. Payload projections are plain offsets; `CheckTag` is `cmp` plus a cold branch to the `unexpected null` or internal-tag panic.
- **Strings.** `{ptr, len, cap}`. A literal's bytes are in `.rodata`, and in `string` position its three words are stored into the slot, with `cap == 0`. A `&string` that only reads is a pointer and a length, so it can name part of a string (`slice`), and a literal in that position is the address and length of its bytes, holding no loan. A `&string` that may grow the string points at the whole `{ptr, len, cap}`. The inferred permission decides which form a reference has (SPEC 11), so a function whose `&string` parameter only reads takes the two-word form. For the same reason a `&string` never becomes an `&I` (section 4): an `&I`'s two words are the object and its table, with no word for a part's length, while a `Box<string>` and a by-value `string` hold the whole `{ptr, len, cap}` and convert. Stored at an `fn` row or closure parameter that writes that parameter, or overriding a method or implementing a member that writes it, such a function is reached through a form conversion: the compiler emits a `StringForm` adapter, which loads the pointer and length from the whole string it is given and calls the function (section 2.6, `TESTING.md` `MR-1`), or, for an `fn` or closure value passed to a closure parameter, a `StringFormEnv` closure that calls through that value; any other `fn` value can't take such a conversion (EN0615). A returned `&string` has the whole form when the result is writable and the two-word form when it is read-only, so one that may be a part, from a `&string` or `&string | null` parameter or a `&string` part of a by-value tuple parameter that the body only reads, from `slice` or from a literal, is read-only and can't grow, and so is a returned tuple with such a part (section 6.7); a writable one comes from whole places, as `&this.name` and `&p.name` are, or from `&string` parameters the body may grow. A generic body is checked once, and its contract never depends on `T` (SPEC 4.10), so the part test above never sees a `&T`: in an instance at `T := string`, a Read `&T` or `&T | null` parameter, or a Read `&T` part of a by-value tuple parameter, that is among the sources of a writable result, and is not stored, takes the whole form, as `pick<T>(a: &T, b: &T, first: bool): &T` needs for `*pick(&s, &t, true) += "!"`, and so does such a parameter of an `fn` or closure type written with type parameters, by its row's declared sources, since that row is computed before substitution (section 6.7). An argument or tuple part that is two words at such a call, as `s.slice(0, 1)` is, through an `fn` or closure value included, is always passed through a statement-temporary descriptor `{ptr, len, cap: 0}`, a `null` staying `null`, and the call's result is converted back to two words at once. That is sound: a two-word argument is either marked, and then so is the result, or not write-live, since otherwise N3 (d) would have made it whole, so nothing writes through the temporary or keeps it. `+` calls `__str_concat(&a, &b)`; `+=` appends in place through the borrow of its place taken before the right-hand side (section 5), copying first if `cap == 0`.
- **Arrays.** `{ptr, len, cap}`. A `&T[]` is always one pointer to the whole `{ptr, len, cap}`, whatever the function does with it, since only a whole array can grow. A `&Slice<T>` is a pointer to the first element and a length, `2P`: `xs.slice(a, b)` makes one for a part (its indexes clamped, so it never panics and never points outside the array, section 10), `FixedToSlice` from a `&T[N]` with its static length and `ArrayToSlice` from a whole `&T[]` by reading its `ptr` and `len`, so a write through it changes the original and nothing can grow it. A constant literal view (section 5) is a read-only static in `.rodata`, laid out as its `T[N]`, and the view is the static's address and its static length, as `FixedToSlice` gives, holding no loan, as a string literal's descriptor holds none. Indexing is `cmp idx, len; jae stub` (the unsigned compare also catches negative `isize`), then a scaled address. An index of another integer type is first checked into `isize` range on the same out-of-bounds path, so a negative or too-large index panics as out of bounds (SPEC 4.6). Growth doubles with minimum capacity 4, and `cap * size <= isize.MAX` is checked on every target (`capacity overflow`, reachable on i386, where IA32 emulation gives a process nearly 4 GiB).
- **Drops.** `DropGlue(p)` calls `drop_glue<T>(p)`, by internal convention, or disappears when `needs_drop` is false.
- **`T[N]`.** Inline; `RepeatLit` is `MemSet` for byte-identical patterns, otherwise a loop.

### 7.8 Checks, panics, stack traces

A failed check branches to a per-site cold stub at the end of the function:

```text
stub:  push len ; push idx ; call __panic_bounds   // i386, 5 bytes plus operand moves
       (idx, len) -> (rdi, rsi) ; call __panic_bounds     // x86-64, through the parallel-move resolver
```

The x86-64 moves go through the resolver because `rsi` and `rdi` are allocatable: with `idx` in `rsi` and `len` in `rdi`, naive moves would report `len` for both. An LIR fixture pins that case. The return address is the location key, resolved through `.enr.trace`.

| Check | Sequence |
|---|---|
| Signed or unsigned overflow | `add`/`sub` then `jo` or `jc`; never `inc`/`dec` because they leave CF unchanged |
| Multiply | 32- and 64-bit: two-operand `imul` with `jo`; unsigned `mul` with `jo`. `i8`, `u8`, `i16`, `u16`: widen to 32 bits in scratch with `movsx` or `movzx`, `imul r32`, then range-compare against the narrow type. `imul al, bl` does not exist [measured] |
| Divide | Explicit zero and `MIN / -1` tests at every width, because hardware raises #DE. `i8`, `u8`, `i16`, `u16`: widen to 32 bits the same way and use `idiv r32` or `div r32`, so 8-bit `div` with its AH remainder is never generated |
| Shift | Range check first, because hardware masks the count |
| `as` int to int | Range compare against target bounds |
| Float to int | Range and NaN test first, because `cvttsd2si` returns indefinite on overflow; lowering per section 7.3 |
| Exact class (`CheckClass`) | `cmp` the object's hidden table pointer with the address of `A`'s table, then `jne` to a stub that calls `__panic_class` with the operation, the holder kind (`via`) and both tables, whose recorded names the message prints |

**Trace table (`.enr.trace`, read-only):**

```text
header  u32 magic "ENRT", u32 fn_count, u32 site_bytes, u32 file_count, u32 string_bytes
fns     fn_count x { u32 start_off_from_text, u32 name_off }        // sorted by start
sites   per function: ULEB128 site_count, then (ULEB pc_delta, SLEB line_delta, ULEB col, ULEB file_idx)
files   file_count x u32 name_off
strings NUL-terminated names
```

`--trace=full` keeps everything; `fn` keeps names and only the panicking site's line and column; `none` drops the section, so messages have no location or stack. The walker follows `ebp`/`rbp`, checks alignment, monotonic increase and stack bounds, and stops at 0 or after 64 frames. `_start` zeroes the frame pointer; interrupt stubs build a synthetic frame. A guard flag sends a second panic straight to exit or halt. Output format is SPEC 9.1 exactly.

### 7.9 `i64` on i386

- Two independent `G32` vregs; address-taken ones are 8-byte slots with alignment 4.
- Helpers are `@lang` functions in `core/int64.en`: `__mulo64`, `__umulo64`, `__sdiv64`, `__udiv64`, `__srem64`, `__urem64`, plus decimal formatting that splits by 10^9.
- **Division traps:** `div r32` raises #DE unless EDX is below the divisor. The helper fast-paths `hi == 0`, uses two `DivWideU32` steps for 32-bit divisors, and uses shift-subtract otherwise. `DivWideU32(hi, lo, d)` has the precondition `hi < d`, lowers to `div r32` (LIR `DivWide`), and is precondition-checked in the VM, so the helper runs in `vm32` and under `--float=soft` with no `asm`.
- **No self-recursion.** Helpers use only `u32` arithmetic plus the `MulWideU32` and `DivWideU32` intrinsics and inline `AluPair`. A meta-test walks the call graph: no compiler-inserted helper may reach itself, whether through soft-float, `i64` operations or panics.
- `Atomic64` needs `i586`; volatile `u64` is EN0805 because it would tear.

### 7.10 Floats

**Decision: three strategies, all from M8: `soft`, `x87` and `sse2`.** On `i386-*` the default follows the level: `soft` at `i386` and `i486`, because a 386 without a 387 and a 486SX have no FPU and a level is never a requirement; `x87` at `i586` and `i686`, where every listed chip has one; `sse2` at `sse2`. `--float=x87` opts in at `i386`, `i486` and `sse2`. All three pass the same bit vectors (SPEC 9.3). The target tables accept `--float=x87` from M0, and code generation for every `i386-*` strategy arrives in M8.

| Strategy | Where | Implementation |
|---|---|---|
| `soft` | Default on `i386-*` at `i386` and `i486` | `core/softfloat/`: IEEE 754 binary32 and binary64 in integer-only Enrang. Add, sub, mul, div, rem, sqrt, compare, conversions. Round to nearest-even only, full subnormals, no traps |
| `x87` | Default on `i386-*` at `i586` and `i686`; opt-in at `i386`, `i486` and `sse2` | SPEC 9.3's recipe: `fninit`, then `fldcw 0x027F` (53-bit precision, round to nearest-even, all exceptions masked); a store after every operation; the strictfp scale pair around f64 multiply and divide; integer moves for copies (section 7.3). The encoder gains the x87 arithmetic forms for the compiler's own use |
| `sse2` | Default and only on `x86_64-*`; `i386-*` at level `sse2` | Scalar forms only: `addss/sd`, `subss/sd`, `mulss/sd`, `divss/sd`, `sqrtss/sd`, `ucomiss/sd`, `cvt*`, `movss/sd`, `movd/q`. No m128 operand, so no alignment fault is possible on a 4-aligned i386 stack |
| Host | VM | Rust `f32`/`f64`, or `--float=soft` to interpret the library |

User `asm` keeps only the x87 probing forms (`fninit`, `fnstsw`, `fnstcw`, `fnsave`, `frstor`, `fwait`), tagged `fpu`, under every strategy.

**Bit-exact guarantee.** SPEC 9.3 states it; the mechanics are:
- `%` is always the `core` function (exact, like `fmod`), on every strategy. SSE2 has no such instruction.
- Negation and `abs` are bit operations through a general register: under `sse2`, `movq rax, xmm0; btc rax, 63; movq xmm0, rax`, and under `x87` the sign bit of the value in its slot. `0 - x` is wrong for `+0`, and `xorpd m128` needs an aligned constant.
- `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc`, `round` are `core` code because `roundsd` is SSE4.1.
- MXCSR stays at `0x1F80` (no FTZ, no DAZ, all masked). Freestanding startup loads it; hosted OSes provide it.
- The x87 control word stays at `0x027F`. Startup loads it, and an interrupt stub reloads it after `fnsave` (section 7.15).
- Startup detects the Pentium FDIV flaw (section 8.3). On a flawed chip `/` and `%` use the software routine, which an x87 build that divides links, counted in the soft-float size budget (section 10).
- `toBits` canonicalises NaN with one compare. No per-operation cost anywhere.
- Soft-float matches SSE2 NaN bits as an internal test, stronger than the language guarantee.
- Float printing (Dragon4, integer-only, stack bignum) and parsing (bignum) are the same code on every strategy.
- The x87 test cells run SPEC 9.3's vectors on the host's own x87 through IA32 emulation. That verifies the code, not old chips.

### 7.11 Atomics

Threads are deferred, so atomics serve interrupt-versus-mainline sharing (SPEC 10.5). All lower to intrinsics:

| Operation | Level | Lowering |
|---|---|---|
| `load`, `store` | all | `mov` of exact width |
| `exchange` | all | `xchg` (implicit lock) |
| `add sub and or xor` | all | `lock` ALU, no result |
| `incAndTestZero`, `decAndTestZero` | all | `lock inc/dec`, ZF |
| `testAndSet`, `testAndClear` | all, on `u16 u32 usize i32`; `bts` has no byte form [measured] | `lock bts/btr`, CF |
| `fence` | all | `lock or dword ptr [esp], 0` |
| `compareExchange`, `fetchAdd` | `i486`+, else EN0910 | `lock cmpxchg`, `lock xadd` |
| `Atomic64` | `i586`+, else EN0910 in phase 4 | `lock cmpxchg8b` (memory operand only) on i386, 64-bit forms on x86-64. `@align(8)` on every target, so it never splits a cache line |

`AtomicValue` and `AtomicBits` are sealed bounds in `core`, so `T` is never a surprise at mono. There is no silent emulation of CAS. In the VM all are plain operations, since it is single-threaded and has no interrupts. `cpu.withoutInterrupts` is `pushfd; cli; ...; popfd`, freestanding and uniprocessor only.

### 7.12 Encoder

One table and one pure function. Forms are declared per family:

```rust
form!(ADD,  [RM32, R32],  op(0x01),        modrm_r,      modes(M16|M32|M64), I386);
form!(ADD,  [RM32, IMM8S], op(0x83),       modrm_digit(0), modes(M16|M32|M64), I386);
form!(CMOV, [R32, RM32],  op0f(0x40+cc),   modrm_r,      modes(M16|M32|M64), CMOV);
form!(ADDSD,[XMM, XMM_M64], pf2_op0f(0x58), modrm_r,     modes(M32|M64),     SSE2);
```

Fields: mnemonic, operand kinds, opcode map and bytes, mandatory prefix, ModRM kind (`/r`, `/digit`, `+r`, none), immediate kind, operand-size attribute, REX.W, valid modes, required `FeatureSet`. About 800 forms in total.

**Byte order:** segment override, address-size `67`, operand-size `66`, `rep`/`lock`, then mandatory `66`/`F2`/`F3`, then REX, then opcode. A mandatory prefix placed after REX voids the REX. This matches GAS's order [measured in `.code16`: `addr32 rep movsd` is `67 66 F3 A5`; `mov byte ptr es:[ebx], 1` is `26 67 C6 03 01`; `lock add dword ptr fs:[eax], 1` is `64 67 66 F0 83 00 01`].

**Mode rules:**
- **16-bit** (boot subset only): the M16 form list is generated from what `lib/boot` assembles, and a meta-test requires the list to equal that set. It includes `mov`, `mov sreg`, ALU, `test`, `lea`, `xchg`, shifts, `mul`, `div`, `push`, `pop`, `call`, `ret`, `int`, `in`/`out`, branches, `cli`, `sti`, `cld`, `hlt`, `lgdt`, `lidt`, far `jmp`, `mov cr0`, string ops, `pushf`/`popf`, `pushfd`/`popfd` and `cpuid`. `66` selects 32-bit operands, `67` selects 32-bit addressing, with its own BX/SI/DI/BP ModRM table. `data32 ljmp 0x08, 0x1000` is `66 EA 00 10 00 00 08 00`; `data32 lgdt [m]` is `66 0F 01 16 ...` [measured].
- **32-bit:** an ESP base requires SIB; an EBP base with no displacement requires disp8.
- **64-bit:** R12 behaves like RSP and R13 like RBP. `mod=00 rm=101` is RIP-relative, and absolute addressing needs SIB. The RIP displacement is measured from the instruction end including trailing immediates [measured: `add dword ptr [rip+0x20], 5` is `83 05 20 00 00 00 05`], so `RipRel32` relocations carry that delta. SIL and DIL need an empty REX; AH to BH cannot coexist with any REX.

**Selection** picks the shortest legal form (`83 /0 ib`, accumulator short forms, `inc r32` outside 64-bit mode). On ties it uses the direction GAS chooses, so the oracle compares bytes directly. Branches start short and only grow, to a fixed point. The exception is an allowed set lacking `Ia32` (section 7.14), where a `Jcc` that cannot stay short is a build error. On an 8086 the long form's `0F` decodes as `pop cs`; GAS at `.arch i8086` emits `73 03 E9 ...` where `i386` emits `0F 82 ...` [measured].

### 7.13 Inline assembler

About 700 lines over the same table. Intel syntax, one instruction per line or `;`, local labels `name:` scoped to one `asm`, per SPEC 7.4.

- **Directives in `@naked` bodies**, user and library: `db`, `dw`, `dd`, `dq`, `align`, and `times <n> db <v>` with `$` and `$$`, so a raw boot sector is user code (SPEC 7.5).
- **Library-only addition** for boot and startup code: symbol operands (`call __rt_start`, `lea eax, [sym]`) resolving against same-module functions and lang items. In user code a symbol operand is EN0903.
- **Operand binding.** `in` and `out` bind places to named physical registers, so there is no allocator interplay: the LIR `Asm` instruction blocks those registers and the clobbers at its position, and saves any callee-saved ones through the prologue. Bind moves go through the parallel-move resolver. Out places count as initialised for init dataflow. Flags and memory are assumed clobbered.
- **Diagnostics:** EN0901 (level), EN0902 (unknown mnemonic with did-you-mean), EN0903 (operands), EN0904 (register), EN0905 (`esp`, `ebp`, `rsp`, `rbp`), EN0915 (interpolation), EN0916 (MMX, 3DNow!, packed SSE), EN0917 (register not valid in this mode), EN0914 (`popa` warning; a `nop` is inserted after it at `i386`).
- **`@naked`, `@bits`.** Body is one `asm` statement, no prologue. `@bits(16)` assembles in `Mode::M16`, and calling such a function is EN0913.
- **x87.** The table holds the x87 arithmetic forms for `--float=x87`, but user `asm` reaches only the probing forms (section 7.10).

### 7.14 Level enforcement

```rust
define_features! {
    Fpu "fpu", Cpuid "cpuid", Bswap "bswap", Cmpxchg "cmpxchg", Xadd "xadd", Cmpxchg8b "cmpxchg8b",
    Rdtsc "rdtsc", Msr "msr", Cr4 "cr4", Cmov "cmov", Fcomi "fcomi", Mmx "mmx", Amd3dnow "3dnow",
    Sse "sse", Sse2 "sse2", Sse3 "sse3", Ssse3 "ssse3", Sse41 "sse4.1", Sse42 "sse4.2", Popcnt "popcnt",
    Fxsave "fxsave", Nopl "nopl", Pae "pae", Nx "nx", Lm "lm", Invlpg "invlpg",
    Ud2 "ud2",      // internal tag, P6 and up; not queryable through cpu.has
    Ia32 "ia32",    // internal tag on every form outside the 8086 subset; not queryable through cpu.has
}
```

Levels are bitsets because the ISA is not a line: K6 has MMX and lacks CMOV, while Pentium Pro has CMOV and lacks MMX [measured with `as`]. Every level has `Ia32`. Stage 1 assembles with an allowed set lacking it, which is what keeps its pre-386 check honest; the `i8086` oracle checks the same thing independently (`TESTING.md` section 15.2). Scalar SSE1 forms and `ldmxcsr`/`stmxcsr` are tagged `sse`, which level `sse2` includes.

**Per-function allowed set.** `allowed = TargetSpec.allowed ∪ @requires(...)`, or `floor ∪ @requires(...)` inside the floor island (section 8.4). `Assembler::new(mode, allowed)` checks every emit with `form.features ⊆ allowed`:

- Generated code above the level is an internal compiler error, exit 3.
- Inline assembly above the level is EN0901, naming the instruction, its feature, the lowest level providing it, and the current level. Every `asm` in a user module is checked in phase 4, reachable or not, by every command that reaches phase 4, whether the module's MIR was built from source or read from its interface file, whose key leaves out the level, so the check is never cached (section 6.8); library `asm` is checked when reached, and all of it by a per-target meta-test.

Under `--float=x87` the allowed set of generated code also holds `fpu`, because startup has already stopped on a machine with no FPU (section 8.3). That covers the x87 arithmetic forms the compiler emits, which user `asm` never reaches (section 7.13). No level's `FeatureSet` gains `fpu` (section 8.2).

Enforcement is verified independently of the tags: assembler oracle per form, disassembly audit of whole output, single-step classification at run time (`TESTING.md` section 15).

### 7.15 Interrupt stubs

The compiler emits for `@interrupt` (SPEC 7.7): `cld`, individual `push`es and `pop`s (never `pusha`/`popa`), a synthetic frame for stack traces, then the body, then `iretd` or `iretq`. Under `sse2`, the state around the body is saved by aligning the stack (`mov ebp, esp; and esp, -16; sub esp, 512; fxsave [esp]`). Under `x87`, the body is wrapped in `fnsave` and `frstor` over a 108-byte area; `fnsave` reinitialises the FPU, so the stub then reloads the control word `0x027F` (section 7.10). Under `soft` there is nothing to save.

`idt.set` checks that vectors 8, 10-14 and 17 pair with `errorCode` handlers. The PIC is remapped to `0x20`/`0x28` with I/O delays before `sti`. `idt.enable()` also clears the NMI mask with `out 0x70, 0x0D` then `in 0x71`, because stage 2 masked it. x86-64 uses the 16-byte IDT and always `fxsave`s. No target uses a red zone, so interrupts on the current stack are always safe, and the `__stack_limit` reserve covers the hardware frame, stub pushes and the `fxsave` or `fnsave` area.

### 7.16 Traps, collected

- `setcc` needs a byte register, then `movzx`.
- 64-bit ALU immediates are sign-extended imm32; anything else goes through a scratch register.
- SSE enablement on freestanding: after detection, clear CR0.EM, set CR0.MP, set CR4.OSFXSR and OSXMMEXCPT, `fninit`, `ldmxcsr` default, all before any float use. CR4 is touched only when CPUID reports a CR4 feature, because the 386 has no CR4.
- x87 enablement on freestanding: after the FPU check, clear CR0.EM, set CR0.MP, `fninit`, `fldcw 0x027F`, all before any float use (section 8.3).
- Flat output has no loader, so startup zeroes BSS using `__bss_start` and `__bss_end`.
- PE `.reloc` is required for absolute data pointers (string descriptors, static `fn` tables, method tables) because of `DYNAMIC_BASE`.
- Size arithmetic (`cap * elemSize`) must be checked: it is reachable on i386.
- 386 POPA erratum: never emitted by the compiler; the inline assembler warns and inserts a `nop`.

## 8. CPU support

### 8.1 Feature matrix

Evidence: **[P]** primary document read during design, **[O]** `as` oracle on this host, **[S]** secondary source, **[M]** memory, not verified. `Y` yes, `.` no, `o` external option, `~` varies by stepping or model, `?` unverified.

Columns: 1 FPU, 2 CPUID, 3 BSWAP, 4 CMPXCHG, 5 XADD, 6 CMPXCHG8B, 7 RDTSC, 8 CMOV, 9 FCOMI, 10 MMX, 11 3DNow!, 12 SSE, 13 SSE2, 14 SSE3, 15 SSSE3, 16 SSE4.1, 17 FXSAVE, 18 NOPL, 19 CR4, 20 INVLPG, 21 PAE, 22 NX, 23 x86-64.

```text
                           1-5   6-10  11-15 16-20 21-23
i386 DX,SX; Am386 DX,SX    o.... ..... ..... ..... ...
i486 SX; Am486 SX          .~YYY ..... ..... ...~Y ...
i486 DX,DX2; Am486 DX,DX2  Y~YYY ..... ..... ...~Y ...
i486 DX4; Am486DX4; Am5x86 YYYYY ..... ..... ...~Y ...
Pentium P5,P54C; K5        YYYYY YY... ..... ...YY ...
Pentium MMX; K6            YYYYY YY..Y ..... ...YY ...
K6-2, K6-III               YYYYY YY..Y Y.... ...YY ...
Pentium Pro                YYYYY YYYY. ..... ..YYY Y..
Pentium II                 YYYYY YYYYY ..... .~YYY Y..
Pentium III                YYYYY YYYYY .Y... .YYYY Y..
K7 model 1                 YYYYY YYYYY Y.... ..?YY Y..
K7 model 2-4, Duron        YYYYY YYYYY Y.... .Y?YY Y..
K7 XP, MP, Morgan          YYYYY YYYYY YY... .Y?YY Y..
Willamette, Northwood      YYYYY YYYYY .YY.. .YYYY Y..
Prescott                   YYYYY YYYYY .YYY. .YYYY Y~~
Smithfield-Presler         YYYYY YYYYY .YYY. .YYYY Y~Y
Pentium M                  YYYYY YYYYY .YY.. .YYYY ~~.
Core Solo/Duo              YYYYY YYYYY .YYY. .YYYY YY.
Bonnell Z5xx, N2xx         YYYYY YYYYY .YYYY .YYYY Y~.
Bonnell 64-bit             YYYYY YYYYY .YYYY .YYYY YYY
Silvermont+ Atom           YYYYY YYYYY .YYYY YYYYY YYY
K8 (not listed)            YYYYY YYYYY YYY~. .YYYY YYY
Core 2 65 nm               YYYYY YYYYY .YYYY .YYYY YYY
Core 2 45 nm incl. Q8400   YYYYY YYYYY .YYYY ~YYYY YYY
Newer                      YYYYY YYYYY ~YYY~ ~YYYY YYY
```

Notes:
- **486 `~`:** CPUID exists only on later Intel steppings [S]; AMD states CPUID starts at Am486DX4 [P]. A 486 has CR4 only with a CPUID-reported CR4 feature such as VME [M]. Rule: touch CR4 only when CPUID.1:EDX reports one.
- **AMD:** K6-2/III have no CMOV, FXSR or PAE. K7 model 1 has no FXSR; models 2, 4 and Duron do. All K7 have CMOV and PAE [P]. K5 model 0 reports PGE at bit 9 [P]. K7 XP SSE and the remaining K5 and K6 cells are [M].
- **Pentium II:** Klamath lacks FXSAVE, Deschutes has it [S]. Test feature bits, never model numbers.
- **NOPL:** Intel P6 and later; Linux states all x86-64 CPUs have it [S]. No K7 source found.
- **Pentium M:** PAE implemented but unreported on Banias and early Dothan; later Dothan reports PAE and NX [S].
- **Prescott:** NX and x86-64 on some steppings only. Long-mode LAHF arrived with stepping G1, and Prescott 630 lacks CMPXCHG16B, both from weak sources [S]. The baseline uses neither.
- **Atom:** Z5xx and N2xx cannot run x86-64 [S]. Some Bay Trail systems pair a 64-bit CPU with 32-bit UEFI [M]; `x86_64-none` will not boot those.
- **45 nm `~`:** Core 2 branded parts, including the owner's Q8400, have SSE4.1; Pentium E5xxx-class derivatives do not [M]. No Core 2 has POPCNT, SSE4.2 or AVX. VT-x sources for the Q8400 conflict, so nothing relies on it.
- **Newer:** K10 lacks SSSE3 and SSE4.1 and keeps 3DNow! [M]. "Better" guarantees only x86-64, SSE2, CMOV, CMPXCHG8B, FXSAVE and NOPL. All else goes through CPUID.

### 8.2 The `--cpu` ladder

SPEC 10.1 fixes the rungs. Implementation: each rung is one table entry plus a small number of codegen predicates.

| Level | `FeatureSet` | Codegen uses | Startup check (floor code) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `i386` | `ia32` | Base forms | None hosted; stage 1 rejects 8086/286 |
| `i486` | + `bswap cmpxchg xadd invlpg` | `bswap` in `swapBytes`; `lock cmpxchg`, `lock xadd` | AC toggle |
| `i586` | + `cpuid cmpxchg8b rdtsc msr cr4` | `Atomic64`; float strategy `x87` by default | ID toggle, TSC and CX8 bits |
| `i686` | + `cmov fcomi ud2` | `cmov` in integer `min`, `max`; float strategy `x87` by default | CMOV bit |
| `sse2` | + `sse sse2 fxsave` | Float strategy `sse2`; `fxsave` in interrupt stubs | SSE2 and FXSR bits; freestanding sets CR4.OSFXSR |
| `x86-64` | `ia32 cpuid cmpxchg8b cmpxchg xadd bswap rdtsc msr cmov fcomi fpu ud2 sse sse2 fxsave nopl lm invlpg cr4 pae` | Baseline; **not** CMPXCHG16B, long-mode LAHF/SAHF, POPCNT, SSE3+. `generic64` accepts `ud2`, `fninit` and `fcomi` [measured] | None hosted; LM bit freestanding |

Aliases per SPEC 10.1 are resolved in `enrang-target` and print a note. `native` is rejected (EN0909): the host is never the target. `core2` is deliberately not a rung, because a non-vectorising compiler gets nothing from SSSE3. MMX, 3DNow! and SSE1 are never rungs; K7 stays at `i686` because SSE without SSE2 cannot do `f64`.

### 8.3 Startup detection

Runs in floor code, before any higher instruction:

```text
detect486:  pushfd; pop eax; mov ecx, eax; xor eax, 0x40000; push eax; popfd     // AC, bit 18
            pushfd; pop eax; push ecx; popfd; xor eax, ecx; test eax, 0x40000    // nonzero: 486+
detectCpuid: same with 0x200000                                                  // ID, bit 21; nonzero: CPUID exists
cpuid(0): max leaf, vendor
cpuid(1): EDX FPU 0, TSC 4, MSR 5, PAE 6, CX8 8, CMOV 15, MMX 23, FXSR 24, SSE 25, SSE2 26
          ECX SSE3 0, SSSE3 9, SSE4.1 19, SSE4.2 20, POPCNT 23
cpuid(0x80000000): EAX is the maximum extended leaf; every extended bit reads 0 unless EAX >= 0x80000001
cpuid(0x80000001): EDX NX 20, LM 29, 3DNow 31
```

The extended-leaf guard is required: the SDM says an out-of-range leaf returns the highest basic leaf's data, so without it a P5 or P6 can show a false LM, NX or 3DNow bit.

CPUID has no bit for some `cpu.has` names. The `checkLevel` table defines them:

| Feature | Defined as |
|---|---|
| `bswap`, `cmpxchg`, `xadd`, `invlpg` | AC toggle |
| `fcomi` | FPU and CMOV bits |
| `cr4` | Any of VME, DE, PSE, TSC, PAE, MCE, PGE in CPUID.1:EDX |
| `nopl` | LM bit only, matching our level table |

`checkLevel(flags)` is one table-driven function, also tested with fake flags. A failure prints `needs i686 (CMOV); this CPU lacks it`. Linux exits 126, Windows calls `ExitProcess(126)`, and freestanding writes VGA and COM1, then halts.

**FPU probe, freestanding only** (SPEC 10.3): clear CR0.EM and TS, seed a word with `0xA5A5`, `fninit`, `fnstsw` into it, expect 0; `fnstcw`, expect `0x037F`. On CPUs without CPUID, also require CR0.ET = 1, because a 287 beside a 386 passes the probe but has ET clear; it counts as no FPU.

**FPU and SSE state.** With `--float=soft` at `i386` and `i486`, startup sets CR0.EM=1, so any stray x87 opcode traps to `#NM` and panics: a run-time proof that no x87 code executes. At `i586` and above, whatever the float strategy, startup leaves CR0.EM clear, so `cpu.has("fpu")` can fold to `true` honestly (SPEC 10.3); there the disassembly audit alone shows that a `soft` build has no x87 code. With `--float=x87`, startup first checks that an FPU is present and, if not, stops with a clear message as the level check does (SPEC 9.3); freestanding startup then clears CR0.EM at every level (section 7.16). It also detects the Pentium FDIV flaw by computing `4195835 / 3145727`: the correct result is `1.333820449136241`, and a flawed Pentium gives `1.333739068902037`. On a flawed chip `/` and `%` use the software routine (section 7.10). At level `sse2` and above, whatever the float strategy, startup leaves CR0.EM=0, CR0.MP=1, CR4.OSFXSR=1 and CR4.OSXMMEXCPT=1, so `cpu.has("sse2")` can fold to `true` honestly. Below that level `unsafe cpu.enableSse()` does the same and refreshes the cache.

`cpu.has` caches a detected bitset on first use; a feature within the static level folds to `true` at mono, and `fpu` also folds to `true` at `i586` and up, where every listed chip has one. On freestanding targets the cached `fpu`, `sse*` and `fxsave` bits mean usable now: the CPUID bit, CR0.EM clear, and for SSE CR4.OSFXSR set. CR4 is read only when `cr4` is true.

### 8.4 The floor island

Startup, the level check and panic printing are compiled at `floor` whatever `--cpu` says (SPEC 10.4). No marks are needed: the island is computed as the call-graph closure from `_start` and from the `__panic_*` entry points, cut at two boundaries: the call to the entry (a program's top-level statements, `main` or `@entry`), and the call to `@panicHandler`, which sit at target level because the level check has passed by then. Shared callees (`core/fmt`, Dragon4) compile once at the floor. On `x86_64-*` the floor is `x86-64` itself.

**Floats in the island.** Inside the island, floats legalise as in the `i386` soft column of section 7.3 whatever the strategy: G32 words, and `toBits` as integer moves. The i386 float ABI is already stack words, so callers interoperate. Without this, `--cpu=sse2 --float=sse2` would put SSE2 in code that runs before the level check. An `Fp` LIR instruction in an island function is an internal error.

A meta-test verifies the island from assembler records and asserts it contains no `Fp` instruction, and the audit disassembles island symbols at `-march=i386`. The only exceptions are the FPU probe forms and, under `x87`, the FDIV check's library `asm`, which audit at `i386+387`.

### 8.5 Errata

| Erratum | Chips | Action |
|---|---|---|
| 32-bit multiply bug, "16 BIT S/W ONLY" [S] | Earliest 386s; sources disagree on steppings | Unsupported; document the markings |
| POPA/POPAD corrupts EAX, or hangs after POPA plus EAX-based address; NoFix [P] | 386 DX; SX [S]; Am386 likely [S, weak] | Never emitted; ISR stubs use individual POPs; inline gets EN0914 plus inserted NOP |
| TSS limit off by two; TF task-switch fault [P] | 386 | OS rule: limit at least 103; no hardware task switching |
| LOCKed instruction into a not-present page gives #UD, not #PF [P] | 386 | Moot until demand paging; then the #UD handler must check |
| XBTS/IBTS at `0F A6/A7` [S] | 386 A0-B0 | Never emitted |
| F00F: `LOCK CMPXCHG8B reg` hangs [S] | P5, MMX before B2 | No register form in the encoder; inline gets EN0903 |
| FDIV [M] | Early P5 | Soft-float and SSE2 immune by construction; under `x87` startup detects the flaw and `/` and `%` use the software routine (section 8.3) |
| K6 model 6 stepping 1 before B9730xxxx [S] | K6 | Hardware defect: unsupported |
| A-step 486 CMPXCHG opcode [M] | Early 486 | `--cpu=i486` unsupported on those; floor output unaffected |

Compiled floor code never contains `popa`, `popad`, `enter`, `ud2` or `loop`; panic stops with `int3` then a halt loop.

### 8.6 What "supported" means, per family

Four classes (SPEC 10.6), generated from test results by `enrang targets --evidence`:

- **C1 level-proven.** Encoder tags, assembler oracle, disassembly audit, single-step classification.
- **C2 run at that level on this host.** Proves the code, not the chip.
- **C3 emulated,** naming the model.
- **C4 real hardware,** only from the owner's report.

Our own `x86sim` bench (`TESTING.md` section 19) proves logic and is never counted as any class. **Nothing is achieved yet: C1, C2 and C3 read "planned", and C4 reads "no hardware" except for the Q8400 and `Newer` rows.** No class is claimed before it is reached.

| Family | Rung | C1, C2 | C3 route | C4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| i386, Am386 | `i386` | Reachable | Bochs custom build with `--enable-cpu-level=3`, unverified for 2.7; no SX or AMD model | None: no hardware |
| i486, Am486, Am5x86 | `i486` | Reachable | QEMU `486`, Bochs `i486dx4`; no SX or AMD model | None |
| Pentium, Pentium MMX | `i586` | Reachable | QEMU `pentium`, Bochs both | None |
| K5 | `i586` | Reachable | None found | None |
| K6, K6-2, K6-III | `i586` | Reachable (3DNow! is not used) | Bochs `amd_k6_2_chomper` | None |
| Pentium Pro, II, III | `i686` | Reachable | QEMU `pentium2`, `pentium3`, Bochs; no Pentium Pro model listed | None |
| K7 | `i686` | Reachable | QEMU `athlon`, a feature approximation | None |
| NetBurst | `sse2` | Reachable | Bochs `p4_willamette`, `p4_prescott_celeron_336` | None |
| Intel mobile: Pentium M, Core Solo/Duo | `sse2` | Reachable | QEMU `coreduo`, Bochs `core_duo_t2400_yonah` | None |
| Atom | `sse2`; `x86-64` on 64-bit parts | Reachable | QEMU `n270`, Bochs `atom_n270` | None |
| Core 2 Duo, Core 2 Quad, Q8400 | `sse2`, `x86-64` | Reachable | QEMU `core2duo`, `Conroe`, `Penryn`; Bochs `core2_penryn_t9600` | Q8400 only, owner's run |
| Newer | `sse2`, `x86-64` | Reachable | QEMU modern models | This i5-12400F, user mode only |

Model names come from published sources; confirm with `qemu-system-i386 -cpu help` and the Bochs model table once installed. Whether TCG rejects instructions above a model is unverified, so QEMU is boot evidence and never level proof.

**A floor binary hits no ISA obstacle on any listed chip.** The limits are environmental:

- **`i386-linux`** needs a kernel with `int 0x80`. Linux 7.1 removes the 486 build options per April 2026 reports, and 386 support ended in 2012. So on real 386 and 486 machines it needs an old kernel, and it uses only old syscalls. Here it runs through IA32 emulation [measured].
- **`i386-none`** needs legacy BIOS or CSM. It is the real route to the oldest chips.
- **UEFI-only machines** need `x86_64-none --format=efi`.
- **Windows:** only `x86_64-windows`. 32-bit Windows is EN1031: not requested, not built, not precluded.

## 9. Output writers

### 9.1 Link model

```rust
pub struct Object { pub sections: Vec<Section>, pub symbols: IdVec<SymId, SymbolDef>, pub entry: SymId, pub imports: Vec<Import> }
pub enum SectionKind { Boot, Text, Rodata, Trace, Data, Bss, Custom { flags: SectionFlags } }
pub struct Section { pub name: Symbol, pub kind: SectionKind, pub align: u32, pub bytes: Vec<u8>, pub bss_size: u64,
                     pub relocs: Vec<Reloc>, pub fixed_addr: Option<u64> }
pub enum RelocKind { Abs32, Abs64, Rel32, RipRel32 { trailing: u8 }, ImportRip32 }
pub struct Reloc { pub offset: u32, pub kind: RelocKind, pub sym: SymId, pub addend: i64 }
pub struct LinkedImage {
    pub segments: Vec<Segment>, pub entry: u64, pub base: u64, pub symbols: Vec<(String, u64)>,
    pub absolute_fixups: Vec<u64>,                 // addresses of pointer-sized absolute data, for PE .reloc
    pub imports: ImportTable, pub bss: (u64, u64),
}
```

**Order (SPEC 7.8):** `.boot*`, `.text*`, `.rodata*`, `.enr.trace`, `.data*`, `.bss*`, then custom sections in first-use order. Text is one blob per function in first-reach order, starting with `_start`. Linker GC drops unreferenced statics and strings; functions are already tree-shaken. `@at` storage takes an address and no bytes; overlap is EN0813.

**Linker symbols:** `__text_start`, `__rodata_start`, `__trace_start`, `__trace_end`, `__data_start`, `__bss_start`, `__bss_end`, `__image_end`, `__stack_bottom`, `__stack_top`. `.bss.stack` holds the stack and is reserved for the compiler. `__stack_limit` is not a linker symbol: it is a `.data` word that start computes from `__stack_bottom` (section 7.5).

A relocation that does not fit its field, and an image span above `2^31 - 1` on a RIP-relative target, are EN0435 and never truncate.

### 9.2 ELF64 (`x86_64-linux`, ET_EXEC)

Ehdr, 64 bytes:

| Off | Size | Field | Value |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| 0 | 16 | `e_ident` | `7F 45 4C 46 02 01 01 00`, 8 zero bytes |
| 16 | 2 | `e_type` | 2 |
| 18 | 2 | `e_machine` | `0x3E` |
| 20 | 4 | `e_version` | 1 |
| 24 | 8 | `e_entry` | `_start` |
| 32 | 8 | `e_phoff` | 64 |
| 40 | 8 | `e_shoff` | 0, or section table with `--symbols` |
| 48 | 4 | `e_flags` | 0 |
| 52 | 2 | `e_ehsize` | 64 |
| 54 | 2 | `e_phentsize` | 56 |
| 56 | 2 | `e_phnum` | 4, or 3 with no RW segment |
| 58 | 2 | `e_shentsize` | 64 |
| 60 | 2 | `e_shnum` | 0 unless `--symbols` |
| 62 | 2 | `e_shstrndx` | 0 unless `--symbols` |

Phdr, 56 bytes, field order: `p_type u32, p_flags u32, p_offset u64, p_vaddr u64, p_paddr u64, p_filesz u64, p_memsz u64, p_align u64`.

| # | Type | Flags | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `PT_LOAD` (1) | R X (5) | Headers, `.boot*`, `.text*` |
| 1 | `PT_LOAD` | R (4) | `.rodata*`, `.enr.trace` |
| 2 | `PT_LOAD` | R W (6) | `.data*`, `.bss*` via `p_memsz > p_filesz`; omitted if empty |
| 3 | `PT_GNU_STACK` (`0x6474E551`) | R W | Omitting it can give an executable stack |

**Size discipline.** No file padding between segments: `p_offset ≡ p_vaddr (mod 0x1000)` holds by bumping the vaddr of the next segment by one page, as `ld -z noseparate-code` does. The boundary file page is mapped twice with different permissions. This is what makes a 12 KB hello reachable, and a hand-built ELF64 of this shape ran here at 4,136 bytes [measured]. `--symbols` appends `.symtab`, `.strtab`, `.shstrtab` and section headers, so `objdump` and `readelf -s` work.

### 9.3 ELF32 (`i386-linux`, ET_EXEC)

Ehdr, 52 bytes: `e_ident` as above with class 1 at byte 4; `e_type u16` 2; `e_machine u16` 3; `e_version u32` 1; `e_entry u32`; `e_phoff u32` 52; `e_shoff u32`; `e_flags u32` 0; `e_ehsize u16` 52; `e_phentsize u16` 32; `e_phnum u16`; `e_shentsize u16` 40; `e_shnum u16`; `e_shstrndx u16`.

Phdr, 32 bytes, **different order**: `p_type, p_offset, p_vaddr, p_paddr, p_filesz, p_memsz, p_flags, p_align`, all `u32`.

Same segment plan and size discipline, base `0x08048000`. Old kernels ignore `PT_GNU_STACK`. A hand-built ELF32 using old `mmap` #90, `write` and `exit` through `int 0x80` ran here at 4,214 bytes [measured].

### 9.4 PE32+ (`x86_64-windows`, EFI)

```text
0x000  DOS header: "MZ", zeros, e_lfanew at 0x3C = 0x40
0x040  "PE\0\0"
0x044  COFF header, 20 bytes
0x058  Optional header PE32+, 240 bytes
0x148  Section table, 40 bytes each
       Headers padded to FileAlignment
```

COFF: `Machine u16 0x8664; NumberOfSections u16; TimeDateStamp u32 0; PointerToSymbolTable u32 0; NumberOfSymbols u32 0; SizeOfOptionalHeader u16 240; Characteristics u16 0x22`.

| Opt off | Field | Value |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | `Magic` | `0x20B` |
| 2 | Linker version | 0, 0 |
| 4, 8, 12 | `SizeOfCode`, `SizeOfInitializedData`, `SizeOfUninitializedData` | From sections |
| 16 | `AddressOfEntryPoint` | `_start` RVA |
| 20 | `BaseOfCode` | `.text` RVA |
| 24 | `ImageBase` u64 | `0x140000000`; `0x100000` for EFI, which firmware relocates through the mandatory `.reloc` |
| 32, 36 | `SectionAlignment`, `FileAlignment` | `0x1000`, `0x200` |
| 40 | OS, image, subsystem versions | 6.0, 0.0, 6.0 |
| 52 | `Win32VersionValue` | 0 |
| 56, 60 | `SizeOfImage`, `SizeOfHeaders` | Computed |
| 64 | `CheckSum` | 0 |
| 68 | `Subsystem` | 3 CUI; 10 EFI application |
